📖 UNDERSTAND
How the 3 Tools Work Together
Before anything else, understand what each tool does and why you need all three. They don't overlap — they stack. Each sees something the others cannot.

🎨 Impeccable

The designer and builder. Reads your source code files and rewrites them. Makes things look professional. Knows what good design looks like. Runs entirely in your codebase.

Works on: .html .css .tsx .jsx .vue .svelte template files

🔍 Chrome DevTools MCP

The inspector. Connects to the running browser and reads what's actually happening at runtime — console errors, network failures, computed CSS, performance data, the accessibility tree.

Sees: console, network, DOM, performance, a11y tree

🤖 Playwright MCP

The robot user. Controls a browser like a human would — navigates to URLs, clicks buttons, fills forms, takes screenshots. Tests whether the page actually works for a real user.

Does: navigate, click, fill, screenshot, keyboard, scroll

The core insight: Impeccable fixes what you wrote. Chrome DevTools shows what the browser computed at runtime. Playwright tests what a real user experiences. You need all three to know a page is actually ready to ship.

When to use each tool

SituationTool to useWhy
UI looks bad, needs redesigningImpeccableIt rewrites the code. The others can't.
Page has errors after I changed somethingChrome DevToolsConsole shows JS errors. Network shows failed API calls.
I want to see if signup flow actually worksPlaywright MCPIt navigates and clicks like a real user would.
I want to check accessibilityChrome DevToolsImpeccableDevTools reads the a11y tree. Impeccable fixes the code.
I want to see how it looks on mobilePlaywright MCPSet viewport size, screenshot each breakpoint.
Pre-launch full QAAll 3 ToolsImpeccable audits code, DevTools checks runtime, Playwright tests journeys.
Something looks wrong but I don't know whyChrome DevToolsPlaywright MCPDevTools inspects computed state. Playwright shows it visually.
Client says "the form is broken"Playwright MCPChrome DevToolsPlaywright reproduces it. DevTools shows what network request failed.

The 3 layers every page has

Layer 1: Source code

The HTML, CSS, JavaScript files you write. This is what Impeccable reads and edits. Problems here: bad design choices, missing states, wrong structure.

Layer 2: Runtime (browser)

What the browser computes when it loads your code. This is what Chrome DevTools sees. Problems here: JS errors, failed requests, layout bugs, accessibility failures.

Layer 3: User experience

What an actual user sees and can do. This is what Playwright tests. Problems here: broken flows, forms that don't submit, buttons that don't respond.

A page can have clean source code, zero runtime errors, and still fail for real users. That's why you need all three checks — they're not redundant, they catch completely different things.

Quick reference — what each tool can and can't do

CapabilityImpeccableChrome DevToolsPlaywright
Edit source files✅ Yes❌ No❌ No
Read console errors❌ No✅ YesLimited
Check network requests❌ No✅ YesLimited
Take screenshots❌ NoPartial✅ Yes
Click buttons / fill forms❌ No❌ No✅ Yes
Read CSS computed values❌ No✅ Yes❌ No
Test at different viewport sizes❌ NoPartial✅ Yes
Check accessibility tree❌ No✅ Yes❌ No
Measure performance❌ No✅ YesLimited
Fix design problems✅ Yes❌ No❌ No
📖 UNDERSTAND
Setup & Install
Get Impeccable running in Claude Code. Do this once. Both MCP tools (Playwright and Chrome DevTools) are assumed to be connected and ready.

Install Impeccable

Option A — Recommended
npx skills add pbakaus/impeccable
Option B — Claude Code Plugin Marketplace
# Type this inside Claude Code /plugin marketplace add pbakaus/impeccable
Option C — Manual
# Download ZIP from impeccable.style → extract → copy: cp -r dist/claude-code/.claude/* ~/.claude/ # global (all projects) cp -r dist/claude-code/.claude your-project/ # this project only
Global vs project install: Global (~/.claude/) gives Impeccable to every project on your machine. Project-specific keeps it isolated. For agency work, global is almost always better.

One-time project setup — run this first

Open Claude Code in your project folder and type:

/impeccable teach

This creates two files that every command reads before acting:

PRODUCT.md

Who the product is for. What problem it solves. The audience, their needs, the business context. Commands read this to stay relevant to your actual users.

DESIGN.md

The visual system. Brand colours, typography approach, spacing, existing component patterns. Commands read this to stay on-brand automatically.

Never skip this. Without PRODUCT.md and DESIGN.md, every command guesses about your brand, audience, and intent. The first 5 minutes here saves you hours of bad output.
Questions /impeccable teach will ask you — be ready

Have these answers ready

  • Who is the target audience? (role, technical level, context — are they staff using this 8 hours/day?)
  • What is the brand personality / tone? (minimal, bold, playful, professional, editorial...)
  • What does "done well" feel like for this product?
  • Any existing brand colours, fonts, or style guide?
  • Primary tasks users need to accomplish
  • Any competitors or references you admire or want to avoid?

Pin your most-used commands

/impeccable pin teach /impeccable pin document /impeccable pin shape /impeccable pin craft /impeccable pin audit /impeccable pin critique /impeccable pin clarify /impeccable pin harden /impeccable pin polish # Now /teach, /document, /shape, /craft, /audit, /critique, /clarify, /harden, /polish all work without typing /impeccable each time. # These nine commands cover ~95% of what the recipes call.

Stay updated

npx impeccable skills update # run periodically

Optional: Chrome Extension

The Impeccable Chrome extension highlights anti-patterns on any live page in real-time — useful for auditing shipped work or checking competitor sites. Install from the Chrome Web Store by searching "Impeccable" or visiting impeccable.style.

🎨 DESIGN — Impeccable
Plan First — /impeccable shape
The most skipped step and the most valuable one. /shape runs a discovery interview and produces a design brief before any code is written. Most AI UI failures happen because someone skipped this step.
Why this matters: Every AI model defaults to "here's a card grid" without asking what the user is trying to accomplish. /shape inverts that. You answer questions, a brief is produced, then building starts from the right place.

What /shape does

/impeccable shape [feature description]

Runs a 5-10 question discovery interview. Does NOT write any code during this phase. Produces a design brief (brief.md) you can review, push back on, and then hand to any build command.

The questions it asks you

Purpose & context

What is this feature for? Who uses it? What state of mind are they in when they use it — rushed, focused, between meetings?

Content & data

What is actually displayed? What are realistic content ranges? What are edge cases — empty, one item, hundreds of items?

Design goals

What is the single most important thing this page must do? What should it feel like? Any references or anti-references?

Constraints

Technical limits, accessibility requirements, content limits, localisation needs, must-keep features from the existing version.

Answer specifically, not generically. "Standard" and "normal" give the AI nothing to work with. If a user is "rushed, on mobile, between meetings" — say exactly that. The brief quality directly determines build quality.

/shape vs /craft — which to use

Use /shape whenUse /craft when
You want just the brief, not the build (yet)You want the full flow — brief + build + visual iteration
You'll build it yourself after the briefYou want the AI to do it all in one go
Multiple stakeholders need to approve the directionYou're the only decision-maker and want speed
You want to document decisions before buildingFeature is clear and you just need the execution
/craft runs /shape internally as its first phase. If you use /craft, you don't need to run /shape separately first.
Ready-made prompts
★ Start a shape session — general
I want to plan a new [feature/page/component] before writing any code. Run /impeccable shape and ask me all the discovery questions. Feature I'm planning: [describe it in 1-2 sentences] I will answer each question carefully. After the interview, produce a design brief (brief.md) that I can review before we build anything. Do NOT write any code yet. This is planning only.
Shape for admin/backend page
Run /impeccable shape for a new admin page. Page purpose: [what this admin page manages — e.g. "lets staff view and process customer orders"] Who uses it: [e.g. "office staff, 5-10 people, use this 6+ hours a day, not technical"] Key actions on this page: [list the main things they need to do] Current pain point: [what's wrong with what they have now, if anything] Ask me your discovery questions. After the interview, produce a brief that focuses on efficiency and clarity — admin users prioritise speed of task completion over aesthetics.
Shape for customer-facing page
Run /impeccable shape for a customer-facing [page type]. Context: [describe the product and who the customers are] This page appears in the flow: [where in the user journey — e.g. "after signup, before first use"] Goal of this page: [what the customer should do or feel here] Brand tone: [how it should feel — professional, friendly, minimal, etc.] Ask all your discovery questions. I want a brief before any code is written.
🎨 DESIGN — ImpeccablePlaywright MCP
Build New UI
Three commands for building from scratch. /shape thinks, /craft builds the full flow, /live iterates in the browser. Know which to use and when.

The three build commands

/impeccable shape

Discovery interview → design brief only. No code. Use when you want to plan before building.

/impeccable craft

Full pipeline: shape interview → load references → build → visual iteration. The complete build command.

/impeccable live

Browser-based iteration on an existing element. Picks an element, generates 3 variants, writes the accepted one to source.

Rule of thumb: New feature from zero → use /craft. Small change to what exists → use /live. Just want the plan first → use /shape.

/impeccable craft — the full build

Craft runs 4 phases in order. None are skippable:

Phase 1 — Shape
Discovery interview about purpose, users, constraints. Produces a brief.
Phase 2 — Load references
Loads the right reference files (typography, colour, motion, layout) based on the brief.
Phase 3 — Build
Builds in order: structure → spacing → type → colour → states → motion → responsive.
Phase 4 — Visual iteration
Opens in browser, checks against brief and anti-patterns, refines until it matches intent.
★ New feature — full craft flow
/impeccable craft [describe the feature] Before starting the discovery interview, read PRODUCT.md and DESIGN.md so you understand the product context. After the interview, show me the design brief before building. I want to approve the direction first. When building: - Match our existing design system from DESIGN.md - Avoid AI-default choices: no Inter font, no purple gradients, no card-in-card layouts - Build fully working code with all states (empty, loading, error, success) - Make it responsive — mobile matters After building, use Playwright MCP to: 1. Navigate to the page in the browser 2. Take a full-page screenshot 3. Tell me what looks off or needs refinement
⚡ Standard — Server Detection (include at start of every browser test)
Before navigating anywhere:
1. Check if a dev server is running — try these ports in order:
   localhost:3000  localhost:3001  localhost:5173  localhost:4321  localhost:8000  localhost:8080
2. If a server responds → use that URL for all subsequent steps
3. If nothing responds → check package.json for "dev" or "start" script and run it.
   Wait until it says it's ready before proceeding.
4. If it fails to start, or there is no package.json → STOP.
   Tell me what happened. I will provide a live staging or production URL to use instead.
Document which URL you are using before doing anything else.
Build a new admin page from scratch
/impeccable craft admin [page name] for [describe what it manages] Admin context: - These pages are used by: [describe the staff who use this] - Primary task on this page: [what they need to do most often] - Data this page shows: [what data/records are displayed] Design direction for admin pages: - Prioritise efficiency and clarity over decoration - Dense but readable — these users spend hours here - Clear visual hierarchy: primary action obvious, secondary actions secondary - Good table/list design is more important than a beautiful hero After building, run Playwright MCP: 1. Navigate to the page 2. Screenshot it at desktop and mobile viewport 3. Check all interactive elements visually Then run: /impeccable audit [page] Fix any P0 and P1 issues found.

/impeccable live — browser iteration

Use live mode when something already exists and you want to visually iterate on a specific element. Not for greenfield builds — use /craft for those.

/impeccable live

Then in the browser: pick an element → describe what you want → hit Go → 3 variants appear → accept one → it writes to your source file.

Live mode — iterate on a specific element
/impeccable live Before starting, make sure the dev server is running. [Include server detection block above] I want to iterate on: [describe the element — e.g. "the pricing card" or "the navigation header"] After the live session, use Chrome DevTools to: - Check the console for any new errors introduced by the accepted variant - Check that no existing styles were broken
⚡ Standard — Server Detection (include at start of every browser test)
Before navigating anywhere:
1. Check if a dev server is running — try these ports in order:
   localhost:3000  localhost:3001  localhost:5173  localhost:4321  localhost:8000  localhost:8080
2. If a server responds → use that URL for all subsequent steps
3. If nothing responds → check package.json for "dev" or "start" script and run it.
   Wait until it says it's ready before proceeding.
4. If it fails to start, or there is no package.json → STOP.
   Tell me what happened. I will provide a live staging or production URL to use instead.
Document which URL you are using before doing anything else.
Live mode is for element-level iteration only. For whole-page redesigns, reach for /craft or /impeccable. Live mode on an element that touches global styles can cause unintended changes across the site.

After any build — standard visual check

Check console + network

Post-build verification (Playwright + DevTools)
After the build is done, run a quick verification with all 3 tools. [Include server detection block] Playwright MCP: 1. Navigate to [URL/page] 2. Take a full-page screenshot at 1440px wide 3. Take a screenshot at 375px wide (mobile) 4. Click every interactive element — buttons, links, dropdowns 5. Note anything that looks broken or unexpected Chrome DevTools MCP: 1. Check the console — list every error and warning 2. Check the network tab — list any failed requests (4xx, 5xx) 3. Check for any layout shift warnings Impeccable: /impeccable audit [area] Focus on P0 and P1 items only Report: - Screenshots (desktop + mobile) - Console errors: [list] - Failed requests: [list] - Audit findings: [list P0s and P1s] - My recommendation on what to fix first

🎨 DESIGN — ImpeccablePlaywright MCP
Onboarding & Empty States
/impeccable onboard designs first-run experiences, empty states, and setup flows. These are the moments that decide whether a new user sticks around or leaves. Most products get them completely wrong.
The core question /onboard asks: What is the "aha moment" — the moment a new user first understands the product's value? Every design decision it makes points toward getting the user there as fast as possible.

What /onboard covers

First-run experience

The moment immediately after signup. Should users see a tour, a blank canvas, a filled example, or nothing? /onboard picks the approach that fits the product.

Empty states

Every zero-data screen gets oriented: where am I, why is this empty, what do I do next, what will it look like when it's full. "No items yet" and nothing else is not an empty state.

Setup flows

Required configuration is minimised, defaults are smart, each step explains why it matters. The goal is minimum friction to first value.

Activation events

The moment a user first experiences the core value. /onboard instruments and celebrates this quietly — not with a big modal, with a toast that says "you did it".

Common failure modes /onboard resists: (1) Over-tutorialised onboarding where users click through a carousel before they can touch anything. (2) Zero onboarding where users are dropped into an empty app and expected to figure it out. Both kill activation.
When to use /onboard
  • New users drop off before reaching value — activation rate is low
  • Empty states just say "no items yet" with nothing else
  • First-run experience is confusing or doesn't show value quickly
  • Users say "I didn't know what to do when I first signed up"
  • You're launching a new feature and want to introduce it properly
  • Admin system has no guidance for new staff members
★ Design onboarding for a new product
/impeccable onboard [describe the product and the feature/flow] Context: - Product: [what it does in one sentence] - New user's goal: [what they signed up wanting to accomplish] - The "aha moment": [the moment they first understand the value — e.g. "when they see their first report generated"] - User experience level: [are they technical? Have they used similar tools?] - Time they have: [do they have 2 minutes or 20 minutes for setup?] Design the following: 1. First-run screen — what should a brand new user see? 2. Empty state for [main data list/view] — what do we show when they have no data? 3. Setup flow — what's the minimum they need to configure to get to the aha moment? 4. Activation moment — how do we celebrate when they first reach the core value? Do NOT add a product tour carousel. Focus on getting them to value fast.
Fix empty states on existing pages
/impeccable onboard [area with empty states] The following pages/views currently have unhelpful empty states: - [Page 1]: currently shows [describe current empty state, e.g. "just says 'no data'"] - [Page 2]: currently shows [describe] - [Page 3]: currently shows [describe] For each empty state, redesign it to answer these questions: 1. Where am I? (orient the user) 2. Why is this empty? (explain the state) 3. What do I do next? (clear call to action) 4. What will it look like when it's full? (show a preview or example) After redesigning, use Playwright MCP to verify the empty states display correctly: 1. Navigate to each page with no data loaded 2. Screenshot each empty state 3. Confirm the CTA is clickable and goes somewhere useful

Admin system — new staff onboarding
/impeccable onboard the admin system for new staff members Admin context: - Staff role: [who they are — e.g. "customer service staff, non-technical"] - First tasks they need to do: [the first 2-3 things a new staff member needs to accomplish] - Current situation: [do they currently get a walkthrough from a colleague? Or are they thrown in?] - System complexity: [how many main sections/pages does the admin have?] Design: 1. First-login experience — what does a new staff member see? 2. Empty states for each main section that makes sense to guide them 3. Helper text / tooltips for the most confusing parts of the UI 4. A "quick start" guide or checklist visible on the dashboard Prioritise efficiency guidance over product promotion. Staff aren't choosing this tool — they have to use it. Make it as clear and fast as possible.
🎨 DESIGN — ImpeccableChrome DevTools
Diagnose & Fix Existing UI
Something already exists and looks or works wrong. These commands identify the problem precisely. Always diagnose before you fix — the right diagnosis leads to the right command.
Golden rule: Always run /audit before changing anything. It gives you a scored report with severity levels. Fix P0s first, then P1s. Never start with /polish on something that hasn't been audited.

The two diagnostic commands

/impeccable audit — technical quality check

Scans across 5 dimensions. Scores each 0-4. Tags every finding P0 (blocks release) to P3 (polish). Returns a report. Makes NO changes to your code.

/impeccable critique — design quality review

LLM design review + deterministic anti-pattern detector running in parallel. Scores Nielsen's 10 heuristics, runs cognitive load checks, tests through persona lenses. Also makes NO changes.

Audit vs Critique: Audit asks "does this hold up technically?" Critique asks "is this actually any good as a design?" Run both for a complete picture. They catch different things.

The 5 audit dimensions

Accessibility
WCAG contrast, ARIA labels, keyboard nav, semantic HTML, form labels. Most sites fail here.
Performance
Layout thrashing, expensive animations, missing lazy loading, bundle weight.
Theming
Hard-coded colours, dark mode coverage, design token consistency.
Responsive
Breakpoint behaviour, touch targets, mobile viewport handling, overflow.
Anti-patterns
25 deterministic checks — gradient text, nested cards, purple palettes, side-tab borders, overused fonts.
Severity scale
P0 Blocks release — fix before shipping anything P1 Fix this sprint — significant impact on users P2 Next cycle — noticeable but not blocking P3 Polish — minor, fix when time allows

Adding Chrome DevTools to the diagnosis

Impeccable audits your source code. Chrome DevTools sees what the browser actually computed at runtime. They catch different problems. Together they're complete.

/audit [area]
→
Check console + network
→
/critique [area]
→
Combined priority list
★ Full diagnostic — all tools combined
Run a complete diagnosis on [page/area]. Do not fix anything yet — this is assessment only. Step 1 — Impeccable code audit: /impeccable audit [area] List all findings by severity (P0 first, then P1, P2, P3). Step 2 — Chrome DevTools runtime check: Open Chrome DevTools on [URL/page]. Check and report: - Console tab: every error and warning (type, message, file, line if available) - Network tab: every failed request (URL, status code, what it was trying to do) - Any CORS errors - Any 'undefined is not a function' or similar runtime errors Step 3 — Impeccable design critique: /impeccable critique [area] Report the AI slop verdict, heuristic scores, and top 3-5 priority issues. Step 4 — Combined priority list: Merge all findings into one list: - P0 items (anything that blocks the page working or that fails WCAG AA) - P1 items (significant quality issues) - P2/P3 items (improvements) - For each item: what it is, why it matters, which command fixes it

Diagnose a specific broken area
Something is wrong with [describe the problem — e.g. 'the modal doesn't close', 'the table overflows on mobile', 'the form error states don't show']. Investigate using all available tools: Chrome DevTools: 1. Navigate to [URL] and reproduce the problem 2. Check the console for errors at the moment the problem occurs 3. If it's a network issue, check the network tab for the failing request 4. If it's a layout issue, inspect the computed CSS of the problematic element Playwright MCP: 1. Navigate to [URL] 2. Problem → Right command lookup

What you're seeingDiagnose withFix with
Looks like generic AI output/critique/bolder or /impeccable craft
Fonts feel wrong or unreadable/audit (anti-patterns dim)/typeset
Spacing feels random or cramped/critique (hierarchy)/layout
Everything is grey, no personality/critique/colorize
Page fails contrast checks/audit (accessibility dim) + DevTools a11y tree/harden + manual fix
Broken on mobilePlaywright viewport + /audit responsive/adapt
Copy is confusing/critique (UX writing)/clarify
JavaScript errorsChrome DevTools consoleFix in code, then /audit
API calls failingChrome DevTools network tabBackend fix, not Impeccable
Loads slowly/audit (performance) + DevTools performance tab/optimize
Missing error/empty/loading statesPlaywright: trigger those states/harden
Design too loud/critique/quieter or /distill
Design too boring/critique/bolder
🎨 DESIGN — Impeccable
Refine One Thing at a Time
The overall direction is right but one specific dimension needs adjusting. These 8 commands each target exactly one design quality. Use them after the main design work is done — not as a first step.
These are dials, not hammers. Use them after /craft has built something solid. Running /bolder on an unfinished page is premature — get the structure right first, then refine.

/impeccable animate [target]

Adds purposeful motion — page load sequences with staggered reveals, meaningful state transitions, hover rewards. Prioritises CSS-only solutions. Respects prefers-reduced-motion. Avoids bounce/elastic easing.

/impeccable bolder [target]

Amplifies a timid, safe, or generic design. Stronger contrast, bolder hierarchy, more personality. Use when the design plays it too safe or looks like every other site.

/impeccable colorize [target]

Introduces strategic colour where the UI is all greys. Not decoration — colour that communicates: brand, status, hierarchy. Avoids the purple gradient default.

/impeccable delight [target]

Adds moments of joy. Small surprises that make the product feel alive and intentional — a satisfying success animation, a clever empty state, an unexpected hover. One or two well-placed, not scattered.

/impeccable layout [target]

Fixes spatial design — spacing system, grid, rhythm, density, visual flow. Use when things feel cramped, randomly spaced, or visually unbalanced without a clear reason why.

/impeccable overdrive [target]

Technically extraordinary effects — shaders, physics, 60fps, cinematic transitions. For hero moments only. Overkill for standard UI. Use this sparingly, deliberately.

/impeccable quieter [target]

Tones down a loud, overwhelming, or noisy design. Reduces visual competition, strips unnecessary decoration, creates breathing room. The opposite of /bolder.

/impeccable typeset [target]

Fixes font choices, type scale, weight usage, line height, letter spacing, hierarchy. Avoids Inter, Roboto, Arial, Space Grotesk, Plus Jakarta Sans, Fraunces. Pairs a distinctive display font with a refined body font.

Combining refine commands

# Design that feels boring and static: /impeccable bolder hero → /impeccable animate hero → /impeccable delight success-screen # Design that's too visually noisy: /impeccable distill sidebar → /impeccable quieter dashboard # Type-heavy page that reads poorly: /impeccable typeset → /impeccable layout → /impeccable polish
Ready-made prompts
Animate — purposeful motion, not decoration
/impeccable animate [area] Guidance: - Page load: staggered reveals with animation-delay so elements don't all appear at once - State transitions: meaningful (not instant snaps, not 500ms waits) - Hover: rewards that feel good but don't distract - Respect prefers-reduced-motion — wrap all animations in a media query Avoid: - Bounce or elastic easing (feels like 2014) - Anything that delays the user reaching content by more than 200ms - Decorative looping animations with no functional purpose - More than 3 distinct animation types on one page
Typeset — fix generic or unreadable typography
/impeccable typeset [area] The typography currently feels: [describe problem — too generic / inconsistent sizes / wrong font for the brand / no hierarchy] When resetting: - Avoid overused fonts: Inter, Roboto, Arial, Space Grotesk, Plus Jakarta Sans, Geist, Fraunces, Recoleta - Pair a distinctive display font with a highly readable body font - Maximum 4-5 distinct type sizes across the page - Body text: 16px minimum, line-height 1.6-1.7, comfortable measure (60-75 chars per line) - Only 2 font weights in body text — regular and medium/semibold - Heading scale should feel decisive, not accidental
Bolder — amplify without breaking brand
/impeccable bolder [area] The design is too safe and generic. Make it bolder while staying within our brand context from PRODUCT.md. Options (use whichever fits best, don't use all): - Stronger typographic contrast between heading and body - More decisive use of brand colour — less grey, more intent - Tighter, more confident spacing — not everything needs to breathe - Stronger visual hierarchy — make the most important thing undeniably the most important Do not add decoration for its own sake. Bold means intentional, not loud.
🎨 DESIGN — Impeccable
Simplify — Adapt, Clarify, Distill
Three commands for reducing complexity. Use when a page has accumulated clutter, unclear language, or doesn't work across all devices. Subtraction is a design skill.

/impeccable adapt [target]

Makes designs work across screens, devices, and contexts without amputating features. Use when a desktop design needs a proper mobile version — not just a shrunk version.

/impeccable clarify [target]

Rewrites confusing UX copy so the interface explains itself. Buttons, error messages, empty state text, placeholder text, tooltips. Generic copy ('Submit', 'An error occurred') kills perceived quality.

/impeccable distill [target]

Ruthless subtraction. Strips a design to its essential elements. Use when a page has accumulated 3 iterations of additions and now feels like it's doing 5 things at once.

When to reach for each

CommandUse whenOutput
/adaptDesktop layout is broken on mobile. Navigation collapses badly. Tables overflow. Touch targets are too small.Responsive implementation that works at all sizes without losing features.
/clarifyUsers are confused about what to do. Buttons say "Submit". Error messages say "Something went wrong". Placeholders say "Enter text here".Copy that explains itself. Every piece of text earns its place.
/distillPage is cluttered. Three competing calls-to-action. Sidebar with 12 items. Page that tries to do everything at once.Stripped version with only what actually earns its place.
Adapt — with Playwright for verification
★ Adapt for mobile — with visual verification
/impeccable adapt [area] for mobile and tablet Focus on: - Navigation that works with a thumb on a 375px wide screen - Tables that scroll or reformat gracefully — no horizontal overflow - Touch targets that are at least 44x44px - Text that doesn't require pinch-zooming to read - Forms that work with mobile keyboards (correct input types, no viewport zoom) After adapting, use Playwright MCP to verify: [Include server detection block] Set viewport to 375x812 (iPhone): - Navigate to [URL] - Take full-page screenshot - Scroll through the entire page - Try tapping the main CTA button Set viewport to 768x1024 (iPad): - Same checks Report any issues still present after the adapt.

Clarify — UX copy overhaul
/impeccable clarify [area] Review and rewrite all of the following: 1. Button labels — do they say what happens when you click them? ("Save changes" not "Submit") 2. Error messages — do they explain what went wrong AND what to do? ("Email already in use — try logging in instead" not "Error") 3. Empty states — do they explain why empty and what to do next? 4. Form placeholders — do they show example formats? ("john@example.com" not "Email") 5. Confirmation messages — do they confirm what specifically happened? 6. Loading states — do they say what's loading? 7. Section headings — do they describe the content, not just label it? Tone: [match the brand tone from PRODUCT.md] Reading level: Plain language. If a 14-year-old can understand it, it's clear enough.
Distill — strip to the essential
/impeccable distill [area] This page/component has accumulated too much. I want to strip it to its essential elements. What currently exists: [describe all the things on it] What users actually come here to do: [the one or two real jobs] Distillation criteria — a feature stays only if: 1. Users need it to complete their primary task, OR 2. Removing it causes a real problem Everything else should go, be moved, or be hidden behind a disclosure. After distilling, verify with Playwright: 1. Navigate to the page 2. Screenshot the before state (or describe it) 3. Screenshot the after state 4. Confirm the primary task is now faster and more obvious

🎨 DESIGN ★ FEATURED — All 3 Tools
Backend / Admin UI Redesign
You have a fully working backend — all the logic, database, APIs, and features are correct. But the admin pages look terrible. You want to redesign the UI to professional standard without touching a single line of backend logic.
The golden rule for this scenario: Only touch UI/template files. Never modify route handlers, API files, database schemas, business logic, or auth. Establish this boundary explicitly before starting — and repeat it in every prompt.

Monorepo vs Separate Repos

Monorepo (frontend + backend together)
Separate repos
Monorepo risk: Claude Code can see all files. Add explicit safe/unsafe file boundaries at the start of every session.
Monorepo safety — add this at the start of every session
IMPORTANT — Read these file boundaries before touching anything: SAFE TO EDIT (UI layer only): - /[your template folder]/** - /[your components folder]/** - /[your styles folder]/** - Files ending in: .html, .css, .scss, [your template extension] NEVER TOUCH — backend logic: - /[your API/routes folder]/** - /[your controllers/models folder]/** - /[your database folder]/** - Files ending in: .sql, .prisma, .env, .env.*, migration files Rules: 1. If a file contains both UI and data-fetching logic (e.g. a React page with useEffect+fetch), STOP and ask me before editing it 2. Never rename a CSS class without checking if it's used in JavaScript first 3. Never change a form field's name attribute 4. Never modify anything in [list specific protected files/folders] Confirm you've read these before we begin.
Separate repos: Open Claude Code in the frontend repo only. The backend is invisible — no risk of touching it. Just document the API contract so the AI understands what data is available.
Separate repos — orient Claude Code
This is the frontend-only repository. The backend is in a separate repository and is not accessible from here. How the frontend connects to the backend: - API type: [REST / GraphQL / tRPC] - Base API URL in dev: [e.g. http://localhost:8000/api or environment variable] - Auth method: [JWT in Authorization header / session cookies / API key] - Key endpoints used in the admin: - GET [/endpoint] — [what it returns] - POST [/endpoint] — [what it does] [list main endpoints] All API calls already exist in the codebase — do not change them. Your job is to redesign the visual layer only. The data fetching, state management, and API integration stays exactly as it is.

Stack-specific notes

Supabase + Next.js/React

Never touch: /api routes, supabase.from() calls, auth files. Safe: /app page components UI layer, /components, /styles. Keep all React hooks and data-fetching logic intact — only change JSX structure and className props.

Django + Templates

Never touch: views.py, models.py, urls.py, forms.py logic. Safe: templates/ HTML files, static/css/. Keep all Django template tags ({ var }, {% block %}, {% url %}) exactly as they are.

Laravel + Blade

Never touch: app/Http/Controllers/, routes/, app/Models/. Safe: resources/views/ Blade files, resources/css/. Keep all Blade directives (@foreach, @if, { $var }) intact.

Node/Express + EJS/Pug

Never touch: route handler functions, app.js/server.js logic, database queries. Safe: views/ template files, public/css/. Keep all template variables (<%- var %>, #{var}) exactly as they are.

🎨 DESIGN — Impeccable
Design System
Build and maintain a design system so every page and component stays consistent automatically. Once established, every /craft, /audit, and /polish reads your design system and stays on-brand without you specifying it every time.

/impeccable teach

Sets up PRODUCT.md and DESIGN.md — the two context files that every command reads. Run once per project.

/impeccable extract [description]

Pulls reusable components and design tokens out of existing code and documents them. Run after building several pages.

/impeccable document

Generates or updates DESIGN.md with current design tokens, typography scale, spacing system, and component patterns.

Build a design system from scratch

1
Run /impeccable teach
Gather project context. Answer the questions carefully. This creates PRODUCT.md and DESIGN.md with initial values.
2
Build 2-3 pages with /impeccable craft
Don't extract yet. Build enough real pages to have real patterns to extract from. You need actual code to pull tokens from.
3
Extract the patterns
Run /impeccable extract to identify what's repeating — buttons, cards, form styles, table styles. These become your component library.
4
Document the system
Run /impeccable document to generate a complete DESIGN.md. Review and annotate — add any brand decisions that weren't captured automatically.
5
Every future page references the system
From this point, every command reads DESIGN.md automatically. New pages stay consistent without manual specification.
★ Build design system from existing pages
/impeccable extract — build a design system from our existing codebase Step 1 — Inventory: Look at these pages: [list pages or folders] What visual patterns are repeating across them? What is inconsistent? Step 2 — Extract: Extract reusable definitions for at minimum: - Button variants (primary, secondary, destructive) - Form input and label styling - Card/panel component - Heading type scale (all sizes in use) - Colour tokens (map every colour used to a token name) - Spacing scale (what spacing values are actually being used) Step 3 — Document: /impeccable document Generate DESIGN.md with all extracted tokens and components. Step 4 — Report: Tell me: - What was extracted and is now documented - What inconsistencies exist in the current codebase (different values used for the same purpose) - What components would benefit from being unified
🔍 TEST & QA — All 3 Tools
Visual Audit — All 3 Tools Together
The most complete audit you can run. Impeccable checks your source code. Chrome DevTools checks what the browser computed. Playwright checks what a real user sees. Together they catch things no single tool can find alone.
When to run this: Before any major client review, before launching, after a significant redesign, or when something "feels off" but you can't say exactly what. This is your full-picture health check.

Console + network

/critique (design)
→
Combined priority list

🔍 TEST & QA — Playwright MCPChrome DevTools
End-to-End User Journey Testing
Test a complete user flow from start to finish — like a real user would experience it. Playwright follows the journey, Chrome DevTools monitors what's happening behind the scenes at every step.
What E2E testing catches that code review doesn't: Things that work individually but break in sequence. A form field that validates correctly alone but fails after a page transition. A dashboard that loads correctly but breaks after logging out and back in.

Common journeys to test

JourneyWhy it matters
Sign up → complete setup → reach key featureNew user activation — most critical path
Log in → perform primary task → log outDaily user workflow — most frequently used
Admin: log in → find record → edit → saveCore admin workflow
Fill form → submit → see confirmationAny form conversion — if this breaks, revenue stops
Search → filter → find item → take actionData management workflows
Error path: try to do something → fail → recoverError handling — most tested last, most seen by users
★ Full E2E journey test — complete prompt
Run a complete end-to-end test of the [describe the journey, e.g. 'user signup to dashboard'] flow. ────────────────────────────────────────── SETUP ────────────────────────────────────────── Before navigating anywhere: 1. Check if a dev server is running — try these ports in order: localhost:3000 localhost:3001 localhost:5173 localhost:4321 localhost:8000 localhost:8080 2. If a server responds → use that URL for all subsequent steps 3. If nothing responds → check package.json for "dev" or "start" script and run it. Wait until it says it's ready before proceeding. 4. If it fails to start, or there is no package.json → STOP. Tell me what happened. I will provide a live staging or production URL to use instead. Document which URL you are using before doing anything else. 🔍 TEST & QA — Playwright MCPChrome DevToolsImpeccable
Form & Interaction Testing
Forms are where things break most often and where users give up most easily. Test every state: happy path, empty submission, invalid data, keyboard-only navigation, and network failure. Then fix what's missing.
Why forms need special attention: A form that works with valid data might silently fail with edge-case input. Error messages that don't appear, success states that are ambiguous, and fields that lose data on validation errors are the most common client complaints.

The 5 form test scenarios

TestWhat it checks
Happy path (valid data)Does submission work? Does the success state show? Does the right API call go out?
Empty submissionDoes validation fire on required fields? Are error messages helpful?
Invalid dataDoes each field validate correctly? Are error messages specific to the field?
Keyboard-only navigationCan you tab through all fields and submit without a mouse?
After server errorIf the API call fails, does the user know what happened and what to do?
★ Complete form test — all 5 scenarios

Start network recording. ══════════════════════════════════════ TEST 1 — HAPPY PATH (valid data) ══════════════════════════════════════ Fill in all fields with valid test data: [list each field and valid test value] Submit the form. Check with Chrome DevTools: - What network request was sent? (method, URL, payload) - What was the response? (status code, response body) Expected UI behaviour: [describe — e.g. "success message appears, user redirected to dashboard"] Screenshot: the success state PASS if: correct request sent, correct response received, UI shows clear success FAIL if: [any deviation] ══════════════════════════════════════ TEST 2 — EMPTY SUBMISSION ══════════════════════════════════════ Clear all fields. Click submit immediately. Expected: Validation errors appear on each required field Each error should say specifically what's needed (not just "required") Screenshot: the validation state List every field and whether its error message is: ✓ Clear and helpful / ✗ Missing / ✗ Generic ("This field is required" is too generic) ══════════════════════════════════════ TEST 3 — INVALID DATA ══════════════════════════════════════ Fill with these invalid values and submit: - Email field: "notanemail" → Expected: "Please enter a valid email address" - [Password field if exists]: "123" (too short) → Expected: specific password requirement error - [Phone field if exists]: "abc" → Expected: "Please enter a valid phone number" - [Any other validatable field]: [invalid value] Screenshot: invalid validation states ══════════════════════════════════════ TEST 4 — KEYBOARD-ONLY NAVIGATION ══════════════════════════════════════ Using only the keyboard (no mouse): 1. 🔍 TEST & QA — Playwright MCPChrome DevToolsImpeccable
Mobile & Responsive Testing
Test how your UI actually looks and works at different screen sizes. Playwright sets the viewport, takes screenshots, and tests interactions at each size. Chrome DevTools checks for overflow and layout issues. /adapt fixes what's found.
Most common mobile failures: Horizontal scroll (content is wider than viewport), tiny tap targets (buttons too small to press accurately), unreadable text (too small or too wide a measure), navigation that collapses badly, tables that overflow.

Standard breakpoints to test

ViewportRepresentsPriority
375 x 812iPhone SE / standard Android⭐ Always test
390 x 844iPhone 14Test if mobile is important
430 x 932iPhone Plus / large AndroidGood to check
768 x 1024iPad portrait⭐ Always test
1024 x 768iPad landscape / small laptopTest for admin UIs
1440 x 900Standard desktop⭐ Always test

Issues to flag: [overflow / unreadable text / tiny buttons / broken nav] ══════════════════ 768px WIDE (Tablet) ══════════════════ Set viewport to 768x1024. Navigate to [URL]. Full page screenshot. 🔍 TEST & QA — Chrome DevToolsPlaywright MCPImpeccable
Performance & Network Audit
Slow pages lose users. Chrome DevTools measures what's actually slow. Playwright can test load behaviour. /impeccable optimize fixes the code-level issues found.
The 3 metrics that matter most: LCP (Largest Contentful Paint — how fast does the main content appear), CLS (Cumulative Layout Shift — does the page jump around as it loads), FID/INP (how fast does the page respond to interaction). All should be in "good" range for a professional product.

Good performance targets

MetricGoodNeeds workPoor
LCP (load)<2.5s2.5–4s>4s
CLS (layout shift)<0.10.1–0.25>0.25
INP (interaction)<200ms200–500ms>500ms
Page weight<1MB1–3MB>3MB
Number of requests<5050–100>100
★ Full performance audit — Chrome DevTools + Playwright
Run a complete performance audit on [page/URL]. [Include server detection block] ────────────────────────────────────────── STEP 1 — NETWORK ANALYSIS (Chrome DevTools) ────────────────────────────────────────── Open Chrome DevTools → Network tab. Do a hard reload (Ctrl+Shift+R) on [URL]. Wait for page to fully load. Report: - Total page weight: [KB/MB] - Total number of requests: [count] - Largest resources (top 5 by size): [file name, size, type] - Slowest resources (top 5 by load time): [file name, time, type] - Any failed requests: [list] - Any blocked-by-CORS requests: [list] - Images that are larger than 200KB: [list — these are almost always unoptimised] - Any render-blocking resources (CSS/JS in without async/defer): [list] ────────────────────────────────────────── STEP 2 — PERFORMANCE TIMELINE (Chrome DevTools) ────────────────────────────────────────── Go to Performance tab → Record → Reload page → Stop. Report: - Time to First Contentful Paint (FCP): [time] - Time to Largest Contentful Paint (LCP): [time] - Any long tasks (>50ms) that block the main thread: [list] - Total blocking time: [time] - Layout shift events: [count and when they happen] ────────────────────────────────────────── STEP 3 — PLAYWRIGHT LOAD TEST ────────────────────────────────────────── Using Playwright: 1. Navigate to [URL] with network throttling if possible (4G: 20Mbps down) 2. Take a screenshot at: 0.5s, 1s, 2s, 3s after navigation starts 3. Note what the user sees at each interval 4. Is the page usable before it's fully loaded? 5. Does content jump around as it loads (layout shift)? ────────────────────────────────────────── STEP 4 — IMPECCABLE FIX ────────────────────────────────────────── Based on findings, run: /impeccable optimize [area] Focus on the specific issues found: - Images: [list unoptimised ones] - Render-blocking resources: [list] - Bundle size: [if large JS bundles identified] Report: before/after comparison of key metrics

🔍 TEST & QA — Chrome DevToolsPlaywright MCPImpeccable
Accessibility Deep Dive
Accessibility problems are often invisible until you look for them. Chrome DevTools reads the accessibility tree. Playwright tests keyboard navigation. /audit's accessibility dimension finds code-level issues. All three together give you a complete WCAG picture.
Why this matters beyond compliance: Accessibility issues affect everyone — not just users with disabilities. Low contrast hurts anyone in sunlight. Keyboard navigation matters for power users. Clear error messages help every user. Fix accessibility and you fix usability.

What each tool checks

Chrome DevTools

Accessibility tree — what screen readers actually see. Contrast ratios. ARIA labels. Element roles. Form label associations.

Playwright MCP

Keyboard navigation — can you tab through the whole page? Focus order logical? Focus indicator visible? Interactive elements reachable?

Impeccable /audit

Accessibility dimension — semantic HTML, ARIA attributes, form labels, heading hierarchy, alt text, skip links.

★ Complete accessibility audit — all 3 tools
Run a complete accessibility audit on [page/area]. Target: WCAG 2.1 AA compliance. [Include server detection block] ══════════════════════════════════════ PART 1 — CHROME DEVTOOLS: ACCESSIBILITY TREE ══════════════════════════════════════ Open Chrome DevTools → Elements tab → Accessibility panel (or Accessibility tab). Navigate to [URL] and check: Heading structure: - Is there exactly one H1? - Do headings follow a logical hierarchy? (H1 → H2 → H3, no skips) - List the heading structure found Form labels: - Does every form input have an associated label? - Are placeholders being used as labels? (they shouldn't be — they disappear) - List any inputs without proper labels Images: - Do all meaningful images have descriptive alt text? - Do decorative images have empty alt=""? - List any images with missing or unhelpful alt text (e.g. alt="image.jpg") ARIA: - Are ARIA roles used correctly? - Any ARIA attributes that reference IDs which don't exist? Contrast (use DevTools CSS Overview or contrast checker): - Body text on background: [ratio] — needs to be 4.5:1 minimum - Large text / headings: [ratio] — needs to be 3:1 minimum - UI components (buttons, inputs): [ratio] — needs to be 3:1 - List any failing contrast pairs ══════════════════════════════════════ PART 2 — PLAYWRIGHT: KEYBOARD NAVIGATION ══════════════════════════════════════ Navigate to [URL] using only keyboard (no mouse clicks): 1. Press Tab — note where focus goes first. Is it logical? 2. Press Tab 10 more times — note the focus order. Is it the reading order? 3. Note if focus indicator is visible at each stop (outline, highlight, etc.) 4. Try to activate the main interactive elements using Enter and Space 5. Try to reach the footer by tabbing — can you get there? 6. If there's a modal/dropdown: open it, check you can navigate inside it, check Escape closes it 7. Check: does focus return to the trigger button when a modal closes? Report: - Focus order: logical / illogical - Focus indicator: always visible / sometimes missing / often missing - Unreachable elements by keyboard: [list] - Modal focus trapping: correct / broken ══════════════════════════════════════ PART 3 — IMPECCABLE CODE AUDIT ══════════════════════════════════════ /impeccable audit [area] Focus specifically on the Accessibility dimension score. List ALL findings in the accessibility category, P0 through P3. ══════════════════════════════════════ COMBINED REPORT ══════════════════════════════════════ Critical (WCAG AA failures — must fix): - [issue]: [location] [how to fix] Important (usability issues): - [issue]: [location] [how to fix] Improvements: - [issue]: [location] [how to fix] Then run: /impeccable harden [area] Tell it specifically: "Focus on accessibility — missing form labels, contrast issues, and keyboard navigation problems found in audit."

🚢 SHIP — All 3 Tools
Pre-Launch Sequence
The exact sequence to run before every client delivery or production push. Non-negotiable. Run all of these in order — skipping steps is how things that look fine in development look broken in production.

The complete sequence

/audit
→
Console + network
→
E2E journey
→
/harden
→
/clarify
→
/optimize
→
Mobile check
→
/polish
Never skip /harden before /polish. Polish fixes visual consistency. Harden adds missing states. If you polish without hardening, you're making a beautiful page that breaks when users hit edge cases. Always harden first.
★ Complete pre-launch sequence — all tools
Run the complete pre-launch sequence for [project/area]. Go through every step in order. ────────────────────────────────────────── STEP 1 — IMPECCABLE AUDIT ────────────────────────────────────────── /impeccable audit [area] List all P0 and P1 findings. Fix all P0s before proceeding. P1s should be fixed before launch if possible. ────────────────────────────────────────── STEP 2 — RUNTIME CHECK (Chrome DevTools) ────────────────────────────────────────── Before navigating anywhere: 1. Check if a dev server is running — try these ports in order: localhost:3000 localhost:3001 localhost:5173 localhost:4321 localhost:8000 localhost:8080 2. If a server responds → use that URL for all subsequent steps 3. If nothing responds → check package.json for "dev" or "start" script and run it. Wait until it says it's ready before proceeding. 4. If it fails to start, or there is no package.json → STOP. Tell me what happened. I will provide a live staging or production URL to use instead. Document which URL you are using before doing anything else. Open Chrome DevTools on [URL]: - Console: any errors? Fix any that relate to the UI - Network: any failed requests? Investigate and fix - Performance tab: LCP under 3 seconds? ────────────────────────────────────────── STEP 3 — E2E JOURNEY TEST (Playwright) ────────────────────────────────────────── Test the primary user journey end-to-end: [Describe the main journey for this product] Use the E2E journey prompt from the E2E Testing page. Journey must PASS before proceeding. ────────────────────────────────────────── STEP 4 — HARDEN ────────────────────────────────────────── /impeccable harden [area] This adds: - Error states for every form and action - Empty states for every list and data view - Loading states for every async operation - Edge cases: what if the user has 0 items? 1000 items? Very long text? ────────────────────────────────────────── STEP 5 — CLARIFY ────────────────────────────────────────── /impeccable clarify [area] Review all copy: - Button labels: do they say what happens? - Error messages: do they explain what went wrong AND what to do? - Placeholder text: does it show a real example format? - Empty states: do they orient and guide the user? ────────────────────────────────────────── STEP 6 — MOBILE CHECK (Playwright) ────────────────────────────────────────── Set viewport to 375x812. Navigate to each main page. Screenshot and note any issues. Fix any critical mobile problems with /impeccable adapt. ────────────────────────────────────────── STEP 7 — OPTIMIZE ────────────────────────────────────────── /impeccable optimize [area] Based on the Chrome DevTools findings from Step 2. Focus on: any images over 200KB, render-blocking resources, bundle size. ────────────────────────────────────────── STEP 8 — FINAL POLISH ────────────────────────────────────────── /impeccable polish [area] This is the last command. It aligns everything with the design system, fixes minor inconsistencies, and ensures the design feels finished. ────────────────────────────────────────── COMPLETION CHECKLIST ────────────────────────────────────────── After all steps, confirm: □ /audit: zero P0s, P1s resolved or documented □ Console: zero errors □ Network: zero failed requests □ E2E journey: PASS □ All forms have error + success states □ All lists have empty states □ Mobile 375px: no horizontal overflow □ Copy reviewed: no placeholder text remaining □ /polish run: design system consistent

Client handoff checklist

  • /audit run — all critical issues resolved, P1s documented
  • Chrome DevTools console: zero errors on all pages
  • Chrome DevTools network: zero failed requests
  • E2E journey test: PASS for primary user flow
  • All forms tested: happy path, empty, invalid, keyboard-only
  • All lists/tables have empty states
  • Error states exist for all async actions
  • Mobile tested at 375px — no horizontal scroll
  • /clarify run — no "Submit" buttons, no "Error occurred" messages
  • /optimize run — no images over 500KB, LCP under 3s
  • /polish run — design consistent throughout
  • DESIGN.md updated with final design tokens
📚 REFERENCE
All 23 Commands
Complete reference for every Impeccable command, organised by category. Every command accepts an optional target to scope its action.

Create

CommandWhat it doesWhen to use
/impeccable [description]Direct build — builds immediately without the craft discovery flow. Reads PRODUCT.md and DESIGN.md first.You know exactly what you want, no discovery needed
/impeccable shapeDiscovery interview only → design brief (brief.md). No code written. The 5-10 question interview that prevents bad builds.Planning before building. Getting a brief stakeholders can approve.
/impeccable craftFull pipeline: shape → load references → build → visual iteration. 4 non-skippable phases.New feature from zero. Want the full workflow in one command.

Evaluate

CommandWhat it doesWhen to use
/impeccable audit5-dimension technical check: Accessibility, Performance, Theming, Responsive, Anti-patterns. P0–P3 severity. No changes made.Before any changes. Before every PR. Start of a quality sprint.
/impeccable critiqueLLM design review + deterministic detector in parallel. Nielsen heuristics, cognitive load, persona testing, AI slop verdict. No changes made.Page is functionally done. Want honest feedback on design quality.

Refine

CommandWhat it doesWhen to use
/impeccable animatePurposeful motion — load sequences, state transitions, hover rewards. CSS-only where possible. Respects prefers-reduced-motion.UI feels static after main design is solid.
/impeccable bolderAmplifies timid designs. Stronger contrast, decisive hierarchy, more personality.Design plays it too safe or looks generic.
/impeccable colorizeIntroduces strategic colour to monochrome interfaces. Avoids purple gradient default.Everything is grey, design lacks identity.
/impeccable delightSmall moments of personality — satisfying success animations, clever empty states, unexpected hovers.Design is functional but feels sterile.
/impeccable layoutFixes spacing system, grid, visual rhythm, and density.Spacing feels arbitrary, cramped, or randomly distributed.
/impeccable overdriveTechnically extraordinary effects — shaders, physics, 60fps, cinematic transitions.Hero moments only. Not for standard UI.
/impeccable quieterTones down loud or noisy designs. Reduces visual competition, more breathing room.Design is too busy or overwhelming.
/impeccable typesetFixes font choices, type scale, weight, line height, hierarchy. Avoids Inter/Roboto/Arial/Space Grotesk.Typography feels generic, inconsistent, or unreadable.

Simplify

CommandWhat it doesWhen to use
/impeccable adaptMakes designs work across screens without amputating features. Proper responsive implementation.Desktop design needs a real mobile version, not just a shrunk one.
/impeccable clarifyRewrites UX copy — buttons, errors, empty states, placeholders, tooltips. Makes interfaces self-explaining.Users confused about what to do. Placeholder copy remaining.
/impeccable distillRuthless subtraction. Strips to essential elements only.Page has accumulated clutter from multiple iterations.

Harden

CommandWhat it doesWhen to use
/impeccable hardenAdds error states, empty states, loading states, edge cases, i18n considerations. The stuff that gets missed.Before every client delivery. After /audit finds missing states.
/impeccable onboardDesigns first-run experiences, empty states, and setup flows. Points every decision at the user's "aha moment".New users drop off. Empty states say "no items yet" and nothing else.
/impeccable optimizePerformance: lazy loading, image optimisation, bundle size, render-blocking resources, animation performance.Performance audit finds issues. Pre-launch.
/impeccable polishFinal design pass: design system alignment, consistency check, spacing uniformity, token application.Last command before every PR, delivery, or deploy.

System

CommandWhat it doesWhen to use
/impeccable documentGenerates/updates DESIGN.md with tokens, typography, spacing, and component patterns.After building/extracting. When design system has drifted.
/impeccable extractPulls reusable components and design tokens from existing code into the design system.After building several pages. Before a redesign sprint.
/impeccable liveBrowser-based iteration. Pick any element, get 3 HMR variants, accept one → writes to source. Alpha.Visual iteration on an existing element. Needs running dev server.
/impeccable teachOne-time project setup. Creates PRODUCT.md and DESIGN.md from a discovery interview.First thing in every new project.
📚 REFERENCE
Problem → Fix Finder
Something is wrong. You don't know which command to use. Find your problem here and jump straight to the right fix.

Design problems

ProblemDiagnoseFix
Looks like generic AI output — Inter font, purple gradient, card soup/critique/bolder + /typeset + /colorize
Font feels wrong, generic, or unreadable/audit (anti-patterns)/typeset
Spacing is random, cramped, or unbalanced/critique/layout
Everything is grey, no visual interest/critique/colorize
Too much going on, overwhelming/critique/quieter or /distill
Too safe, boring, forgettable/critique/bolder or /delight
Feels static, no life/critique/animate + /delight
Copy is confusing or placeholder text is still therePlaywright: read the copy/clarify
New users don't know what to do firstPlaywright: simulate new user/onboard
Empty states just say "no items"Playwright: trigger empty states/onboard

Technical problems

ProblemDiagnoseFix
Page has JavaScript errorsChrome DevTools: console tabFix in code (not Impeccable)
API calls are failingChrome DevTools: network tabBackend fix, then verify with Playwright
Page loads slowlyChrome DevTools: performance + network/optimize
Images are huge and slow to loadChrome DevTools: network (sort by size)/optimize — compress images
Fails accessibility checksChrome DevTools: accessibility tree + /audit/harden for labels, fix contrast manually
Missing error statesPlaywright: trigger errors deliberately/harden
Missing loading statesPlaywright: slow network throttle/harden
Form doesn't submit correctlyPlaywright: submit + DevTools networkCheck field names first, then /harden

Responsive/mobile problems

ProblemDiagnoseFix
Horizontal scrollbar on mobilePlaywright: set viewport 375px + screenshot/adapt
Navigation broken on mobilePlaywright: mobile viewport + interact/adapt
Buttons too small to tap on mobilePlaywright + DevTools: check sizes/adapt
Tables overflow on mobilePlaywright: mobile viewport screenshot/adapt — specify: "fix table overflow"
Text too small on mobilePlaywright: screenshot at 375px/typeset + /adapt

Pre-launch problems

ProblemWhat to run
Don't know if anything is brokenVisual audit (all 3 tools) — see Visual Audit page
Don't know if user flows workE2E journey test — see E2E page
Don't know if forms workForm testing — see Form Testing page
Not sure if it's ready to shipPre-Launch Sequence — see Pre-Launch page
📚 REFERENCE
Best Practices
How to get the most out of all three tools based on how they actually work. What to do and what to avoid.

The non-negotiables

Always /impeccable teach first on every project

Without PRODUCT.md and DESIGN.md, every command guesses. 5 minutes here saves hours of bad output. Never skip it.

Always run /audit before /polish

Audit finds broken things. Polish fixes visual consistency. Polishing broken code produces beautiful broken code.

Always use Playwright to verify after changes

Impeccable edits files. The browser might render them differently. Always screenshot the result to confirm what you intended.

Always check DevTools console after any change

If Impeccable renames a CSS class that JavaScript references, you'll get silent breakage. DevTools shows you immediately.

Do this, not that

✓ DO
  • Run /impeccable shape before /craft for anything complex
  • Be specific with targets: /audit checkout form vs /audit
  • Add context to commands — who the audience is, what's wrong
  • Approve the design brief before building
  • Use Playwright to screenshot before AND after every redesign
  • Check DevTools console after every Impeccable change
  • Chain commands: /audit /polish [area]
  • Tell Claude Code explicitly which files are off-limits
  • Run /harden before /polish on every delivery
  • Update DESIGN.md when you make design decisions
✗ DON'T
  • Skip /impeccable teach and hope for the best
  • Run /polish as the first command on a new project
  • Let Claude Code touch backend files without explicit boundaries
  • Accept the first design direction without asking for alternatives
  • Use /overdrive for standard UI — it's for hero moments only
  • Run 5 commands in a row without reviewing what changed
  • Trust that Playwright screenshot = DevTools shows no errors
  • Skip accessibility testing because "we'll fix it later"
  • Run /craft on a small change — use /live or a specific refine command
  • Rush the /shape discovery interview — specificity is everything

Getting better results from every command

Add direction, not just a target

Instead of /impeccable craft hero, write: /impeccable craft hero for a B2B SaaS targeting senior engineers, dark theme, editorial aesthetic — not startup-generic. More direction = more intentional output.

Use negative direction

"No Inter, no purple gradients, no card-in-card layouts" is often more useful than describing what you do want. The anti-pattern list tells the AI exactly what common mistakes to avoid.

Ask for options before committing

On any major design decision: "Give me 3 different directions before building." One sentence descriptions, not code. You choose, then it builds. Much cheaper to change direction before code is written.

Give real references

"Make it feel like Linear's dashboard" is more useful than "make it minimal and professional". Real product references give a precise aesthetic target the AI can actually hit.

Review incrementally

Don't run 6 commands and then review. Run one, check Playwright screenshot + DevTools, run the next. Compound errors are much harder to undo than single ones.

Anti-patterns Impeccable detects

Anti-patternWhy it's badFix
Inter / Space Grotesk / Geist / Plus Jakarta SansUsed in 80%+ of AI output. Signals no design intent./typeset
Purple-to-blue gradientsThe #1 AI default colour. Instantly signals "AI generated"./colorize with actual brand direction
Cards inside cardsCreates visual noise and unclear hierarchy./distill or /layout
Gray text on coloured backgroundsFails contrast. Looks like a mistake./audit catches this
Bounce / elastic easingFelt fresh in 2015. Now signals AI-default motion./animate with explicit easing direction
Side-tab left borders as the only decorationOverused "category card" pattern./layout or /impeccable craft
Italic serif headlines in hero sectionsDominated AI marketing pages in 2025-26. Now a cliché./typeset
Everything the same shade of tealAI "safe" colour when no brand colour is specified./colorize with brand direction

Agency-specific ideas

Idea: Create a project template with PRODUCT.md and DESIGN.md pre-filled with your agency defaults — typography philosophy, colour approach, accessibility baseline, prohibited anti-patterns. Copy it into every new project and customise per client. Cuts /impeccable teach from 5 minutes to 30 seconds.
Idea: Keep a "reference bank" of 8-10 products you consider excellent design (admin UIs, SaaS dashboards, client-facing tools). Reference them by name in your prompts — "make the tables feel like Retool's", "navigation like Linear's". The AI has seen these and can match their aesthetic precision.
Pre-client-review habit: Run /critique before any client review meeting. Get the honest AI feedback before the client sees it. Fix the obvious problems so the client sees finished work, not a work-in-progress.
📚 REFERENCE
Prompt Library
Every ready-made prompt in one place. Copy, fill in the brackets, paste into Claude Code. All prompts use the correct tools for the job.

Session starters

Start a new project session
I'm starting work on [project name]. Before anything else: 1. Read PRODUCT.md and DESIGN.md — summarise what you know about this project 2. List the UI files you can see in the codebase 3. Confirm the Impeccable skill is loaded and ready Today's task: [what you're building or fixing] Start with /impeccable shape if we're building something new, or tell me what you find if we're fixing something existing.
Hand an existing codebase to Claude Code for the first time
I'm handing you a codebase I need you to understand before we do any work. Do the following — don't change anything: 1. Describe the folder structure and what each main folder contains 2. Identify the tech stack: framework, CSS approach, database, API type 3. List every UI page/view with: file path, what it shows, main user action 4. List every backend file type and folder (these are off-limits for UI work) 5. Note any existing design system: CSS variables, component library, style guide 6. Note the 3 most obvious UI problems you can see from reading the code After this inventory, we will use the Impeccable skill to improve the UI.

Impeccable — build

Build new page — ask questions first
/impeccable shape [describe the page in one sentence] Ask me the discovery questions. I will answer each one. After the interview, show me the design brief before writing any code. I want to approve the direction first. Once I approve: /impeccable craft using the agreed brief.
Build with explicit constraints
/impeccable craft [feature] Read PRODUCT.md and DESIGN.md first. Hard constraints: - Framework: [e.g. Next.js 14 with App Router] - CSS approach: [e.g. Tailwind CSS only / CSS modules / styled-components] - No new dependencies without asking me first - Must be responsive — mobile and desktop Design constraints: - Tone: [e.g. professional, minimal, not startup-flashy] - Avoid: Inter font, purple gradients, card-in-card layouts - Colour: [any brand colours to use or avoid] After building: - Playwright: screenshot at 1440px and 375px - Chrome DevTools: confirm no console errors
⚡ Standard — Server Detection (include at start of every browser test)
Before navigating anywhere:
1. Check if a dev server is running — try these ports in order:
   localhost:3000  localhost:3001  localhost:5173  localhost:4321  localhost:8000  localhost:8080
2. If a server responds → use that URL for all subsequent steps
3. If nothing responds → check package.json for "dev" or "start" script and run it.
   Wait until it says it's ready before proceeding.
4. If it fails to start, or there is no package.json → STOP.
   Tell me what happened. I will provide a live staging or production URL to use instead.
Document which URL you are using before doing anything else.
Give me 3 design directions before building
Before building [feature/page], I want to see 3 different aesthetic directions. For each direction, give me: 1. Name / personality (2 words) 2. Typography approach (what kind of fonts, what feeling) 3. Colour approach (what strategy, not specific hex values) 4. Layout character (dense/airy, structured/organic, symmetrical/asymmetric) 5. A real product reference with similar aesthetic 6. How well it fits our PRODUCT.md context (1-5 rating + reason) Don't write any code. I will choose a direction, then we build.

Impeccable — evaluate & fix

Full audit with fix plan
/impeccable audit [area] After the audit, give me: 1. Scores for all 5 dimensions (Accessibility, Performance, Theming, Responsive, Anti-patterns) 2. All P0 findings — what, where, why critical 3. All P1 findings — what, where, why important 4. A recommended fix sequence: which command to run first, second, third 5. Estimated effort for each fix Do not fix anything yet. I want to review the full plan first.
Critique with specific persona
/impeccable critique [area] After the standard critique, also evaluate through this specific persona: [Describe your target user in detail — e.g. "A 45-year-old operations manager who uses this admin system every day, is not technical, and is always in a hurry"] Specifically ask: through this persona's eyes, what is the biggest frustration with this page? What would make it immediately 10% better for them?

Combined workflow prompts

★ Complete new client project kickoff
I have a new client project. Let's set it up properly from the start. Step 1 — Understand the codebase: [Map all files as per the codebase inventory prompt above] Step 2 — Establish boundaries (if existing code): [Use the monorepo/separate repo safety prompt as relevant] Step 3 — Set up Impeccable: /impeccable teach [Answer all questions thoroughly — have the answers ready] Step 4 — Document the current state (if redesigning existing): [Use Playwright to screenshot current state of all pages] Step 5 — Run initial audit: /impeccable audit [Full 3-tool audit as per the Visual Audit page] Step 6 — Create fix plan: Based on all findings, create a prioritised plan: - What to fix immediately (P0s, console errors) - What to redesign (pages that need /craft) - Suggested sequence and estimated effort per item Present the plan before doing any work.
🧪 RECIPE 1 — ImpeccablePlaywrightChrome DevTools
Admin / Backend Pages Redesign
A complete workflow that takes your existing admin from its current state to a professional, consistent, design-system-driven UI — without losing a single feature or breaking any backend logic. The AI does the heavy lifting; you approve at every step.
Progress will not be saved in this browser. Mode
↑Try Guided mode for a focused, one-phase-at-a-time view with progress tracking. Toggle any time.

    Before you start: if it's been a while since you last ran Impeccable, update the skill first — npx impeccable skills update from your project root. critique scores (Phase 6) ├── CLARIFY-PASS.md ← copy-pass diff (Phase 7 Gate 2) ├── HARDEN-PASS.md ← harden recommendations report (Phase 7 Gate 3 — chat-based; you implement Part B) ├── HARDEN-RECS-*.md ← per-page harden recommendations (Phase 6 Step 4) ├── ADAPT-PASS.md ← responsive change log (Phase 7 Gate 5, if it ran) ├── ONBOARD-PASS.md ← onboard design recommendations (Phase 7 Gate 6 — chat-based; you implement Part B) ├── POLISH-PASS.md ← polish diff (Phase 7 Gate 7) ├── FINAL-AUDIT.md ← Phase 7 implementation re-score ├── FINAL-CRITIQUE.md ← Phase 7 design re-score ├── PERF-REPORT.md ← Phase 7 Gate 4 (if optimize ran) ├── EXTRACT-REPORT.md ← Phase 8 pattern consolidation log ├── RECIPE-PROGRESS.md ← your checklist ├── FINAL-REPORT.md ← completion report ├── baseline/ ← before screenshots └── briefs/ ← (recipe-supplementary; /impeccable shape natively writes a single brief.md at root, the recipe renames each invocation's output into this folder) ├── 00-shared-components-brief.md ├── dashboard-brief.md ├── users-brief.md └── [all your admin pages]-brief.md
    If the AI continues past a STOP without your input — immediately type: STOP — return to Phase [N] pause point and re-run that phase's prompt. Don't let it improvise past a checkpoint. Pause points are not optional.
    STOP vs continuous run: the recipe's prompt blocks include "STOP and wait for review" instructions at every checkpoint. STOP is the safer default — it gives you review gates between expensive operations. If you want continuous run instead (faster, but skips review), edit "STOP and wait for me to review" → "continue to the next step" in the prompt before pasting. This trades safety for speed; only do it for low-stakes phases or after you've run the recipe end-to-end once and trust your setup.
    Working with the AI — session strategy. Long sessions lose context and degrade silently. Between phases that change in character, this recipe inserts five session-handoff cards (Phase 3→4, 4→5, 5→6, 6→7, 7→8) — purple-bordered accordions interleaved with the work phases below. Each handoff card has a closing checklist, a copy-paste re-prime prompt, and a one-line explanation. Treat them as required steps, not advice. The 5→6 handoff repeats once per page redesigned.
    Session Strategy Reference (legacy summary — click to expand)
    For users who want the per-phase rhythm at a glance. The five handoff cards above supersede this list — but the rhythm here is the same:
    • Phases 0a–3: one continuous session is fine.
    • Phase 4: fresh session — re-prime via the 3→4 handoff card.
    • Phase 5: fresh session — re-prime via the 4→5 handoff card.
    • Phase 6: fresh session per page — re-prime via the 5→6 handoff card, repeated.
    • Phase 7: fresh session — re-prime via the 6→7 handoff card.
    • Phase 8: fresh session — re-prime via the 7→8 handoff card. (Post-redesign DESIGN.md is deliberately NOT re-read; Phase 8 regenerates it.)
    Generic re-prime template (only if a handoff card is missing or you need to improvise): "Read these files first, then continue from Phase [N]: [list of files]. Do not start any work until you have read them all and confirmed back to me what each one contains. If anything has been compacted or summarised in your context, read the original file again."
    Need to redo an earlier phase? — open if you discover a mistake mid-recipe
    Mistakes happen. The recipe doesn't break if you back up and re-run a phase — but file outputs cascade, so know which downstream files need re-running too:
    • PRODUCT.md is wrong (audience, voice, register, KEY_TASKS) → re-run /impeccable teach. Cascade: re-run Phase 4 /impeccable document (DESIGN.md depends on PRODUCT.md), then re-run Phase 5 briefs.
    • DESIGN.md is wrong (tokens, components, anti-references) → re-run Phase 4 /impeccable document. Cascade: re-run Phase 5 briefs.
    • One brief is wrong → just re-run shape for that one page, rename the new brief.md, edit the affected row in IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md if the slug changed.
    • One page's craft output is wrong → see the escape valves block in Phase 6 (covers craft-failed, brief-was-wrong, and park-this-page recovery).
    • Direction (Phase 3) needs adjustment → edit the ## Design Direction section of PRODUCT.md directly. Phase 4 reads PRODUCT.md natively, so it picks up the change. No earlier command to re-run.
    Rule: if you change PRODUCT.md or DESIGN.md after Phase 5 has already run, regenerate the affected briefs — don't try to "patch" them by hand. Briefs are cheap to regenerate; downstream code is not.

    How the three tools layer in this recipe

    Impeccable is the designer. Every /impeccable … step is where Claude plans, writes, or refines code. This is the backbone of the recipe.

    Playwright is the visual checker. It opens your running site in a browser, takes screenshots, and fills forms.

    Chrome DevTools is the browser inspector. It reads console errors, network requests, and accessibility info — facts the source code can't tell us alone.

    The recipe always uses Impeccable to make changes, and Playwright + Chrome DevTools to check changes. If you ever see a step asking the model to inspect or screenshot something without using one of these tools, type STOP — that step is wrong.

    Cost & time per phase (rough — varies with codebase size).
    • Phase 0a: 💰 (~10–20 min)
    • Phase 0: 💰💰 (~20–40 min — reads everything)
    • Phase 1: 💰 (~10–15 min)
    • Phase 2: 💰💰 (~20–40 min — screenshots + copy/a11y audit)
    • Phase 3: 💰 (~15 min)
    • Phase 4: 💰💰 (~30–45 min — dense design tokens)
    • Phase 5: 💰💰💰 (one plan per page; 10 pages ≈ 60–90 min, big token spend)
    • Phase 6: 💰💰💰💰 (actual implementation, 20–60 min per page)
    • Phase 7: 💰💰💰 (~45–60 min — lots of QA prompts)
    If you have a token / usage cap, plan to run Phase 5 and Phase 6 across multiple sessions.

    For sites you only edit through a CMS or hosting panel — no local code on your machine. The AI walks you through making a staging copy specific to your host so we never touch live until you sign off.
