RULE: If you don't know my host, ask me — do not invent. ════════════════════════════════════════ STEP 1 — IDENTIFY THE HOST ════════════════════════════════════════ Ask me which platform the site runs on. Give me click-by-click instructions for my specific host. - Wix: Duplicate the site from the Sites dashboard (Site Actions → Duplicate Site). - Squarespace: no native staging — ask me whether to (a) build in unpublished pages then swap, or (b) export the site for a developer site environment. - Webflow: use the built-in Staging environment. - Shopify: duplicate the theme — Online Store → Themes → Actions → Duplicate. Edit the duplicate. Never edit the live theme directly. - Duda / GoDaddy / others: ask me. Live URL 2. Staging URL 3. Exactly how I would roll the live site back if needed Then STOP. Wait for me to say "ready for Phase 0".
    Default rule for all branches — no edit reaches the live URL until Phase 7 sign-off. If the AI ever proposes a change that would touch the live site before then, type STOP.
    ✅ What to check before continuing:
    1. SAFETY-NET.md records WHICH backup path you chose (A / B / C) and the exact rollback command for it.
    2. Open the backup zip, browse the staging URL, or confirm the redesign-baseline tag exists — actively prove the safety net works before trusting it.
    3. Test-restore drill (required, not optional): in a throwaway directory, actually perform the rollback once and confirm the codebase is healthy after.
      Branch A or B-with-git, Mac/Linux: mkdir /tmp/rollback-test && cd /tmp/rollback-test && git clone [your-repo] . && git reset --hard redesign-baseline then run the dev server — confirm it boots.
      Branch A or B-with-git, Windows (PowerShell): New-Item -ItemType Directory -Path "$env:TEMP\rollback-test" -Force | Set-Location; git clone [your-repo] .; git reset --hard redesign-baseline then run the dev server — confirm it boots.
      Branch B without git: extract the backup zip into a fresh directory and run the project — confirm it boots.
      Branch C: revert the staging snapshot once and confirm the staging URL recovers.
      Record the restore time in SAFETY-NET.md so you know how long rollback takes under stress. STOP if the restore fails — fix the safety net before any redesign work begins.
    4. The rule "no edits to live until Phase 7 sign-off" is written in SAFETY-NET.md so neither of us forgets mid-recipe.
    Don't have a branching workflow? — open for simple defaults
    Not using git? Close this block. Your Phase 0a safety net already covers you.
    If you ARE using git but don't have a branching convention, here's the minimal one for this recipe:
    • Before Phase 6 starts, create one branch named redesign off your current main.
    • All Phase 6 work happens on this branch. Commit after each page (Phase 6 already enforces this).
    • When Phase 7 is fully done and signed off, merge redesign into main.
    That's it. No PRs needed unless your team requires them. No staging environments unless you have one. No naming patterns beyond redesign.

    0
    Codebase Reconnaissance~20–30 min  ·  RECON.md + RECIPE-PROGRESS.md
    ▶
    The model will map everything autonomously. You don't need to explain what's in the codebase — it figures that out. You only need to answer if it asks you a specific question.
    ★ Phase 0 — Run this first
    You are about to help redesign the admin/backend-facing pages of this codebase. This is Phase 0 — Reconnaissance. Do NOT change any files. This phase is understanding only. ════════════════════════════════════════ STEP 1 — MAP THE CODEBASE ════════════════════════════════════════ Explore every folder and file. Identify: - Folder structure and the purpose of each folder - Tech stack: frontend framework, CSS approach, backend language, database, auth method, API type - Entry point files (app.js, server.py, index.tsx, etc.) - Any config files that hint at the deployment setup ════════════════════════════════════════ STEP 2 — CLASSIFY EVERY PAGE / ROUTE ════════════════════════════════════════ Find every page, view, route, and template. Classify each as: - ADMIN: pages only staff / operators / managers see (behind auth, or clearly admin routes) - CUSTOMER: pages the public or end-users see - SHARED: layouts, partials, components used by both - API / SYSTEM: split this category. (a) Error pages and 404s — UI-bearing → classify as ADMIN-redesignable. (b) API routes (handlers, controllers, serializers) — NOT UI-bearing → add IMMEDIATELY to the untouchable list (Step 4 will list backend logic, but API routes belong there from the start; they should NEVER appear in the ADMIN page inventory). If a page is genuinely ambiguous, add it to your questions list. Otherwise decide. For every ADMIN page, document: | Field | Detail | - File path (exact) - URL or route pattern - Purpose (1 sentence: what does this page do?) - User role required (if role system exists) - Key UI elements: tables, forms, charts, stat cards, modals, filters, search - Forms: for EACH form found, list every input name attribute exactly ════════════════════════════════════════ STEP 3 — SHARED COMPONENTS + BLAST RADIUS ════════════════════════════════════════ Find every shared component used across admin pages: - Main navigation / sidebar - Header / topbar - Layout wrappers - Any reused card, table, or form components For each shared component: - Where is the file? - Which admin pages depend on it? This is the "blast radius" — changing it affects all dependent pages simultaneously. ════════════════════════════════════════ STEP 4 — MARK UNTOUCHABLE FILES ════════════════════════════════════════ Flag every file that contains backend logic. These are permanently off-limits. Include: routes, controllers, models, API handlers, auth files, DB schemas, migrations, server config. Tag each: [DO NOT TOUCH] — [file path] — [reason: e.g. "route handler"] ════════════════════════════════════════ STEP 5 — FEATURE PRESERVATION MANIFEST ════════════════════════════════════════ For every admin page, list every interactive element: - Forms: name attr of each input, action URL, method (GET/POST) - Buttons: text, what they trigger, any data-* attributes, form they belong to - Links: href, destination - JS hooks: every class or ID used in JavaScript selectors (these cannot be renamed) - API calls made from this page - Analytics events: every event fired (event name, trigger element, parameters). Common sources: Google Analytics gtag calls, Segment track() calls, Mixpanel mixpanel.track(), PostHog posthog.capture(), or custom analytics endpoints. The redesign must continue firing these events with the SAME names AND parameters — internal admin events (audit trails, click tracking, who-did-what dashboards) silently lose data if a redesign drops them. Nothing in this manifest can be silently removed during redesign. ════════════════════════════════════════ STEP 6 — CHECK LIVE SITE ════════════════════════════════════════ Note: if the admin area is behind a login page or VPN, I will provide credentials or access instructions before you begin. SERVER DETECTION — run first, before any navigation: 1. First check /redesign/SAFETY-NET.md. If it says "Safety Net Chosen: C — staging on [host]", use the staging URL recorded there. Skip the rest of server detection. 2. If Branches A or B were chosen (local code on disk), check if a dev server is already running. Try in order: localhost:3000 localhost:3001 localhost:5173 localhost:4321 localhost:8000 localhost:8080 3. If a port responds → use that URL for all subsequent steps. 4. If nothing responds → check package.json for a "dev" or "start" script and run it. Wait until the server confirms it is ready. 5. If it fails to start → STOP. Tell me what happened. I will provide a staging or production URL. State the URL you are using before doing anything else. Use Playwright MCP: 1. Navigate to the admin login or home page 2. Screenshot every admin page you can reach (ask me for credentials if needed) 3. Note: page title, URL, what's visible Use Chrome DevTools MCP on each page: 1. Console tab: list all existing errors and warnings 2. Network tab: list any existing failed requests (These are PRE-EXISTING issues — not caused by our redesign) ════════════════════════════════════════ STEP 7 — EXISTING DESIGN SYSTEM CHECK ════════════════════════════════════════ Look for: CSS variables files, any component library (Bootstrap, Tailwind, Chakra etc.), theme files, or brand guidelines inside the codebase. Note what exists and how consistently it is applied. ════════════════════════════════════════ STEP 8 — ACCESSIBILITY BASELINE ════════════════════════════════════════ For every admin page, count and document: - Images: total count / how many have an alt attribute / how many have descriptive (non-empty, non-filename) alt - Form inputs: total count / how many have a properly associated <label> (or aria-label / aria-labelledby) - Heading hierarchy: pages with no H1, multiple H1s, or skipped levels (e.g. H1 → H3 with no H2) - Obvious contrast issues: spot-check Chrome DevTools' contrast warnings on the main content of each page - Keyboard traps: tab through the page — does focus ever get stuck or skip an interactive element? This is the score we have to beat. Without this baseline, "Phase 7 accessibility improved" is just a claim. Save to /redesign/A11Y-BASELINE.md, one section per page. ════════════════════════════════════════ STEP 9 — IMAGE & ASSET INVENTORY ════════════════════════════════════════ For every image referenced by an admin page (img tags, CSS background-image, icon sprites): - Path - Current file size (KB) - Format (PNG / JPG / SVG / WebP / AVIF / other) - Has descriptive alt text? (yes / no / generic-only) - Issue (one of: too large >200KB / wrong format / missing alt / generic alt / low resolution / stock-looking / none) - Proposed action (auto-convert to WebP / compress / replace with new photo / add alt / leave alone) Save to /redesign/IMAGES-NEEDED.md as a table. Phase 6 will refer to this and add new entries as more images are encountered during implementation. ════════════════════════════════════════ OUTPUTS ════════════════════════════════════════ Create folder /redesign/ (if not exists). Save /redesign/RECON.md with these sections: 1. Tech Stack Summary 2. Admin Pages Inventory (table: page | file path | route | purpose | key elements) 3. Customer-Facing Pages (list — we will NOT touch these) 4. Shared Components & Blast Radius (table: component | file | dependent pages) 5. Untouchable Files (list with reasons) 6. Feature Preservation Manifest (per page: all forms, buttons, JS hooks) 7. Existing Design System (what was found and how consistent it is) 8. Pre-Existing Issues (console errors, failed requests from live check) 9. Questions For You (only truly ambiguous items — aim for zero) Save /redesign/A11Y-BASELINE.md (from Step 8 — accessibility scores per page). Save /redesign/IMAGES-NEEDED.md (from Step 9 — image inventory + actions). Save /redesign/RECIPE-PROGRESS.md: ## Admin Redesign — Progress - [x] Phase 0a — Safety Net & Backup: COMPLETE - [x] Phase 0 — Reconnaissance: COMPLETE - [ ] Phase 1 — Business Context - [ ] Phase 2 — Visual Baseline - [ ] Phase 3 — Design Direction - [ ] Phase 4 — Design System - [ ] Phase 5 — Page Plans - [ ] Phase 6 — Implementation - [ ] Phase 7 — QA After saving all files, summarise your key findings in 10 bullet points. Then STOP and wait for me to review RECON.md, A11Y-BASELINE.md, and IMAGES-NEEDED.md before we proceed.
    ✅ What to check in RECON.md, A11Y-BASELINE.md, and IMAGES-NEEDED.md:
    1. RECON.md's page count looks roughly right (within ±10% of what you remember). Pages aren't missing or invented.
    2. Pick 3 random files listed under "Untouchable Files" — open each in your project. If any doesn't exist, the AI hallucinated. Flag it.
    3. Omission check: pick 3 random files NOT flagged as untouchable. Open each. If any imports backend modules, hits a database, defines API routes, or contains business logic, the AI missed it — add it to the untouchable list before Phase 6 starts. Hallucination detection is one direction; omission detection is the inverse.
    4. A11Y-BASELINE.md and IMAGES-NEEDED.md mention pages and images you recognise — not generic placeholders.
    📅 Reality check before you commit to the recipe. Now that RECON.md is written, you know your page count. Total time depends on it:
    • Phase 5 briefs: ~5–10 min × your page count
    • Phase 6 builds: ~30–60 min × your page count
    • For 12 pages: ~7–14 hours of focused work + 💰💰💰 token band
    Decide now whether to commit. If your scope is 20+ pages or your token cap is tight, plan the recipe across 3+ multi-day sessions before starting Phase 1.
    Where to actually source the images IMAGES-NEEDED.md asks for — open if some entries need new photography
    If this recipe needs no new images — all existing images are already optimised and no new photography is required — close this block and continue. No action needed.
    IMAGES-NEEDED.md tells you what imagery is missing. Here's how to actually source it:
    • Stock (license-free): Unsplash, Pexels, Pixabay. Best for hero backgrounds, generic context shots. Match your brand voice — don't pick the first result. Always credit per the license.
    • Client-supplied: ask for the highest-resolution originals you can get. Phone photos are fine if recent and well-lit.
    • AI generation: Midjourney, DALL-E, Stable Diffusion. Good for stylised illustrations, abstract product shots, mood imagery. Risky for hero photography that implies "real people using real product".
    • Repurpose existing site assets: if the original site has good imagery, keep it. The redesign doesn't have to throw out working photography.
    • Placeholder + revisit: if a real photo is months away, ship with a tasteful gradient or illustration placeholder rather than a stock cliché. Tag the element with data-redesign-needs-photo so it shows up in Phase 7's pre-deploy search.
    Step 10a — Foundation Floor Check (the minimum below which nothing ships)
    Every site needs a stack-agnostic operational floor before redesign begins: seven universal items (HTTPS, auth, backups, error tracking, environment separation, security headers, transactional email) plus any legal and regulatory items derived from the jurisdiction and business type captured in PRODUCT.md in Phase 1. Output is /redesign/FOUNDATION-FLOOR.md — recipe-supplementary, lives in /redesign/. This is not /impeccable audit — that command runs a technical-quality audit in Phase 7 and is entirely separate from this operational floor check.
    Step 10a — produce FOUNDATION-FLOOR.md

    1
    Business Context~10 min  ·  PRODUCT.md
    ▶
    PRODUCT.md is the file every Impeccable command reads before doing anything. Without it, every command guesses about your business. We build PRODUCT.md in two passes: the skill writes the canonical fields, then we layer admin-specific context on top.

    Step 1 — Let Impeccable interview you

    What this command does — /impeccable teach runs a short interview about your product. It asks roughly five questions one at a time, then writes PRODUCT.md at your project root. This file captures who the admin is for, the brand voice, and aesthetics to avoid (called "anti-references" — these are what stop later commands producing generic AI output). Note for Claude Code users: the teach interview is naturally a brainstorming-character task — invoke Superpowers' brainstorming skill before starting if your harness supports it.
    /impeccable teach
    Before you fire the command — open the cheat sheet's "Setup & Pinning" page (left nav) and read the "Questions /impeccable teach will ask you — be ready" panel. Five minutes of prep prevents freezing mid-interview. What you'll see — Claude will scan your codebase to form an early hypothesis ("looks like a healthcare admin?"), then ask you ~5 questions one at a time. It will quote your answers back before writing anything. Answer plainly — if you don't know, say "I don't know" and Claude will note the gap rather than invent. What you'll get back — PRODUCT.md at your project root. Leave it there — the skill expects it at root; Step 2 will append admin-specific sections to it in place. Sections teach writes: Register, Users, Product Purpose, Brand Personality, Anti-references, Design Principles, Accessibility.
    One field you must set explicitly — when Claude asks about register, say "product". Admin work is product-register (design serves the task, fluent users of Linear / Notion / Stripe should trust it). The Impeccable docs reference register influencing typography / motion / color defaults across downstream commands but don't enumerate per-command branching, so the safest bet is the register that matches your work — for an admin redesign, "product" is the right pick.
    RECIPE-SPECIFIC DEVIATION: decline the auto-offer of /impeccable document — when teach finishes, the skill asks "want me to run /impeccable document now?" The Impeccable docs say the default expectation is that you accept ("Users can accept unless they have reason to delay"). This recipe asks you to decline because Phase 4 will overwrite the early DESIGN.md anyway, and running document twice creates churn. The decline is a recipe-specific override of skill default behavior, not how the skill is meant to be used out of the box. Say NO when prompted.

    Step 2 — Add the admin-specific fields teach doesn't ask about

    Teach captures register, users, voice, and anti-references. The recipe needs three more things teach doesn't ask: who specifically uses this admin, what design standard you're targeting, and what CSS framework the codebase is locked into. We append these to PRODUCT.md.
    FILL IN THE FIELDS BELOW FIRST — fill every placeholder in the card below before pasting the framing prompt. Blank placeholders pollute downstream PRODUCT.md and break later phases.

    [ADMIN_USERS]Role + headcounte.g. 2 office managers + 1 receptionist

    ✅ What to check in PRODUCT.md before continuing:
    1. The Register line says product (not brand) — required for admin recipe.
    2. The Anti-references section names specific aesthetics to avoid (no glassmorphism, no purple gradient defaults, no Inter / Plus Jakarta / Cormorant by reflex). This list is what stops the redesign producing AI-slop.
    3. Your admin-specific fields are appended in their own section — not mixed into teach's sections.
    4. Niche specificity is a precise description of the business — specific enough that Claude could describe your target customer in detail. Not just a category like "booking business."
    5. Jurisdiction names a country or region. If the industry has a regulatory body, it is named. "No specific regulatory body" is a valid answer — blank is not.
    6. Business scale picks exactly one of the five options (solo / small team ≤5 / mid 5–25 / multi-location / enterprise) and adds one line of context explaining the pick.
    7. Business model picks exactly one of the six options: B2C / B2B / D2C / marketplace / SaaS / hybrid.
    8. Payment model lists at least one option. If multiple apply, all are listed — e.g. "deposit+balance + subscription."

A reasonable customer of THIS business would be frustrated or confused without it 2. The owner couldn't perform an essential daily operational task without it 3. Its absence creates legal, regulatory, or data-protection risk Do NOT flag items just because businesses of this type often have them — that alone is not enough. ADMIN-LENS NOTE: this recipe focuses on internal staff and owner operations — booking management, scheduling, permissions, reporting, exports. Flag admin-essential items. Apply the same discipline filter. --- SECTION 2C: Tech-Stack Utilization --- What does the chosen stack enable that isn't wired up? What's partially configured? What's installed but broken or unused? TAGGING — for every item across all three sections, apply all four tags on one line: - Priority: BLOCKS-REDESIGN | BLOCKS-LAUNCH | NICE-TO-HAVE | DEFER - Zone: 1 | 2 | 3a | 3b | 3c Zone 1: in codebase, broken/incomplete, fixable within existing stack, no third-party config/secrets/billable usage, fully reversible Zone 2: adds something new but within existing stack and chosen tools Zone 3a: new tool requiring account/key/cost Zone 3b: significant architectural change — Architecture Decision Record (ADR) written using the Engineering plugin's /architecture skill format Zone 3c: out of scope / new product direction Tiebreaker: third-party config / secrets / env vars / billable usage → Zone 2 minimum - Status: NOT-STARTED | PARTIAL | BROKEN - Evidence: one-line observation from codebase or PRODUCT.md that justifies the flag TOP PRIORITIES — at the end of the file, emit a "Top Priorities" list of the 5–10 items most critical to address, in priority order.     Step 3 — Score the implementation quality

    What this command does — /impeccable audit scores your existing admin on five technical dimensions: accessibility, performance, theming, responsive design, anti-patterns. Each dimension scores 0–4. Issues are tagged P0 (blocks release) to P3 (polish). Audit produces a report — it makes no changes to your code. Argument grammar: the recipe uses natural-language scope arguments throughout (/impeccable audit admin for site-wide, /impeccable audit [page] for per-page). The Impeccable docs describe audit as accepting natural-language input, but exact argument grammar may vary by skill version. If a scope argument fails ("unknown scope"), retry with a file path or a single-word scope, and note the working form for use in the rest of the recipe.
    TYPE THIS COMMAND — type the green command below into Claude Code. Wait for it to finish before pasting the framing prompt.
    /impeccable audit admin
    THEN PASTE THIS PROMPT — paste the framing prompt below into Claude Code. It captures the output and applies the per-page actions.
    Step 3 — Run audit and save the baseline
    After audit produces its report: 1. Save the FULL output (every dimension score, every P0/P1/P2/P3 finding) to /redesign/BASELINE-AUDIT.md. 2. Verify the file contains all 5 dimension scores: Accessibility, Performance, Theming, Responsive, Anti-patterns. 3. Count P0 findings — these are blockers Phase 7 must clear. 4. Tell me the 5 dimension scores in plain English plus the P0 count, then STOP.

    Step 4 — Score the design quality

    What this command does — /impeccable critique is different from audit. Audit measures implementation quality (a11y, perf, anti-patterns). Critique measures design quality — visual hierarchy, cognitive load, persona testing, plus an "AI-slop verdict" (does this look machine-generated?). It scores Nielsen's 10 usability heuristics 0–4 each. Like audit, it produces a report — no changes.
    TYPE THIS COMMAND — type the green command below into Claude Code. Wait for it to finish before pasting the framing prompt.
    /impeccable critique admin
    THEN PASTE THIS PROMPT — paste the framing prompt below into Claude Code. It captures the output and applies the per-page actions.
    Step 4 — Run critique and save the baseline
    After critique produces its report: 1. Save the FULL output to /redesign/BASELINE-CRITIQUE.md. 2. Verify the file contains all 10 Nielsen heuristic scores (visibility / match / control / consistency / error prevention / recognition / flexibility / aesthetic / error recovery / help). 3. Verify the AI-slop verdict is present (pass / borderline / fail). 4. Tell me the 10 heuristic scores plus the AI-slop verdict in plain English, then STOP.
    Why both audit AND critique? A redesign that improves audit but not critique looks technically clean and still generic. A redesign that improves critique but not audit looks polished and ships with bugs. Neither alone is sufficient.
    Phase 2 wrap-up
    After Steps 1–4 are saved, summarise: - How many pages were screenshotted (desktop + mobile count) - Pre-existing console error count - The 5 audit dimension scores (Accessibility, Performance, Theming, Responsive, Anti-patterns) - The 10 Nielsen heuristic scores plus AI-slop verdict - Any pages that could not be reached and why Live preview handoff: confirm the dev server is still running. Tell me the EXACT URL. I will click through the existing admin once before we redesign anything. Update /redesign/RECIPE-PROGRESS.md — mark Phase 2 complete. Then STOP. We look at the baseline together before designing.
    ✅ What to check before continuing:
    1. Open 3 baseline screenshots and compare against the live admin — they should match what's actually rendered.
    2. BASELINE-AUDIT.md has 5 dimension scores. BASELINE-CRITIQUE.md has 10 Nielsen scores plus an AI-slop verdict. Both files exist and look honest.
    3. If the AI-slop verdict says "pass" — that's worth noticing. Your existing admin is already non-generic; the redesign needs to preserve whatever's working, not flatten it.
    ⏸
    STOP — review before continuing
    Two baseline files (audit + critique) plus the screenshots are all you need to prove "improved" later. Without them, "Phase 7 improved accessibility" is just a claim.

    4
    Design System Creation~30–40 min  ·  DESIGN.md
    ▶
    This is the most important output. Phase 5 briefs and Phase 6 builds all read DESIGN.md. We build it in two passes: /impeccable document writes the canonical six-section format, then we append admin-specific component patterns.

    Step 1 — Generate the canonical DESIGN.md

    What this command does — /impeccable document writes DESIGN.md in the canonical Google Stitch format with six fixed sections (Overview, Colors, Typography, Elevation, Components, Do's and Don'ts) plus a machine-readable DESIGN.json sidecar. This is the file every later Impeccable command reads. Do not invent a different format.
    TYPE THIS COMMAND — type the green command below into Claude Code. Wait for it to finish before pasting the framing prompt.
    /impeccable document --seed
    Plain-English gloss for the prompt below — the prompt uses some technical terms because /impeccable document needs them. You don't need to evaluate the math; just paste and trust the AI.
    • OKLCH = a modern way to write colour values that produces visually-balanced palettes (more reliable than HSL or hex).
    • Chroma 0.005–0.015 = a tiny amount of colour mixed into otherwise grey neutrals, so backgrounds and borders feel like they belong to your brand.
    • WCAG ratio (4.5:1, 3:1) = the contrast required for text to be readable. The AI prints these next to every colour pair so you can verify accessibility at a glance.
    • Modular scale (ratio ≥ 1.25) = font sizes that step up by a consistent ratio (so headings feel related, not random).
    What to check after — the "What to check in DESIGN.md" list at the bottom of this phase covers the things you CAN evaluate without understanding the math: every colour pair has a ratio printed next to it, fonts aren't on the anti-references list, and every component listed actually exists in your RECON.md.
    THEN PASTE THIS PROMPT — paste the framing prompt below into Claude Code. It captures the output and applies the per-page actions.
    Phase 4 Step 1 — Generate the design system
    Read first: /redesign/RECON.md, PRODUCT.md (including the new ## Design Direction section). IMPORTANT — file location: /impeccable document writes DESIGN.md AND DESIGN.json to PROJECT ROOT. That's where they MUST stay — the skill expects them at project root. DESIGN.json sits at root as a sidecar to DESIGN.md, used by Live Mode's design panel. DO NOT move these files into /redesign/. Only recipe-supplementary files (RECON.md, BASELINE-*, RECIPE-PROGRESS.md, briefs/, etc.) live inside /redesign/. Generate a NEW DESIGN.md based on the direction in PRODUCT.md. This is greenfield — do not extract from current code. Write the six canonical sections: 1. Overview — design principles + atmosphere (3-5 sentences specific to THIS admin and these users — no generic platitudes) 2. Colors — using OKLCH colour space (not HSL or hex by default) - Tinted neutrals: chroma 0.005–0.015 toward the chosen brand hue - Roles: surface-page, surface-card, surface-input, surface-hover, surface-selected - Borders: subtle, default, strong, focus - Text: primary, secondary, muted, inverse - Actions: primary, primary-hover, secondary, destructive - Status: success/warning/error/info (bg + text per status) - Every pair must include the WCAG ratio next to it (AA minimum 4.5:1 for body, 3:1 for large text) 3. Typography — fonts that respect PRODUCT.md anti-references (NEVER pick Inter / Plus Jakarta / Cormorant / Fraunces / Space Grotesk / Instrument by reflex — those are AI defaults). Specify font-display, font-body, font-mono. 5-step modular scale, ratio ≥ 1.25. 4. Elevation — surface scale, shadow rules. For an admin (product-register), shadows should be flat by default; depth via surface lightness only. 5. Components — ONLY components that exist in /redesign/RECON.md. Do not invent. For each: default / hover / focus / active / disabled / loading / error states. Keyboard focus ring is mandatory. 6. Do's and Don'ts — derived from PRODUCT.md anti-references. Be specific. Validation: - Every colour pair has a WCAG ratio printed next to it - Every component listed exists in RECON.md - Density (compact / comfortable / spacious) matches USAGE_INTENSITY in PRODUCT.md - Fonts are NOT in the anti-references list Tell me the system summary in plain English.     Phase 4 Step 2 — Append admin patterns
    Read DESIGN.md and /redesign/RECON.md. CAVEAT — recipe-supplementary section: per the Impeccable docs, DESIGN.md has six fixed canonical sections in fixed order (Overview, Colors, Typography, Elevation, Components, Do's and Don'ts). The "## Admin-Specific Patterns" section appended below is recipe-supplementary, not skill-native. /impeccable document may not preserve it on regenerate (Phase 8 has an explicit preserve-and-reappend ritual for exactly this reason). Append this section at the BOTTOM of DESIGN.md: ## Admin-Specific Patterns (Specify ONLY patterns that exist in RECON.md. Use existing tokens from the six canonical sections — do not introduce new tokens.) ### Data Table Density: [compact 32px / comfortable 44px / spacious 56px — pick based on USAGE_INTENSITY] Header: surface-card bg, text-secondary, scale-sm, font-weight bold Row: surface-page, border-bottom border-subtle Row hover: surface-hover Row selected: surface-selected (if checkboxes exist) Cell padding: [px from spacing scale] Sort indicator: [chevron / arrow] Empty state: centred icon + heading + body + CTA ### Table Actions Recommendation: [inline buttons / hover-revealed actions / three-dot menu] ### Bulk Actions [If RECON.md found checkboxes — bulk action bar appearance and position] ### Search and Filter [Standard treatment based on what exists in RECON.md] ### Status Badge Shape: [pill / rounded-rect] Per status: bg + text from existing token pairs ### Pagination [If found in RECON.md — style and position] ### Print Considerations [For pages likely to be printed — hide nav, expand content, ensure black-and-white legibility] (Note: the next two subsections — Motion Tokens and Status Communication — are design-system guidelines. /impeccable animate may produce specific durations within these ranges, e.g. 180ms / 220ms / 260ms per its docs. Values are tuneable as long as they fit the fast / normal / slow philosophy.) ### Motion Tokens duration-fast: 150ms (toast appear, dropdown, hover state) duration-normal: 250ms (modal open/close, tab switch) duration-slow: 400ms (page transition, drawer slide) easing: ease-out for entrances, ease-in for exits, ease-in-out for two-way reduced-motion: @media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) — disable all transitions ### Status Communication Success: toast top-right, auto-dismiss 4s, status-success colours Error: inline below the field for form errors; toast for system-level errors Warning: inline banner if action-blocking; toast if informational Loading: skeleton screens for data; spinner for action buttons Confirmation: modal for destructive actions; inline for non-destructive Do NOT modify the six canonical sections /impeccable document wrote. Append only. Update /redesign/RECIPE-PROGRESS.md — Phase 4 complete. Present the additions in plain English. Wait for confirmation before Phase 5.

    5
    Per-Page Briefs~45–60 min  ·  /redesign/briefs/*.md (one per page)
    ▶
    Each brief is a complete one-shot input for /impeccable craft. We generate them with /impeccable shape — the per-feature discovery interview command — then add draft copy with /impeccable clarify. You can edit any brief before Phase 6 runs. If a page comes out wrong in Phase 6, just re-run that one brief.
    Implementation options once briefs are ready: (A) continue in the same Claude Code session, feeding each brief back in. (B) copy each brief into a fresh session for cleaner context. (C) use different models for different briefs.
    Heads-up: shape runs twice per page. Per the Impeccable docs, /impeccable craft runs /impeccable shape internally as its first non-skippable phase. Phase 5 runs shape standalone to produce a reviewable brief; Phase 6 then runs craft, which runs shape again using your brief as discovery input. The token cost reflects this doubling. The upside is your brief is reviewed and editable before any code is written. If your token budget is tight and you don't need the per-page review gate, you can skip Phase 5 standalone shape and feed PRODUCT.md + RECON.md directly to Phase 6's craft — accepting that the brief is generated implicitly inside craft and not separately reviewable.

    Step 1 — Shape a brief for every admin page

    What this command does — /impeccable shape runs a 5–10 question discovery interview for ONE feature, then writes a brief. We run it once per admin page from RECON.md. Tell shape it's redesigning, not greenfield — so it asks about preservation, not from-zero direction. Convention: use the URL slug for [page-name] (e.g. dashboard, users, settings) — not the file path or display title. The same slug is used in the brief filename, IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md row, and per-page-scope file.
    How this step works. Phase 5 Step 1 produces one brief per admin page. Each page needs its own shape invocation — Impeccable's shape command is an interactive interview that runs once per feature, and Claude Code cannot loop it automatically. You drive the loop manually:

    1. Paste the first prompt below ("Generate per-page shape command list"). Claude Code reads RECON.md and emits a ready-to-paste list of /impeccable shape commands, one per admin page, ordered: shared components first, KEY_TASKS-heavy pages next, then the rest.

    2. For each command in that list: type the command into Claude Code, answer shape's discovery questions, wait for brief.md to be written at project root.

    3. After each shape interview completes, paste the second prompt below ("After each shape interview"). It instructs Claude Code to rename brief.md to /redesign/briefs/[page-slug]-brief.md and append the Recipe Context + Implementation Notes sections that downstream phases need.

    4. Repeat steps 2–3 for every command in the list. Move down the list top to bottom.

    5. After the final command in the list completes and Step 1b has run for the last page: paste the Step 1c prompt below. This runs once only — it produces the post-loop summary and updates RECIPE-PROGRESS.md.

    Phase 5 Step 1b — After each shape interview
    Read first: /redesign/RECON.md, PRODUCT.md, DESIGN.md, /redesign/BASELINE-CRITIQUE.md. Create the folder /redesign/briefs/. CRITICAL — handling shape's brief output: the Impeccable docs say shape produces a "brief" but don't explicitly specify the filename, location, or multi-run behavior (overwrite/append/version). Empirically, shape writes a single file called brief.md at project root, and the next shape invocation overwrites it. This recipe assumes that empirical behavior. To collect one brief per page, you MUST move/rename brief.md after every single shape call BEFORE running the next one. Recipe convention: rename to /redesign/briefs/[page-slug]-brief.md. The 00-shared-* prefix is a recipe convention to make shared-component briefs sort first — these are recipe-supplementary file names, not skill-native. The skill only ever sees brief.md at root. After shape writes brief.md, rename it to /redesign/briefs/[page-slug]-brief.md. Shape produces a brief whose format depends on the context it detects. It may produce a minimal 5-section brief (Purpose / User / Content / Feeling / Constraints) or a richer 10-section brief (Feature Summary / Primary User Action / Design Direction / Scope / Layout Strategy / Key States / Interaction Model / Content Requirements / Recommended References / Open Questions). Both are valid shape output — do NOT rewrite or reformat whichever one shape produced. Per the docs it's "a compass, not a spec. It captures intent, not UI." After shape's interview completes, append TWO recipe-supplementary sections at the END of the brief file: first `## Recipe Context`, then `## Implementation Notes`. Both are always required — shape never writes either of these. For `## Recipe Context`: always generate in full from RECON.md — no extra interview needed. For `## Implementation Notes`: check what shape already wrote. If shape produced the 10-section format and already has Key States and Layout Strategy sections, Implementation Notes only needs: (1) a one-line pointer — "See ## Key States above for per-state intent and ## Layout Strategy above for per-viewport intent" — and (2) the Verification steps line. If shape produced the minimal 5-section format, write the full Implementation Notes as specified below. Generate Recipe Context from RECON.md (you've already read it) — no extra interview needed. The brief file structure should end up looking like: # Brief: [page-name] ## Purpose (shape native) ## User (shape native) ## Content (shape native) ## Feeling (shape native) ## Constraints (shape native) ## Recipe Context (recipe-supplementary — append after shape finishes) - Files to edit (from RECON.md) - Files to NEVER touch (from RECON.md untouchable list) - Feature Preservation Manifest (forms, buttons, JS hooks, API calls — from RECON.md) - Information hierarchy (top priority → bottom) - Design direction (which DESIGN.md components, which tokens) ## Implementation Notes (recipe-supplementary — append after Recipe Context) Per-state intent (for Phase 6 Step 4 harden): - Empty: Heading / Body / CTA / Visual treatment - Loading: skeleton vs spinner / behaviour - Error: Heading / Body / Action (retry / go back / contact) - Permission denied (if role-based): what to show Per-viewport intent (for Phase 6 Step 1 craft + Step 3 adapt): - Mobile (375px): Navigation collapse / Tables (scroll vs reformat vs hide-columns — name the columns) / Forms stacking / Action button placement (sticky bottom bar?) / Content reflow - Tablet (768px): only if different from mobile/desktop derivation - Desktop (1440px): primary canvas spec Verification steps (for Phase 6 Step 6 verify): Playwright + DevTools + /impeccable audit + /impeccable critique Phase 6 craft will read both shape's 5 native sections AND the Recipe Context + Implementation Notes appendices. Don't be alarmed when shape's brief looks shorter than the original recipe template described — the long detail lives below shape's native output, not inside it. STOP.     Backend Layer 3 — Per-Page Gap Analysis + IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md backend section

    Layer 3 is the per-page backend gap check across every brief in /redesign/briefs/. The block has three sub-passes — Part A, Part A.5, Part B — that all run AFTER Phase 5's main Steps 1–3 are complete. Part A is a single batched pass across every brief (so the gap review doesn't disrupt shape's compass-not-spec rhythm during Step 1). Part A.5 reconciles FOUNDATION-FLOOR.md + BUSINESS-COMPLETENESS.md predictions against what the briefs actually need. Part B appends a backend section to IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md after Step 3 generates the per-page rows.
    Lightweight when empty. A page brief with zero backend gaps gets one line ("no backend gaps found for this page") and the model moves on. Overhead must be proportional to gaps found, not constant per page. Scope discipline: a gap qualifies only if (a) a specific element on this specific page directly requires it and won't work without it, OR (b) it would be embarrassing to launch a professional version of this business type without it. The model is not timid, but is precise — and asks before adding anything that feels out of scope.
    When to paste this prompt. Run ONCE after ALL Phase 5 briefs (Step 1 shape + Step 2 clarify) are complete and reviewed. Layer 3 is spec-level activity (BUILD/STUB/FAKE/DEFER decisions, plan files); injecting it per-page would conflict with shape's compass-not-spec rhythm. Batch across every brief in one focused session.
    Layer 3 Part A — Batched gap review (run ONCE after all briefs complete)
    WALK EVERY BRIEF IN ONE PASS: For each /redesign/briefs/*.md, walk every design element / state / interaction. LAYER 3 — DESIGN-DRIVEN GAPS FOR THIS PAGE Walk every design element / state / interaction in the brief. For each, ask: does the backend already support this exactly, or does it require a capability that does not yet exist? A gap qualifies for the list ONLY if: (a) a specific element on this specific page directly requires it and won't work without it, OR (b) it would be embarrassing to launch a professional version of this page without it. Do not list nice-to-haves. Do not list capabilities the user can confirm later. Be precise, not timid. If zero gaps for this page, write exactly one line in the brief: "Backend gaps: none for this page." and return — do not continue to the action review. If one or more gaps: 1. Number each gap (1, 2, 3...) with a one-line description of what's missing and which brief element requires it. 2. For EACH gap, propose ONE action — BUILD / FAKE / STUB / DEFER: - BUILD = the brief depends on real data or behavior that doesn't exist; we must build it. Will produce a plan file. - FAKE = the brief shows the feature but the page can ship before it works. Render the placeholder behind a feature flag (e.g. `if (FLAGS.feature_X_real) { ... real component ... } else { ... placeholder ... }`) so the swap-out at production is a flag flip, not search-and-replace through HTML. Add the flag to `/redesign/feature-flags.md` (recipe-supplementary) with one line: `feature_X_real: false # flip to true when [BUILD-feature-X.md] ships`. Mark the placeholder DOM with `data-backend-fake="[feature-slug]"`. Will be noted as a placeholder in the brief. - STUB = visible but inactive in UI, flagged as coming soon. Lower-priority plan file. - DEFER = not relevant enough now. Recorded in DEFERRED-COMPLETENESS.md, no plan file written. 3.     6
    Implementation~varies by codebase size  ·  Redesigned admin pages
    ▶
    Phase 6 is an executing-plans task per page. Invoke Superpowers' executing-plans skill at the start of each per-page session.
    How autonomy works during craft — Zones 1, 2, 3
    BUSINESS-COMPLETENESS.md tagged each gap with a zone in Phase 1. Phase 6 craft applies them.
    Zone 1 means craft fixes it inline without asking — for things already in the codebase, broken or incomplete, fixable within the existing stack, with no third-party config or secrets touched. Zone 1 is processed via Ralph loop:
    Ralph — Zone 1 batch processor
    /ralph-loop "Process Zone 1 fixes from BUSINESS-COMPLETENESS.md one at a time. Find the next item with status NOT-STARTED or PARTIAL and Zone tag 1. Apply the fix within the existing tech stack. Log a one-line entry to /redesign/AUTONOMOUS-LOG.md (filename, what changed, why). Mark the item HANDLED in BUSINESS-COMPLETENESS.md. If no Zone 1 items remain, output <promise>ZONE-1-BATCH-COMPLETE</promise>." --max-iterations 30 --completion-promise "ZONE-1-BATCH-COMPLETE"
    Each Zone 1 fix gets a one-line entry in /redesign/AUTONOMOUS-LOG.md as it happens, AND a consolidated summary surfaces at every existing recipe STOP gate. No new STOP gates added.
    Zone 2 means craft writes a one-paragraph plan, asks yes/no, proceeds on yes. Tiebreaker: anything touching third-party config, secrets, environment variables, or billable usage drops into Zone 2 even if it looks like a fix.
    Zone 3 (3a / 3b / 3c) — craft fully stops and waits. These should already have plan files in /redesign/backend-plans/ from Phase 5: BUILD-*.md / STUB-*.md / ADR-NNN-*.md / PROPOSAL-*.md.
    Per-page polish loop
    After /impeccable craft produces the initial page implementation, polish-to-brief is a Ralph candidate. Run:
    Ralph — per-page polish loop
    /ralph-loop "Read the brief at /redesign/briefs/[page-slug]-brief.md. Compare the current implementation to the brief's requirements (5 native sections + Recipe Context + Implementation Notes). Apply one focused improvement. Re-check against the brief. If all brief requirements are met, output <promise>PAGE-POLISH-COMPLETE</promise>." --max-iterations 8 --completion-promise "PAGE-POLISH-COMPLETE"
    Use only after craft has produced a working initial version. Don't use Ralph during initial craft itself — that's not the right shape.
    NEVER implement more than one page between checks. After each page: review screenshots, confirm no console errors, confirm forms still work. Only then proceed to the next page.
    Git strategy (commit after each page) means you can always revert one page without losing the others. Pair with a fresh Claude session per page (see the session-strategy callout above this accordion).
    Pick your responsive priority:
    M — Mobile-first
    D — Desktop-first
    B — Balanced (default)
    Pick this if mobile traffic dominates this admin (field staff using phones), or mobile is the harder layout you want to solve first. Less common for admin.
    Pick this for admin dashboards, data-dense tools, anything used at a desk for hours. Most admin systems should pick this — staff are at desktops, mobile is a fallback.
    Default if unsure. Build mobile and desktop in parallel — both are first-class. Tablet is a derived intermediate.

    The per-page loop

    For every page (in implementation order: shared components first, then KEY_TASKS pages, then the rest), run the seven-step loop below. Five Impeccable commands run per page: craft → iterate → harden → clarify → audit → critique. Verification with Playwright and Chrome DevTools sits between Impeccable commands as the secondary check, not the primary work.

    🚀 Ready to start? Open /redesign/IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md and find the next row that's still [ ] (or the one labelled "Currently on"). Copy that row's pre-filled re-prime prompt into a fresh Claude Code session and paste. Reply primed — go after the session confirms it's read everything, then follow the seven-step loop below.
    Don't have an IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md? Go back to Phase 5 Step 3 — Phase 6 expects it as input.
    ⚠ Things going wrong? — escape valves (open if a page is stuck)
    Phase 6's per-page loop assumes the happy path. When it isn't, pick the failure that matches and use the matching prompt.
    Failure 1 — craft produced something wrong (visual disaster, broke the layout, ignored the brief):
    Recovery — discard and re-run craft with corrections
    The page craft just produced is wrong. Discard the changes (Branch A or B-with-git: git checkout -- [page-files]; Branch B without git: restore from the per-page progress zip; Branch C: revert the staging snapshot for this page). Wait for me to approve the new craft output before moving on.
    Failure 2 — the brief turns out wrong mid-build (build is going OK but you realise the brief asked for the wrong thing):
    Recovery — edit the brief, re-run craft against the updated brief
    The brief at /redesign/briefs/[page-slug]-brief.md is wrong about [specific section — Purpose / States / Copy / etc.]. STEP 1 — STOP the current craft if it's still running. STEP 2 — Edit /redesign/briefs/[page-slug]-brief.md directly to fix the wrong section. Save the file. STEP 3 — Discard the in-progress build (same revert path as Failure 1). STEP 4 — Re-run /impeccable craft redesign of admin page [page-name]. When craft's internal shape phase asks discovery questions, point it at the UPDATED brief — quote each section back to me as confirmation. STEP 5 — Tell me which brief section was wrong and how the new build differs before approval.
    Failure 3 — this page just won't come together (multiple craft attempts failed, the shape is wrong, the page is more complex than the brief assumed):
    Recovery — park this page, continue with the next one
    This page is parking. Mark its row in /redesign/IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md from [ ] to [~] and add a one-line reason in the file footer ("parked: [page-slug] — reason: [specific issue]"). Discard any in-progress build for this page (same revert path as Failure 1). When you return to a parked page, start fresh — re-read the brief, identify what went wrong last time, and use the escape valve prompts from failure mode 1 or 2 as your entry point. Then move to the next undone row in IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md (skip the parked row). Tell me which page you're moving to, then await "primed — go" against its re-prime prompt.

    Step 1 — Build the page

    What this command does — /impeccable craft is the primary build command. Per the Impeccable docs, craft accepts a feature description as a natural-language argument and runs /impeccable shape internally as a non-skippable discovery phase, then loads references, then builds. The recipe pre-prepared a brief in Phase 5; we feed that brief into shape's discovery questions instead of re-interviewing.
    How this step works. Phase 6 Step 1 builds one admin page at a time. The page list lives in /redesign/IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md (produced in Phase 5 Step 3) — open that file and pick the next row marked [ ]. Then drive the per-page build manually:

    1. Fill in the two fields in the placeholder card below ([page-name] and [RESPONSIVE_STRATEGY]) — these get substituted into the prompt before you paste.

    Step 2 — Iterate (only if it's "almost right but not quite")

    Look at the page in the browser. If something feels off, run a single named-axis refine command instead of describing the problem in prose. Each axis has one command. If a command isn't recognised by your skill version, drop back to /impeccable craft with a refinement prompt — the named-axis commands are listed in the Impeccable docs but specific availability may vary by skill version.
    If the page looks…Run
    Generic, like every SaaS/impeccable bolder [page]
    Too loud, too many colours/impeccable quieter [page]
    Grey, lifeless, no identity/impeccable colorize [page]
    Fonts feel default or inconsistent/impeccable typeset [page]
    Spacing is off, things feel cramped/impeccable layout [page]
    Static, jumpy, no motion/impeccable animate [page]
    Functional but cold/impeccable delight [page]
    Too much on the page/impeccable distill [page]
    If you can SEE that something is wrong but cannot put it into words — run /impeccable live. It opens a browser tool: click the element you want to change, get 3 hot-swapped variants, accept the one that feels right. The accepted variant is written back to source automatically. This is the right command when you're not a designer and can't name the issue.

    Note — Live Mode reads DESIGN.md and PRODUCT.md from your project root for on-brand variants. DESIGN.json (the sidecar file /impeccable document generated alongside DESIGN.md in Phase 4) sits at project root and powers Live Mode's design panel. Keep both at project root.
    /impeccable live
    One iteration rule — never chain refine commands without looking in between. Run one, look, decide whether another is needed. Chained refines undo each other.

    Step 3 — Adapt for responsive (run before harden)

    What this command does — /impeccable adapt adds breakpoint-specific rules for tablet (768px) and mobile (375px). Admin panels are primarily desktop tools but staff check dashboards on tablets and mobile frequently. Run this before harden so that harden catches any responsive edge cases adapt introduces. Adapt does NOT touch desktop layout — it only adds responsive rules.
    TYPE THIS COMMAND — type the green command below into Claude Code. Wait for it to finish before pasting the framing prompt.
    /impeccable adapt [page] for mobile and tablet
    THEN PASTE THIS PROMPT — paste the framing prompt below into Claude Code. It captures the change log and verifies responsive coverage before harden runs.
    Step 3 — Verify adapt output before harden
    After adapt finishes: 1. Screenshot at 768x1024 (tablet) — confirm navigation, sidebars, and data tables render without horizontal scroll or clipping. 2. Screenshot at 375x812 (mobile) — confirm core admin tasks are accessible (viewing data, submitting forms). Full feature parity is not required at 375px — functional parity is. 3. Confirm no horizontal scroll at either breakpoint. 4. Confirm form inputs are tappable without zoom on mobile (min 44px touch targets). 5. Save change log screenshots to /redesign/baseline/[page]-adapt-after.png for both breakpoints. If anything is broken at tablet or mobile, re-run adapt with a specific constraint before continuing. Tell me which breakpoints changed before Step 4 (harden).

    Step 4 — Harden the states

    What this command does — /impeccable harden analyses the page against real-world data scenarios (long names, billions-scale numbers, RTL text, network failures, empty lists, overflow). The Impeccable docs don't explicitly state whether harden writes code, returns analysis only, or does both — treat any code harden writes as draft. So this step has two parts: (1) review what harden returned (recommendations + any code changes), then (2) verify and extend — adding missing states from the brief that harden didn't cover.
    TYPE THIS COMMAND — type the green command below into Claude Code. Wait for it to finish before pasting the framing prompt.
    /impeccable harden [page]
    THEN PASTE THIS PROMPT — paste the framing prompt below into Claude Code. It captures the output and applies the per-page actions.
    Step 4 — Run harden, then implement its recommendations
    Read /redesign/briefs/[page-slug]-brief.md (specifically the "States" section, plus any "## Implementation Notes" appendix) and the page you just crafted in Step 1. Harden may return a chat report, code changes, or both. Save the FULL recommendations report (and a note listing any code changes harden made) to /redesign/HARDEN-RECS-[page-slug].md so we have a record. Implement what harden recommended: After the report is saved, write the actual state code into the page. Add empty / loading / error / permission-denied / overflow states per the report. Match DESIGN.md tokens. Cross-check against the brief's States list — if the brief asked for a state and the report missed it, add it anyway. Verification: - 60-character names render without breaking layout - Large numbers don't overflow - Empty lists show a useful empty state (not a blank screen) - Error responses display a clear next-action UI Tell me which states were added (and the file paths touched) before moving to Step 5.
    What you'll get back from harden alone — a recommendations report. What you'll get from this whole step — added empty-state UI, error UI, loading skeleton, and overflow handling for the page (because Part B implements the recommendations). Verify by switching test data to extremes.

    Step 7 — Per-page audit + critique
    Save the per-page audit summary (5 dimension scores + P0/P1/P2/P3 findings) appending to /redesign/PER-PAGE-SCORES.md under a "## [page-name] — audit" heading. ROUTE FINDINGS: - P0 audit findings → STOP. Fix before this page is approved. Do not start Step 8 (handoff). - P1 audit findings → tag for Phase 7 gauntlet (do not fix per-page). - P2/P3 → list in PER-PAGE-SCORES.md, leave for next sprint. - Critique AI-slop verdict regressed vs baseline → STOP.     🔬 Functional smoke test before commit (per page) — visual approval is not functional approval. Before Step 7 commits this page, run through the brief's Feature Preservation Manifest manually:
    • ☐ Click every form, button, and link the brief's Feature Preservation Manifest names. Each one must do what it did before the redesign.
    • ☐ Submit one form successfully — open Network tab, verify the request hit the same endpoint as in the BASELINE notes.
    • ☐ Test keyboard navigation: Tab order is sensible, Enter/Space activates buttons, Esc closes any open modal.
    • ☐ Verify any JS hook the brief flagged (data attributes, event listeners, third-party callbacks) still fires.
    • ☐ Verify analytics events still fire — check the Network tab for the event calls listed in the Feature Preservation Manifest's Analytics events section. Internal admin events (audit trails, click tracking) silently lose data if a redesign drops them — do not skip this check.
    If any of these fail: do NOT commit. Open the escape valves block above; failure mode 1 (re-run craft with corrections) is usually the right path.

    Step 8 — Live preview handoff and commit

    Step 8 — Hand off and commit
    Confirm the dev server is still running. Tell me the EXACT URL of the redesigned page (e.g. http://localhost:3000/dashboard). Send me the screenshots and the audit + critique scores. Note any deviations from the brief. Wait while I open the URL on my own devices, click around, and approve. Do NOT start the next page until I say "approved". After approval — per-page rollback path depends on which Phase 0a branch you chose: DIFF REVIEW BEFORE COMMIT (required): Run `git diff --stat` and compare the changed-files list against /redesign/per-page-scope/[PAGE-SLUG]-scope.md (the scope contract written before craft ran). For any file changed that isn't in the scope's "Files to edit" list — STOP and review. Either revert the unscoped change or update the scope file with my approval. This is the scope-creep guard. Run `git diff` (full) on the most recent change and confirm nothing unexpected appears before staging. BRANCH A (git was set up): Read /redesign/per-page-scope/[PAGE-SLUG]-scope.md. For each file in the "Files to edit" section, run `git add [file]` explicitly. Do NOT use `git add .` or `git add -A`. git commit -m "redesign: [page-name]" After staging and before commit, run `git diff --cached` and confirm only files from the scope file are staged. If any unscoped file is staged, unstage it (`git reset HEAD [file]`) before committing. BRANCH B with git init (you said yes to git during Phase 0a): Same as Branch A — git add + commit per page. BRANCH B without git init (zip backup only): After each approved page, make an incremental zip: ../redesign-progress-[YYYYMMDD]-[page-name].zip Stash it next to the original pre-redesign-backup zip from Phase 0a. This is your per-page rollback point. BRANCH C (CMS / staging): After each approved page, save the staging snapshot per your host's flow: - WordPress (WP Engine / Kinsta / SiteGround): create a manual Backup Point named "redesign-[page-name]" - Webflow: publish to staging only — DON'T publish to production yet - Shopify: the duplicate theme stays the rollback target; do not Publish until Phase 7 sign-off - Wix / Squarespace: no native versioning — keep the duplicated/unpublished page set as your rollback target Log each snapshot's identifier in /redesign/RECIPE-PROGRESS.md so you can name it for rollback later. Then move to the next brief. Use /redesign/RECIPE-PROGRESS.md to track which pages are done. Rollback options: - Brief is intact — re-run /impeccable craft with it in a fresh session - baseline screenshots in /redesign/baseline/ - DESIGN.md is unchanged — your design direction is not lost regardless of what happens to a single page - Branch A or B-with-git: git revert HEAD (single page) or git reset --hard redesign-baseline (all) - Branch B without git: extract the relevant per-page progress zip - Branch C: restore the named Backup Point / republish the duplicate theme / revert to the last unpublished snapshot per host
    ✅ What to check (PER PAGE, before approving):
    1. Open the live URL the AI shared — does the page work end-to-end (forms submit, buttons go where they should)?
    2. Both viewports look right — check on actual devices or via browser DevTools.
    3. Audit returned no P0s. Critique didn't regress on AI-slop verdict vs baseline.
    4. Console errors: zero new errors vs BASELINE-ISSUES.md.
    ⏸
    STOP — approve each page before the next
    Auto-approving every page defeats the recipe. The pause point per page is what catches drift early.
    6→7
    Session Handoff — Phase 6 to Phase 7~5 min  ·  REQUIRED — close prior session, paste re-prime in fresh one
    ▶
    Required step. Skip ONLY if your current session is under ~30 minutes old AND you've sent fewer than ~10 messages since the last Phase 6 page approval.
    Why a fresh session here — Phase 7 evaluates the WHOLE redesign equally. A fresh session resets the AI from "thinking about the last page I just built" to "judging all pages alike against the baseline."

    7
    Pre-Ship Gauntlet~30–45 min  ·  FINAL-AUDIT.md + FINAL-CRITIQUE.md + FINAL-REPORT.md
    ▶
    Phase 7 runs the named "pre-ship gauntlet" from the Impeccable docs (audit → clarify → harden — three commands), followed by polish as a separate post-gauntlet finishing step. The gauntlet plus polish run every project, every time. Three more commands (optimize, adapt, onboard) fire conditionally based on what audit flags, and slot in between the gauntlet and polish.
    Gate-ordering tradeoff: optimize runs BEFORE polish so polish corrects any post-optimize layout shifts (e.g. lazy-loaded images, removed render-blocking JS). Reversing the order would have polish do CLS work that optimize then undoes. The current order is deliberate; the cost is that polish must survive optimize's structural changes.

    Gate 0 — Production Readiness Re-check

    Gate 0 — Production Readiness Re-check
    Open /redesign/BUSINESS-COMPLETENESS.md and /redesign/FOUNDATION-FLOOR.md. Surface the item, explain the gap, and wait for resolution. Use Superpowers' verification-before-completion skill to validate every BLOCKS-REDESIGN item's HANDLED status before continuing. LAYER 1 RUNTIME VERIFICATION For each of the following FOUNDATION-FLOOR.md items, produce a brief real-time verification — these weren't verifiable in Phase 0 before the redesigned site was running: - Monitoring firing: trigger a test error and confirm it appears in the error tracking service. - Error tracking firing: confirm Sentry / Rollbar / equivalent received the test event. - Backup tested with successful restore: confirm the most recent backup has been test-restored to a throwaway environment within the past 24 hours. - Transactional email actually sending: trigger a password reset or test notification and confirm delivery. For every PASS: one-line evidence. For every FAIL: note what's missing. STOP for review before proceeding to Gate 1.

    Gate 1 — Audit (the canonical first step)

    What this command does — re-scores implementation quality across all redesigned admin pages. Output drives every other gate in this phase.

    Gate 2 — Clarify (rewrite copy for the actual audience)

    What this command does — pre-ship copy pass across every admin page. Targets the six surfaces (labels, buttons, errors, empty states, tooltips, confirmations).
    How this gate works. Gate 2 re-clarifies copy ONLY on pages Gate 1's audit flagged for copy issues (or where Phase 7 critique scored low on Heuristic 6). It does NOT re-clarify site-wide — Phase 6 Step 5 already approved per-page copy and a site-wide re-clarify would regress that voice silently. You drive the per-flagged-page loop manually:

    1. Paste the first prompt below ("Generate flagged-page clarify command list"). Claude Code reads /redesign/FINAL-AUDIT.md (from Gate 1) and emits a ready-to-paste list of /impeccable clarify [slug] commands, one per page flagged for copy issues.

    2. For each command in that list: type it into Claude Code. Clarify proposes copy changes for that one page only.

    3. After each command runs, paste the framing prompt below to apply per-page diff-review, save the diff to CLARIFY-PASS.md, and approve/reject before applying. Move to the next command.

    4. When every flagged page is handled, declare Gate 2 done and proceed to Gate 3. If no pages were flagged, skip the gate entirely and note that in CLARIFY-PASS.md.
    Gate 2a — Generate flagged-page clarify command list
    Read /redesign/FINAL-AUDIT.md (produced by Phase 7 Gate 1) and /redesign/BASELINE-CRITIQUE.md (Phase 2). Identify every page flagged for copy issues by Gate 1's audit, AND every page where critique scored low on Heuristic 6 (Recognition rather than recall). De-duplicate by page slug. For each flagged page, emit a single line in this exact format: /impeccable clarify [slug] Where [slug] is the URL slug for that page (lowercase, hyphens for spaces, no file path). Output the list as a single code block, one command per line, no commentary between commands. After the code block, briefly note (1-2 sentences) how many pages were flagged and which audit categories triggered the flags. If zero pages are flagged for copy issues, output exactly: "No pages flagged — skip Gate 2 and note in CLARIFY-PASS.md." Then STOP. Otherwise STOP. Wait for the user to confirm the list before they paste the framing prompt and start running commands.
    TYPE EACH COMMAND — pick the next command from the list above and type it directly into Claude Code (the green cmd-block below shows the canonical [page]-placeholder form; the list above already has slugs filled in). Wait for clarify to propose its diff before pasting the framing prompt.
    /impeccable clarify [page]
    THEN PASTE THIS PROMPT — once clarify has proposed its diff for one flagged page, paste the framing prompt below into Claude Code. It tells the AI to save the diff to CLARIFY-PASS.md, surface it to you for approval, and apply only on approval. Repeat for each command in the list above.
    Gate 2b — Per-flagged-page framing
    Scope: every page Gate 1's audit flagged for copy issues, OR where critique scored low on Heuristic 6 (Recognition rather than recall). The Gate 2a list above has the per-page commands ready to type. After clarify finishes per page: 1. Save the proposed diff to /redesign/CLARIFY-PASS.md (which page, which surface, before → after). 2. Surface every diff to me before applying — do NOT auto-apply. I review and approve or reject each per-page diff. 3. Spot-check approved changes — voice should match PRODUCT.md Brand Personality, errors should never blame the user, no "Submit" buttons should remain. 4. If clarify proposed generic copy on any page, push back with specific instructions. 5. Tell me how many copy changes were applied before Gate 3. If no pages were flagged for copy issues, skip this gate entirely — say so in CLARIFY-PASS.md.
    Voice source — PRODUCT.md (Brand Personality). Clarify will keep voice consistent across pages.

    Gate 3 — Harden (stress-test reality)

    What this command does — across every page, harden looks at edge-case data handling, error UI, empty UI, loading skeletons, overflow handling. The Impeccable docs don't explicitly state whether harden writes code, returns analysis only, or does both — treat any code harden writes as draft. This gate has two parts: (1) review what harden returned (recommendations + any code changes), then (2) verify and extend coverage per page. If the codebase is large, run per-page rather than globally.
    TYPE THIS COMMAND — type the green command below into Claude Code. Wait for it to finish before pasting the framing prompt.
    /impeccable harden admin
    THEN PASTE THIS PROMPT — paste the framing prompt below into Claude Code. It captures the output and applies the per-page actions.
    Gate 3 — Run harden, then implement its recommendations
    (If the codebase is large or harden times out globally, run per-page: /impeccable harden [page] for each admin page, gathering reports.) Harden may return chat recommendations, code changes, or both. Save the FULL recommendations report (and a note listing any code changes harden made) to /redesign/HARDEN-PASS.md, organised by page. Implement the fixes: Walk through HARDEN-PASS.md page-by-page and write the actual state code per recommendation. Match DESIGN.md tokens. Pay special attention to: - Empty / error / loading state gaps Gate 1 flagged — those gaps should now be closed - Edge-case data: 60-character names, empty lists, large numbers, server errors - Overflow handling on data tables (scroll, truncation, ellipsis) Verification (Playwright at the end of Part B): - Spot-check 3 pages with test data at extremes - Every state renders cleanly without layout breakage Tell me which state gaps closed before Gate 4.
    Harden Part B applies recommendations sequentially. Wrap in Ralph:
    Ralph — Harden Part B processor
    /ralph-loop "Read HARDEN-PASS.md. Apply the next harden recommendation that is not yet HANDLED. Mark it HANDLED. If no recommendations remain, output <promise>HARDEN-PART-B-COMPLETE</promise>." --max-iterations 15 --completion-promise "HARDEN-PART-B-COMPLETE"

    Gate 4 — Optimize (only if audit flagged performance)

    What this command does — diagnoses and fixes UI performance: LCP, INP, CLS, render efficiency, animation jank, asset weight, bundle size. Run only if Gate 1's Performance score < 3 OR LCP > 2.5s on any page.
    TYPE THIS COMMAND — type the green command below into Claude Code. Wait for it to finish before pasting the framing prompt.
    /impeccable optimize admin
    THEN PASTE THIS PROMPT — paste the framing prompt below into Claude Code. It captures the output and applies the per-page actions.
    Gate 4 — Run optimize and save metrics
    SKIP THIS GATE if Gate 1's Performance score was ≥ 3 AND no page LCP exceeded 2.5s. After optimize finishes: 1. Save before/after metrics to /redesign/PERF-REPORT.md (LCP, INP, CLS per page). 2. Verify every metric improved or held — no regressions. 3. List what optimize did (lazy-loaded images / removed bundle bloat / fixed render thrash / etc.). 4. Tell me the before/after deltas before Gate 5.

    Gate 5 — Adapt (only if audit flagged responsive)

    What this command does — fixes responsive issues across screens, devices, and contexts without removing features. Run only if Gate 1's Responsive score < 3 OR mobile QA found issues.
    TYPE THIS COMMAND — type the green command below into Claude Code. Wait for it to finish before pasting the framing prompt.
    /impeccable adapt admin for mobile and tablet
    THEN PASTE THIS PROMPT — paste the framing prompt below into Claude Code. It captures the output and applies the per-page actions.
    Gate 5 — Run adapt for mobile + tablet
    SKIP THIS GATE if Gate 1's Responsive score was ≥ 3 AND no mobile QA issues. After adapt finishes: 1. Save the change log to /redesign/ADAPT-PASS.md (which pages changed, what changed at each breakpoint). 2. Playwright at 375x812 and 768x1024 — screenshot every admin page, confirm no horizontal scroll, no clipped UI, primary actions reachable. 3. Confirm critical functionality is preserved on mobile (no features removed for "convenience"). 4. Tell me which pages changed before Gate 6. If any admin pages changed significantly during adapt, re-run Gate 3 harden on those specific pages before Gate 6 — adapt reveals responsive edge cases that harden needs to catch.

    Gate 6 — Onboard (only if first-run / empty-state weak)

    What this command does — onboard looks at first-run experience, empty states, and activation moments for new users. The Impeccable docs don't explicitly state whether onboard writes code, returns analysis only, or does both — treat any code onboard writes as draft. So this gate has two parts: (1) review what onboard returned (design recommendations + any code changes), then (2) verify and extend coverage. Run only if Gate 1 flagged weak empty states OR PRODUCT.md mentions onboarding new staff.
    TYPE THIS COMMAND — type the green command below into Claude Code. Wait for it to finish before pasting the framing prompt.
    /impeccable onboard admin for new staff members
    THEN PASTE THIS PROMPT — paste the framing prompt below into Claude Code. It captures the output and applies the per-page actions.
    Gate 6 — Run onboard, then implement its recommendations
    SKIP THIS GATE if Gate 1 didn't flag weak empty states AND PRODUCT.md doesn't mention new-staff onboarding. Onboard may return a chat-based design recommendations report, code changes, or both. Save the FULL recommendations (and a note listing any code changes onboard made) to /redesign/ONBOARD-PASS.md. Implement the design: Walk through ONBOARD-PASS.md page-by-page and write the actual code per recommendation. Match DESIGN.md tokens. For each page that got a recommendation: - Empty state has a useful next-action CTA (not just "No data") - First-run screens orient the user toward the top KEY_TASKS from PRODUCT.md - No tour overlay added unless PRODUCT.md explicitly requested one Verification (Playwright): - Force the empty state on 2 random pages → screenshot the empty UI → confirm CTA is useful - Tell me which pages changed and what was added before Gate 7.

    Gate 7 — Polish (final alignment / consistency / state coverage — ALWAYS LAST)

    What this command does — the meticulous final-detail pass. Polish refines what exists; it does not redesign. Catches alignment drift, spacing inconsistency, missing interaction states, copy-tone misalignment. Per the Impeccable docs, polish is the LAST step — it runs after every other command that might restructure code, so its alignment work isn't undone. Re-cycle rule for Gate 7 → Gate 8 critique: if Gate 8 critique flags STRUCTURAL issues (broken hierarchy, drift, anti-patterns), re-run the relevant earlier gate (typically harden or one of the conditional optimize/adapt/onboard gates) to fix the structure, THEN re-run polish. If Gate 8 critique flags only AESTHETIC issues (alignment, spacing, hover states), polish handles them in place — no re-cycle needed. Don't re-run polish more than once per page; if it's still wrong after one re-run, the underlying brief is the problem, not polish.
    How this gate works. Gate 7 runs polish — the meticulous final-detail pass that fixes alignment drift, spacing inconsistency, missing interaction states, and copy-tone misalignment. Polish is the LAST code-mutating gate in Phase 7; Gates 8 and 9 are verification only. You drive the gate in two steps:

    1. Type the green /impeccable polish admin command into Claude Code. Polish proposes alignment and consistency changes across every redesigned admin page.

    2. Paste the yellow framing prompt below. It tells the AI to save the polish diff to POLISH-PASS.md, spot-check focus rings and hover states on 3 pages, and confirm polish did not restructure layouts (if it did, that's a regression — revert and re-run with the "alignment and consistency only, no structural changes" constraint).

    3. If FINAL-AUDIT.md (from Gate 1) still has P0/P1 issues, use the Ralph loop further down this gate to iterate one fix at a time before declaring polish complete.
    TYPE THIS COMMAND FIRST — type the green command below into Claude Code. Polish runs across every admin page; expect the diff to be visual and small. Wait for polish to finish before pasting the framing prompt.
    /impeccable polish admin
    THEN PASTE THIS PROMPT — once polish has produced its diff, paste the framing prompt below. It captures the diff to POLISH-PASS.md, runs the spot-check, and verifies polish stayed in scope (alignment only, no structural changes).
    Gate 7 — Post-polish framing
    After polish finishes: 1. Save the polish diff summary to /redesign/POLISH-PASS.md (alignment fixes / spacing fixes / interaction-state additions / copy-tone tightening). 2. Spot-check 3 pages — focus rings visible on every interactive element, hover states feel intentional, spacing rhythm consistent. 3. Polish should NOT have restructured layouts or redesigned components. If it did, that's a regression — revert and re-run with constraint "alignment and consistency only, no structural changes". 4. This is the LAST command that mutates the admin code in Phase 7. Gates 8–9 are verification only. 5. Tell me what polish changed before Gate 8.
    Polish should ideally run on a clean audit. /redesign/FINAL-AUDIT.md was produced by Gate 1 at the start of the gauntlet; if any P0/P1 issues remain in it, use Ralph below to work through them one at a time before declaring Polish complete.
    Polish is iterative refinement against an audit. Wrap in Ralph:
    Ralph — Polish gate processor
    /ralph-loop "Read /redesign/FINAL-AUDIT.md (produced by Phase 7 Gate 1). Find the first P0 or P1 issue not yet marked HANDLED. Apply one focused fix to resolve it. Mark it HANDLED in FINAL-AUDIT.md. Re-read FINAL-AUDIT.md. If no P0 or P1 issues remain unhandled, output <promise>POLISH-COMPLETE</promise>." --max-iterations 10 --completion-promise "POLISH-COMPLETE"
