The AI-slop verdict must be "pass" — or at minimum, not regress. If any heuristic regressed, name the page and the issue. We fix before sign-off.

    Gate 9 — Cross-checks (Playwright + Chrome DevTools)

    Gate 9 — Cross-checks
    SERVER DETECTION — state the URL. Playwright: - Navigate every admin page using ONLY navigation links — confirm no dead links and the active state highlights correctly - Screenshot every page at 1440x900 and 375x812 — visual regression vs /redesign/baseline/ - Test the most-used 3 forms end-to-end (happy path, empty submission, invalid data) - Mobile navigation (hamburger / drawer) at 375px works - Cross-browser quick check: Chrome / Firefox / Edge / Safari (whichever you have) - REAL-DEVICE TESTING (required, not optional): - Real iOS Safari on a real iPhone (not just Mac WebKit — rendering differs). At least one model. - Real Android Chrome on a real device. At least one model. - Document the device models and OS versions tested in the final report. - ASSISTIVE-TECH TESTING (required): - Screen reader pass on the most-used 3 admin pages — VoiceOver on macOS/iOS or NVDA on Windows. Navigate the primary user action end-to-end and confirm it completes. - Keyboard-only test on every form: Tab through every field, Enter submits, Esc closes modals, no traps, focus order is logical. Admin staff heavily rely on keyboard navigation — failure is a P0. - NETWORK-FLAKY TESTING (required): - Chrome DevTools throttle to "Slow 4G", reload the most-used 3 admin pages, confirm content remains usable. - Submit one form, then disconnect Wi-Fi mid-flight — confirm the UI surfaces a recoverable error (specific message, retry path), not a silent failure. - For one critical API call, use DevTools to override response to a 502 — confirm the UI handles it gracefully. - Data tables and lists: for every table or list, verify real data loads / sorting works (if present) / pagination works (if present) / empty state appears correctly when no data / row hover and selected styling match DESIGN.md tokens Chrome DevTools: - Console errors: zero NEW vs BASELINE-ISSUES.md across every page - Network: same endpoints + payloads vs RECON.md - Print stylesheet check on any reportable page (invoices, reports, order confirmations) — content readable, no UI chrome bleeding Compare per-page accessibility stats against /redesign/A11Y-BASELINE.md: - Alt-text coverage ≥ baseline - Form-label coverage ≥ baseline - No new heading-hierarchy regressions - No new keyboard traps

    Completion — Final Report

    Phase 7 — Final report
    Create /redesign/FINAL-REPORT.md: # Admin Redesign — Final Report ## Audit scores (vs baseline) | Dimension | Before | After | [5 rows: Accessibility, Performance, Theming, Responsive, Anti-patterns] ## Critique scores (vs baseline) | Heuristic | Before | After | [10 Nielsen rows] AI-slop verdict: [pass / borderline / fail] ## Gauntlet commands run - Gauntlet (audit → clarify → harden): complete - Polish (post-gauntlet finishing step): complete - Conditional: [optimize / adapt / onboard if any] ## Pages redesigned [list] ## Features preserved [every item from Feature Preservation Manifest is intact] ## Known remaining issues [pre-existing backend issues from BASELINE-ISSUES.md, anything else] ## Deploy Readiness (must be filled before "yes deploy") The recipe doesn't deploy, but no production deploy should happen without these in place. Hand this checklist to whoever ships: 1. **Rollout strategy:** [canary % + ramp schedule, e.g. "10% for 1hr → 50% for 1hr → 100%". Default for admin: full rollout in off-hours window. State which.] 2. **Pre-defined revert triggers:** [specific metric thresholds + time windows that trigger automatic rollback, e.g. "rollback if error rate >2x baseline over 15 min" or "rollback if any P0 admin action fails in production". List two or three.] 3. **Monitoring confirmed:** [link to dashboard watching the redesigned pages, on-call rotation aware of the change, alerts wired to the right channel. Link or describe.] 4. **Backup verified-restorable:** [database backup taken in last hour, test-restore performed within last 24 hours to a throwaway environment, restore time recorded.] 5. **Compliance signoff:** [privacy/legal/accessibility check completed where applicable. WCAG AA manually verified. List any reviewers.] 6. **Written stakeholder approval:** [captured in writing — email, ticket, signed PDF, chat message you can search later. Reference the artifact (PR URL, screenshot bundle, or final-report file). Self-attestation by saying "approved" to the AI is not a paper trail.] ## Recommendations for next sprint [E.g. dark mode, A/B test the dashboard, deeper responsive work] Live preview handoff: confirm dev server is running, give me the EXACT URL, walk me through the redesigned admin as a tour. Production deployment / merge to main happens AFTER my explicit "yes deploy" — and only when every item in Deploy Readiness above is filled in. Update /redesign/RECIPE-PROGRESS.md — Phase 7 complete. (NOT the recipe — Phase 8 Maintain comes next.)

    The redesign is functionally complete but the design system needs consolidation. Phase 8 captures that.
    7→8
    Session Handoff — Phase 7 to Phase 8~5 min  ·  REQUIRED — close prior session, paste re-prime in fresh one
    ▶
    Required step. Skip ONLY if your current session is under ~30 minutes old AND you've sent fewer than ~10 messages since Phase 7 ended.
    Why a fresh session here — Phase 8 extracts patterns from your shipped code to refresh the design system. A fresh session prevents Phase 7's audit-mindset from re-judging things instead of consolidating them.

    Step 1 — Close the Phase 7 session cleanly

    Confirm before closing:

    • ☐ All gauntlet pass-logs that fired exist: CLARIFY-PASS.md, HARDEN-PASS.md, ADAPT-PASS.md (if it ran), ONBOARD-PASS.md (if it ran), PERF-REPORT.md (if optimize ran)
    • ☐ /redesign/FINAL-REPORT.md exists

    • ☐ Every FAKE placeholder still showing in the shipped frontend has a DOM marker (e.g. data-backend-fake="[feature]") so Phase 8's extract pass can flag them as not-yet-real components

    • ☐ No BLOCKS-REDESIGN plan file is in an ambiguous state — every one is HANDLED, STAGED, or explicitly DEFERRED-WITH-FAKE-ACCEPTED in writing

    Step 2 — Open a fresh Claude Code session and paste the re-prime

    8
    Maintain — Consolidate & Refresh DESIGN.md~25 min  ·  DESIGN.md (refreshed) + EXTRACT-REPORT.md
    ▶
    Maintain is the fourth phase the Impeccable docs name. Even a clean redesign creates drift — the same card, button, or form pattern repeats with tiny differences across pages. Without consolidation, the next sprint inherits this drift silently.
    Why this matters even for non-coders — when you (or a future developer) come back in three months to add a new admin page, the next /impeccable craft call reads whatever DESIGN.md says. If DESIGN.md still describes the system from Phase 4 — before any pages were built — craft has no way to match the patterns Phase 6 actually established. Phase 8 closes that loop.

    Step 1 — Find the patterns that repeat

    What this command does — /impeccable extract finds patterns repeated 3 or more times across the redesigned admin and consolidates them into reusable tokens or components. Two usages are not a pattern — extract requires three.
    TYPE THIS COMMAND — type the green command below into Claude Code. Wait for it to finish before pasting the framing prompt.
    /impeccable extract
    THEN PASTE THIS PROMPT — paste the framing prompt below into Claude Code. It captures the output and applies the per-page actions.
    Phase 8 Step 1 — Run extract
    Read DESIGN.md, every brief in /redesign/briefs/, and the redesigned admin source code. Find every pattern that appears 3+ times across the redesigned admin. Examples: - A button variant that appears with the same styling in 4 places - A card layout used on 5 pages - A data-table density that's repeated - A form input style with the same border / focus ring / label position For each pattern propose: - A token name or component name - A variant API if it has variants - The CSS rule that consolidates them Migrate every duplicated CSS in the same pass. Extract is not a "find now, fix later" command — it consolidates and migrates atomically. Save the migration log to /redesign/EXTRACT-REPORT.md (which patterns, where they were, what they became). Then STOP. I will review the migration before Step 2.

    Step 2 — Refresh DESIGN.md to reflect the post-redesign state

    What this command does — /impeccable document regenerates DESIGN.md from the actual code as it ships now. This OVERWRITES the Phase 4 DESIGN.md. That's correct — the Phase 4 version is preserved in git history and in the redesign-baseline tag from Phase 0a.
    How this step works. /impeccable document overwrites DESIGN.md — you must preserve the supplementary patterns section BEFORE it runs, then re-attach it after. Three parts in strict order:

    1. Paste the first prompt below (Step 2a). It reads DESIGN.md and saves the ## Admin-Specific Patterns section to a temp file at /redesign/.preserved-design-section.md. Then STOP.

    2. Type the green /impeccable document command. Let it finish and regenerate DESIGN.md.

    3. Paste the second prompt (Step 2b). It confirms the file location and re-attaches the preserved patterns section from the temp file.
    Step 2a — Preserve supplementary section before document runs
    Read the current DESIGN.md (at project root). Find the "## Admin-Specific Patterns" section (added in Phase 4 Step 2 — covers data table density, bulk actions, status badges, and other admin-specific component variants). Copy the entire ## Admin-Specific Patterns section verbatim to a temp file at /redesign/.preserved-design-section.md. Confirm the temp file exists and contains the full section. Then STOP — do not run /impeccable document yet. Type the green command above when ready.
    THEN TYPE THIS COMMAND — type the green command below into Claude Code. Wait for it to finish before pasting the framing prompt.
    /impeccable document
    THEN PASTE THIS PROMPT — paste the framing prompt below into Claude Code. It captures the output and applies the per-page actions.
    Phase 8 Step 2b — Re-attach after document runs
    STEP C — Confirm file location: /impeccable document writes DESIGN.md AND DESIGN.json to PROJECT ROOT (overwriting the Phase 4 versions). That's the correct location — leave them there. NOTE — document confirms before overwriting: when /impeccable document is about to overwrite an existing DESIGN.md, it will pause and ask you to confirm. Confirm yes. STEP D — Re-attach the preserved section: Read /redesign/.preserved-design-section.md (the temp file you wrote in Step 2a). Append its contents to the BOTTOM of the new DESIGN.md (after the last canonical section, before any EOF). VERIFY: open DESIGN.md (at project root). It should contain the six canonical sections (Overview, Colors, Typography, Elevation, Components, Do's and Don'ts) PLUS the re-attached ## Admin-Specific Patterns section at the bottom. Update /redesign/RECIPE-PROGRESS.md — Phase 8 complete. The recipe is now done.
    ✅ What to check after Phase 8:
    1. EXTRACT-REPORT.md lists at least one consolidated pattern. If it lists none — either the redesign was unusually clean, or extract ran on the wrong files.
    2. The new DESIGN.md describes components that match what's actually rendered. Open one redesigned page and check three styles match the spec.
    3. The "## Admin-Specific Patterns" section was reattached.
    ⏸
    STOP — recipe complete
    You can now safely deploy. Production deployment / merge to main happens AFTER your explicit "yes deploy". If you've been running this recipe in a git repo with feature branches, invoke Superpowers' finishing-a-development-branch skill before merging or closing the branch — it walks the close-out checklist (commit hygiene, branch cleanup, final verification) better than freehand.

    After the recipe — adding new pages later

    The recipe was built for a planned redesign of an existing site. Once shipped, the artifacts you generated — PRODUCT.md (audience, voice, register, KEY_TASKS), DESIGN.md (tokens, components, patterns), and the brief format — keep paying dividends. Here's the minimal workflow when a client or product owner asks for a new page later.

    Workflow (one new page)

    1. Open a fresh Claude Code session. Briefs and design system are at project root, so no recipe-style re-prime is needed.
    2. Run /impeccable shape for the new page. It reads PRODUCT.md and DESIGN.md automatically. Save the resulting brief to /redesign/briefs/[new-page-slug]-brief.md to preserve the convention.
    3. Run /impeccable clarify draft copy for brief /redesign/briefs/[new-page-slug]-brief.md to append a ## Copy section.
    4. Run /impeccable craft redesign of admin page [new-page-name] with the brief loaded. Same mechanic as Phase 6 Step 1.
    5. Run /impeccable audit [page] and /impeccable critique [page]. P0 fix-on-the-spot, P1 batch for next time.
    6. Smoke test, commit, ship. No new gauntlet needed for one page.
    7. (Optional) Append a row to /redesign/IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md for record-keeping. Mark [x] immediately with commit hash.
    Adding 3 or more pages at once? Run /impeccable shape for all pages first, save each brief, then follow the Phase 6 per-page loop in sequence — one fresh session per page. Don't skip straight to craft without briefs for each.

    What NOT to do

    • Don't re-run /impeccable teach — PRODUCT.md already exists and is correct.
    • Don't re-run /impeccable document — DESIGN.md already exists and is correct.
    • Don't re-run the Phase 7 gauntlet site-wide — gauntlet is for redesign-scale work, not single-page additions.

    When to refresh DESIGN.md

    If the new page introduces a genuinely new component pattern (not just a variant of existing components), run /impeccable extract on the new page after it ships, then /impeccable document to regenerate DESIGN.md. That's Phase 8's mechanic, repeated manually.

    The two prompts you need — open
    Prompt 1 — New page workflow (shape → clarify → craft → audit/critique)
    I want to add a new admin page using the existing design system. Project context is already in place: PRODUCT.md and DESIGN.md are at project root, /redesign/briefs/ contains historical briefs from the original redesign. The new page: [describe purpose, audience, what it does — 2-3 sentences] Suggested page slug: [new-page-slug] STEP 1 — Read PRODUCT.md (especially KEY_TASKS, Brand Personality, Anti-references) and DESIGN.md (full file). Confirm in one message what you read. STEP 2 — Run /impeccable shape for the new page. Save the resulting brief to /redesign/briefs/[new-page-slug]-brief.md. STEP 3 — Run /impeccable clarify draft copy for brief /redesign/briefs/[new-page-slug]-brief.md. The clarify pass appends a ## Copy section to the brief. STEP 4 — Show me the brief, including its Copy section. STOP. Wait for me to approve before craft. STEP 5 — On approval, run /impeccable craft redesign of admin page [new-page-name]. Use the brief as the source for craft's internal shape discovery (don't re-interview me). STEP 6 — Run /impeccable audit [page] and /impeccable critique [page]. P0 audit findings: fix before commit. P1: list and we'll batch later. STEP 7 — Smoke test (forms, buttons, keyboard nav, JS hooks per the brief's Feature Preservation Manifest). STEP 8 — Commit. Optionally append a row to /redesign/IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md.
    Prompt 2 — Refresh DESIGN.md if the new page introduced a new pattern
    The new page [page-name] introduced a component pattern that isn't currently in DESIGN.md (e.g. [describe the pattern: a new card variant, a new dialog style, a new data-density mode]). I want to consolidate it back into the design system. STEP 1 — Run /impeccable extract on [page-name]. Save the extract report to /redesign/EXTRACT-REPORT-[YYYYMMDD].md. STEP 2 — Show me the extracted patterns + which are genuinely new vs. variants of existing patterns. STOP for my review. STEP 3 — On approval, run /impeccable document. It will overwrite DESIGN.md and DESIGN.json at project root — that's correct, the new version absorbs the new pattern. STEP 4 — If the original DESIGN.md had a custom appended section (e.g. "## Admin-Specific Patterns"), preserve it before /impeccable document overwrites, then re-append after. STEP 5 — Tell me which sections of DESIGN.md changed before I commit.

      The SEO-MANIFEST.md and COPY-AUDIT.md are unique to this recipe — they don't exist in the admin recipe. They protect your Google rankings and give you a clear before/after comparison of your content quality.
      If the AI continues past a STOP without your input — immediately type: STOP — return to Phase [N] pause point and re-run that phase's prompt. Don't let it improvise past a checkpoint. Pause points are not optional.
      STOP vs continuous run: the recipe's prompt blocks include "STOP and wait for review" instructions at every checkpoint. STOP is the safer default — it gives you review gates between expensive operations. If you want continuous run instead (faster, but skips review), edit "STOP and wait for me to review" → "continue to the next step" in the prompt before pasting. This trades safety for speed; only do it for low-stakes phases or after you've run the recipe end-to-end once and trust your setup.
      Working with the AI — session strategy. Long sessions lose context and degrade silently. Between phases that change in character, this recipe inserts five session-handoff cards (Phase 3→4, 4→5, 5→6, 6→7, 7→8) — purple-bordered accordions interleaved with the work phases below. Each handoff card has a closing checklist, a copy-paste re-prime prompt, and a one-line explanation. Treat them as required steps, not advice. The 5→6 handoff repeats once per page redesigned. For customer-facing this matters double — SEO regressions from context bleed cost rankings.
      Session Strategy Reference (legacy summary — click to expand)
      For users who want the per-phase rhythm at a glance. The five handoff cards above supersede this list — but the rhythm here is the same:
      • Phases 0a–3: one continuous session is fine.
      • Phase 4: fresh session — re-prime via the 3→4 handoff card.
      • Phase 5: fresh session — re-prime via the 4→5 handoff card.
      • Phase 6: fresh session per page — re-prime via the 5→6 handoff card, repeated.
      • Phase 7: fresh session — re-prime via the 6→7 handoff card.
      • Phase 8: fresh session — re-prime via the 7→8 handoff card. (Post-redesign DESIGN.md is deliberately NOT re-read; Phase 8 regenerates it.)
      Generic re-prime template (only if a handoff card is missing or you need to improvise): "Read these files first, then continue from Phase [N]: [list of files]. Do not start any work until you have read them all and confirmed back to me what each one contains. If anything has been compacted or summarised in your context, read the original file again."
      Need to redo an earlier phase? — open if you discover a mistake mid-recipe
      Mistakes happen. The recipe doesn't break if you back up and re-run a phase — but file outputs cascade, so know which downstream files need re-running too:
      • PRODUCT.md is wrong (audience, voice, register, KEY_TASKS) → re-run /impeccable teach. Cascade: re-run Phase 4 /impeccable document (DESIGN.md depends on PRODUCT.md), then re-run Phase 5 briefs.
      • DESIGN.md is wrong (tokens, components, anti-references) → re-run Phase 4 /impeccable document. Cascade: re-run Phase 5 briefs.
      • SEO-MANIFEST.md is wrong (a page's title / meta / H1 captured incorrectly) → edit SEO-MANIFEST.md directly, then re-run Phase 5 Step 3 (SEO append per brief) for the affected pages.
      • CUSTOMER-JOURNEY.md is wrong → edit it directly. Cascade: re-run Phase 5 Step 4 (Lock Implementation Order) so the journey-driven page order in IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md reflects reality.
      • One brief is wrong → re-run shape for that one page, rename the new brief.md, re-run clarify + SEO append for it, edit the affected row in IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md if the slug changed.
      • One page's craft output is wrong → see the escape valves block in Phase 6 (covers craft-failed, brief-was-wrong, park-this-page, and SEO regression recovery).
      • Direction (Phase 3) needs adjustment → edit the ## Design Direction section of PRODUCT.md directly. Phase 4 reads PRODUCT.md natively. No earlier command to re-run.
      Rule: if you change PRODUCT.md, DESIGN.md, or SEO-MANIFEST.md after Phase 5 has already run, regenerate the affected briefs — don't try to "patch" them by hand. Briefs are cheap to regenerate; downstream code is not.

      How the three tools layer in this recipe

      Impeccable is the designer. Every /impeccable … step is where Claude plans, writes, or refines code. This is the backbone of the recipe.

      Playwright is the visual checker. It opens your running site in a browser, takes screenshots, and fills forms.

      Chrome DevTools is the browser inspector. It reads console errors, network requests, and accessibility info — facts the source code can't tell us alone.

      The recipe always uses Impeccable to make changes, and Playwright + Chrome DevTools to check changes. If you ever see a step asking the model to inspect or screenshot something without using one of these tools, type STOP — that step is wrong.

      Cost & time per phase (rough — varies with codebase size).
      • Phase 0a: 💰 (~10–20 min)
      • Phase 0: 💰💰 (~25–40 min — recon + SEO catalogue + a11y baseline)
      • Phase 1: 💰 (~15 min)
      • Phase 2: 💰💰 (~25–40 min — screenshots + copy audit)
      • Phase 3: 💰 (~15 min)
      • Phase 4: 💰💰 (~30–45 min — design system + copy system)
      • Phase 5: 💰💰💰 (one plan per page; 10 pages ≈ 75–110 min, big token spend)
      • Phase 6: 💰💰💰💰 (actual implementation, 20–60 min per page)
      • Phase 7: 💰💰💰 (~50–70 min — QA + SEO + cross-browser)
      If you have a token / usage cap, plan to run Phase 5 and Phase 6 across multiple sessions.

      Default rule for all branches — no edit reaches the live URL until Phase 7 sign-off. If the AI ever proposes a change that would touch the live site before then, type STOP.
      ✅ What to check before continuing:
      1. SAFETY-NET.md records WHICH backup path you chose (A / B / C) and exact rollback steps for the live site.
      2. Open the backup zip, browse the staging URL, or confirm the redesign-baseline tag exists — prove the safety net actually works.
      3. Test-restore drill (required, not optional): in a throwaway directory, actually perform the rollback once and confirm the codebase is healthy after.
        Branch A or B-with-git, Mac/Linux: mkdir /tmp/rollback-test && cd /tmp/rollback-test && git clone [your-repo] . && git reset --hard redesign-baseline then run the dev server — confirm it boots.
        Branch A or B-with-git, Windows (PowerShell): New-Item -ItemType Directory -Path "$env:TEMP\rollback-test" -Force | Set-Location; git clone [your-repo] .; git reset --hard redesign-baseline then run the dev server — confirm it boots.
        Branch B without git: extract the backup zip into a fresh directory and run the project — confirm it boots.
        Branch C: revert the staging snapshot once and confirm the staging URL recovers.
        Record the restore time in SAFETY-NET.md. Customer-facing rollback under stress costs SEO and conversions — STOP if the restore fails.
      4. The rule "no edits to live until Phase 7 sign-off" is written down so neither of us forgets when momentum builds.
      Don't have a branching workflow? — open for simple defaults
      Not using git? Close this block. Your Phase 0a safety net already covers you.
      If you ARE using git but don't have a branching convention, here's the minimal one for this recipe:
      • Before Phase 6 starts, create one branch named redesign off your current main.
      • All Phase 6 work happens on this branch. Commit after each page (Phase 6 already enforces this).
      • When Phase 7 is fully done and signed off, merge redesign into main.
      That's it. No PRs needed unless your team requires them. No staging environments unless you have one. No naming patterns beyond redesign.

      Confirm SAFETY-NET.md exists and the rollback command/path is recorded, then proceed to Phase 0.

      Reconnaissance + SEO Preservation~25–35 min  ·  RECON.md + SEO-MANIFEST.md
      ▶
      SEO preservation is done here, before any work starts. This catalogues every meta tag, heading, schema, and alt text. Nothing in this catalogue can be lost. If SEO rankings are important to the business, this step is not optional.
      ★ Phase 0 — Run this first
      Phase 0 — Reconnaissance + SEO Preservation. This phase maps the codebase AND catalogues all SEO data before anything changes. Do NOT touch any files. ════════════════════════════════════════ STEP 1 — MAP THE CODEBASE ════════════════════════════════════════ Same codebase mapping as admin recipe: - Folder structure and purpose of each folder - Tech stack: framework, CSS, backend, database, CMS if present - Entry point files ════════════════════════════════════════ STEP 2 — CLASSIFY ALL PAGES ════════════════════════════════════════ Find every page and classify: - CUSTOMER: pages the public sees — THESE are what we redesign - ADMIN: staff-only pages — we will NOT touch these - SHARED: layouts, partials shared between both For every CUSTOMER-FACING page, document: | Field | Detail | - File path (exact) - URL / route - Page purpose (1 sentence) - Page type: homepage / service / product / pricing / about / contact / booking / account / blog / legal - Key sections visible: hero, features, testimonials, CTA, form, gallery, etc. - Forms found: every input name attribute - Conversion actions: what is the user supposed to DO on this page? (book, sign up, contact, buy) ════════════════════════════════════════ STEP 3 — SEO PRESERVATION CATALOGUE ════════════════════════════════════════ For EVERY customer-facing page, catalogue all SEO elements. NOTHING in this catalogue can be removed or changed without an explicit decision. PLAIN-ENGLISH GLOSS (for the human reading this): the items below are HTML/JSON snippets that Google reads to rank pages and decide how they appear in search results. You don't need to understand the syntax — Claude does the extraction. What matters is that nothing on this list disappears during the redesign. - title tag = the blue clickable headline in Google results - meta description = the grey body text under the headline in Google results - canonical URL = tells Google "this page's official address" (prevents duplicate-content penalties) - Open Graph tags (og:title, og:image, etc.) = how the page looks when shared on Facebook / LinkedIn / Slack - Twitter Card tags = same idea, for Twitter / X - Schema.org JSON-LD = the data that produces rich results (star ratings, prices, recipes, FAQ accordions) - H1 / H2-H4 = the page's heading hierarchy (Google uses this to understand topic structure) - alt text = what screen readers say for images, AND what Google uses to index them - internal links = the href values pointing to other pages (your site's URL structure) - hreflang = signals to Google "this page is the English version, that one is the Spanish version" (only relevant if multilingual) For each page, document: - <title> tag text - meta description content - meta keywords (if present) - canonical URL (if set) - Open Graph tags: og:title, og:description, og:image, og:url - Twitter Card tags (if present) - Schema.org structured data (JSON-LD scripts, itemscope/itemtype attributes) - H1 tag text (there should be exactly one per page) - H2-H4 heading texts (in order) - Alt text on all images - Internal links (href values — these are the URL structure, must be preserved) - Any hreflang tags (international targeting) Save to: /redesign/SEO-MANIFEST.md ════════════════════════════════════════ STEP 4 — CUSTOMER JOURNEY MAPPING ════════════════════════════════════════ From the page inventory, map the conversion paths: - How does a visitor move from discovery to conversion? - What is the main conversion goal? (book / buy / sign up / contact) - Which pages lead to which pages? - Where are the CTAs and where do they go? - Is there a multi-step flow (e.g. service page → booking form → confirmation)? Draw a simple flow diagram in text: Homepage → [page] → [page] → [conversion goal] This map will drive how we prioritise the redesign and what copy each page needs. ════════════════════════════════════════ STEP 5 — FEATURE PRESERVATION MANIFEST ════════════════════════════════════════ For every customer-facing page: - Forms: name attributes, action URLs, method, any validation - Interactive elements: buttons (text + href/action), booking widgets, chat plugins - Third-party integrations: booking systems, payment processors, maps, calendars - JavaScript dependencies: class names and IDs used in JS selectors ════════════════════════════════════════ STEP 6 — UNTOUCHABLE FILES (BACKEND) ════════════════════════════════════════ Flag all backend logic files permanently off-limits: routes, controllers, models, API handlers, payment logic, booking system logic, authentication, database schemas, migrations. ════════════════════════════════════════ STEP 7 — LIVE VISUAL SCAN ════════════════════════════════════════ Note: if the site requires credentials or is behind a VPN, I will provide access details before you begin. SERVER DETECTION — run first, before any navigation: 1. First check /redesign/SAFETY-NET.md. If it says "Safety Net Chosen: C — staging on [host]", use the staging URL recorded there. Skip the rest of server detection. 2. If Branches A or B were chosen (local code on disk), check if a dev server is already running. Try in order: localhost:3000 localhost:3001 localhost:5173 localhost:4321 localhost:8000 localhost:8080 3. If a port responds → use that URL for all subsequent steps. 4. If nothing responds → check package.json for a "dev" or "start" script and run it. Wait until the server confirms it is ready. 5. If it fails to start → STOP. Tell me what happened. I will provide a staging or production URL. State the URL you are using before doing anything else. Playwright MCP: 1. Navigate to the homepage 2. Visit every customer-facing page found in Step 2 3. Screenshot each at 1440px width (desktop) 4. Screenshot each at 375px width (mobile) 5. Note any pages that look broken, incomplete, or clearly outdated Chrome DevTools MCP on each page: 1. Console errors: list all errors and warnings 2. Network: list all failed requests 3. Performance: note LCP time if visible ════════════════════════════════════════ STEP 8 — ACCESSIBILITY BASELINE ════════════════════════════════════════ For every customer-facing page, count and document: - Images: total count / how many have an alt attribute / how many have descriptive (non-empty, non-filename) alt - Form inputs: total count / how many have a properly associated <label> (or aria-label / aria-labelledby) - Heading hierarchy: pages with no H1, multiple H1s, or skipped levels (e.g. H1 → H3 with no H2) - Obvious contrast issues: spot-check Chrome DevTools' contrast warnings on hero, body, and CTAs - Keyboard traps: tab through the homepage and a conversion form — does focus ever get stuck or skip an interactive element? This is the score we have to beat. Customer-facing sites often score poorly on a11y — that's an opportunity, but only if we measure it now. Save to /redesign/A11Y-BASELINE.md, one section per page. ════════════════════════════════════════ STEP 9 — IMAGE & ASSET INVENTORY ════════════════════════════════════════ For every image referenced by a customer-facing page (img tags, CSS background-image, hero/og images, icon sprites): - Path - Current file size (KB) - Format (PNG / JPG / SVG / WebP / AVIF / other) - Has descriptive alt text? (yes / no / generic-only) - Issue (one of: too large >200KB / wrong format / missing alt / generic alt / low resolution / stock-looking / none) - Proposed action (auto-convert to WebP / compress / replace with new photo / add alt / leave alone) Save to /redesign/IMAGES-NEEDED.md as a table. This is the master list — Phase 6 will refer to it and add new entries during implementation. ════════════════════════════════════════ OUTPUTS ════════════════════════════════════════ Create folder /redesign/ (if not exists). Save /redesign/RECON.md (same structure as admin recipe + customer journey map section) Save /redesign/SEO-MANIFEST.md (every SEO element per page) Save /redesign/A11Y-BASELINE.md (from Step 8 — accessibility scores per page) Save /redesign/IMAGES-NEEDED.md (from Step 9 — image inventory + actions) Save /redesign/RECIPE-PROGRESS.md with: ## Customer-Facing Redesign — Progress - [x] Phase 0a — Safety Net & Backup: COMPLETE - [x] Phase 0 — Reconnaissance + SEO: COMPLETE - [ ] Phase 1 — Context + Target Market - [ ] Phase 2 — Baseline + Copy Audit - [ ] Phase 3 — Design Direction - [ ] Phase 4 — Design System - [ ] Phase 5 — Page Plans + Copy - [ ] Phase 6 — Implementation - [ ] Phase 7 — QA + SEO Check Summary: 10 key findings. Then STOP and wait for my review of RECON.md, SEO-MANIFEST.md, A11Y-BASELINE.md, and IMAGES-NEEDED.md.
      ✅ What to check in RECON.md, SEO-MANIFEST.md, A11Y-BASELINE.md, IMAGES-NEEDED.md:
      1. Page count and conversion paths reflect your real site (within ±10% of what you remember).
      2. SEO-MANIFEST.md verification: do NOT spot-check just 3 random pages — the manifest drives downstream decisions for the whole redesign. Programmatically crawl the live site for every customer-facing page and diff against the manifest. For each page, fetch the live HTML, extract <title>, <meta name="description">, <h1>, JSON-LD blocks, canonical, and Open Graph tags, then compare against the manifest entry. Output any mismatches as a list. Review every mismatch before continuing — if SEO-MANIFEST drifts from the live site here, every Phase 5 brief inherits the drift.
      3. A11Y-BASELINE.md and IMAGES-NEEDED.md mention real pages and images, not generic placeholders.
      4. Untouchable-list omission check: pick 3 random files NOT flagged as untouchable. Open each. If any imports backend modules, hits a database, defines API routes, or contains business logic, the AI missed it — add it to the untouchable list before Phase 6 starts. Hallucination detection is one direction; omission detection is the inverse.
      📅 Reality check before you commit to the recipe. Now that RECON.md and SEO-MANIFEST.md are written, you know your page count. Total time depends on it:
      • Phase 5 briefs (incl. SEO append + copy draft): ~10–15 min × your page count
      • Phase 6 builds (incl. mobile adapt): ~40–70 min × your page count
      • For 12 pages: ~10–17 hours of focused work + 💰💰💰 token band
      Decide now whether to commit. Customer-facing redesigns also touch SEO and conversion paths — the cost of a half-finished pass is higher than for admin. If your scope is 20+ pages or your token cap is tight, plan across 3+ multi-day sessions before starting Phase 1.
      Where to actually source the images IMAGES-NEEDED.md asks for — open if some entries need new photography
      If this recipe needs no new images — all existing images are already optimised and no new photography is required — close this block and continue. No action needed.
      IMAGES-NEEDED.md tells you what imagery is missing. Here's how to actually source it:
      • Stock (license-free): Unsplash, Pexels, Pixabay. Best for hero backgrounds, generic context shots. Match your brand voice — don't pick the first result. Always credit per the license.
      • Client-supplied: ask for the highest-resolution originals you can get. Phone photos are fine if recent and well-lit.
      • AI generation: Midjourney, DALL-E, Stable Diffusion. Good for stylised illustrations, abstract product shots, mood imagery. Risky for hero photography that implies "real people using real product".
      • Repurpose existing site assets: if the original site has good imagery, keep it. The redesign doesn't have to throw out working photography.
      • Placeholder + revisit: if a real photo is months away, ship with a tasteful gradient or illustration placeholder rather than a stock cliché. Tag the element with data-redesign-needs-photo so it shows up in Phase 7's pre-deploy search.
      Customer-facing extra:
      • Hero photography is conversion-critical — if the brief implies real people, real product, a real moment, do not ship stock. The visitor's first impression depends on this not feeling fake.
      • Product imagery deserves studio time. A flat lay shot in 30 minutes against a real backdrop beats AI-generated alternatives almost always.
      Step 10a — Foundation Floor Check (the minimum below which nothing ships)
      Every site needs a stack-agnostic operational floor before redesign begins: seven universal items (HTTPS, auth, backups, error tracking, environment separation, security headers, transactional email) plus any legal and regulatory items derived from the jurisdiction and business type captured in PRODUCT.md in Phase 1. Output is /redesign/FOUNDATION-FLOOR.md — recipe-supplementary, lives in /redesign/. This is not /impeccable audit — that command runs a technical-quality audit in Phase 7 and is entirely separate from this operational floor check.
      Step 10a — produce FOUNDATION-FLOOR.md
      Read: PRODUCT.md (specifically the niche specificity, jurisdiction, business scale, business model, and payment model lines added in Phase 1 — if Phase 1 has not run yet, note the fields are pending and proceed with the Universal Floor section only), /redesign/RECON.md, and /redesign/SEO-MANIFEST.md (if written — it captures analytics, tracking, and consent-related infrastructure already present). Produce /redesign/FOUNDATION-FLOOR.md with TWO clearly labeled sections: --- SECTION 1: Universal Floor --- Seven stack-agnostic items every site needs. Inspect the codebase and the live customer-facing site for each: 1. HTTPS — live site HTTPS-only? HSTS set? Mixed-content warnings present? 2. Auth basics — auth-gated pages behind real authentication? (For pure marketing sites with no auth, mark N-A.) 3. Backups configured — automated database backup exists? Storage location known? Restore ever tested? 4. Error tracking installed — Sentry, Rollbar, server logs, or equivalent catching server errors and frontend exceptions? 5. Environment separation — non-production environment (staging/dev) distinct from live? 6. Security headers — Content-Security-Policy, X-Frame-Options, X-Content-Type-Options, Referrer-Policy present? Note any header that would interact with embedded third-party scripts (analytics, chat widgets, payment iframes). 7. Transactional email working — sender for confirmations / password resets / contact-form notifications? Fails silently? For each item: status PRESENT / PARTIAL / MISSING with one-line evidence from the codebase. Tag each item: BLOCKS-LAUNCH or NICE-TO-HAVE. --- SECTION 2: Jurisdiction-Derived Legal Floor --- Using the Jurisdiction and Niche specificity fields from PRODUCT.md. If those fields are not yet written (Phase 1 still pending), leave this section with its heading and a single note: "Run Step 10a again after Phase 1 Step 2 completes to fill this section." If the fields ARE present: research what legal / regulatory / data-protection items apply to THIS business in THIS jurisdiction. Examples to guide — not a complete list: - UK B2C → GDPR + ICO cookie consent + Equality Act 2010 accessibility AA + PECR for email marketing - US e-commerce → CAN-SPAM + state sales tax disclosure + ADA accessibility AA - UK complementary therapy → ICO registration + CNHC code-of-practice data requirements List 3–8 items specific to this business. Same format: status (PRESENT / PARTIAL / MISSING) + one-line evidence. Tag each: BLOCKS-LAUNCH or NICE-TO-HAVE. Do NOT cross-reference with /impeccable audit — that runs a separate technical-quality pass in Phase 7. Update /redesign/RECIPE-PROGRESS.md — append: "- [x] Foundation Floor Check (Step 10a): COMPLETE" STOP. Wait for review. BUSINESS-COMPLETENESS.md is produced in Phase 1 Step 3 once PRODUCT.md is complete.
      After Step 10a, /redesign/FOUNDATION-FLOOR.md should exist with two clearly-labeled sections, every item PRESENT/PARTIAL/MISSING-tagged, every item priority-tagged (BLOCKS-LAUNCH or NICE-TO-HAVE). The model should NOT have asked clarifying questions about jurisdiction — that information is already in PRODUCT.md from Phase 1.

      STOP — review before continuing

      1
      Business Context + Target Market + Brand Voice~15 min  ·  PRODUCT.md + CUSTOMER-JOURNEY.md
      ▶
      PRODUCT.md is the file every Impeccable command reads before doing anything. For customer-facing work, the brand voice and target market that go into PRODUCT.md drive every word of copy and every visual choice on the redesigned site. We build it in two passes.

      Step 1 — Let Impeccable interview you

      What this command does — /impeccable teach runs a short interview about your business. It asks ~5 questions, scans your codebase to form an early hypothesis, and writes PRODUCT.md at your project root with: Register, Users, Product Purpose, Brand Personality, Anti-references, Design Principles, Accessibility. Note for Claude Code users: the teach interview is naturally a brainstorming-character task — invoke Superpowers' brainstorming skill before starting if your harness supports it.
      /impeccable teach
      Before you fire the command — open the cheat sheet's "Setup & Pinning" page (left nav) and read the "Questions /impeccable teach will ask you — be ready" panel. Five minutes of prep prevents freezing mid-interview. What you’ll see — Claude scans the codebase, then asks questions one at a time about: who the site is for, what register it lives in (brand vs product — for customer-facing this is "brand"), the brand voice, and aesthetics to avoid (anti-references — these are what stop later commands producing generic AI output). It quotes your answers back before writing. What you’ll get back — PRODUCT.md at your project root. Leave it there — the skill expects it at root; Step 2 will append customer-specific sections to it in place.
      The single most important field — when Claude asks about register, say "brand". Customer-facing pages are brand register (design IS the product). The Impeccable docs reference register influencing typography / motion / color defaults across downstream commands but don't enumerate per-command branching, so the safest bet is the register that matches your work — for customer-facing marketing-quality work, "brand" is the right pick.
      When teach asks about anti-references, name the saturated 2026 aesthetic lanes you don’t want to drift into: editorial-typographic (display serif italic + ruled separators — the Klim-influenced lane), brutalist-utility, acid-maximalism. Plus the AI-default fonts: Inter, Plus Jakarta, Cormorant, Fraunces, Space Grotesk, Instrument. These belong on your anti-references list unless your brief literally requires one.
      RECIPE-SPECIFIC DEVIATION: decline the auto-offer of /impeccable document — when teach finishes, the skill asks "want me to run /impeccable document now?" The Impeccable docs say the default expectation is that you accept ("Users can accept unless they have reason to delay"). This recipe asks you to decline because Phase 4 will overwrite the early DESIGN.md anyway, and running document twice creates churn. The decline is a recipe-specific override of skill default behavior, not how the skill is meant to be used out of the box. Say NO when prompted.

      Step 2 — Add target-market and conversion fields teach doesn’t ask about

      Teach captures register, users, voice, anti-references. The customer recipe needs three more areas teach doesn’t ask: detailed target market (demographics, psychographics, buyer behaviour, trust signals), conversion strategy, and integration constraints (third-party widgets, broken backend features). We append these to PRODUCT.md.

      Visual Baseline + Copy Audit~25 min  ·  baseline/ screenshots + COPY-AUDIT.md
      ▶

      Step 3 — Score the implementation quality

      What this command does — /impeccable audit scores your existing customer-facing pages on five technical dimensions: accessibility, performance, theming, responsive design, anti-patterns. Each dimension scores 0–4. Issues are tagged P0 (blocks release) to P3 (polish). Audit produces a report — it makes no changes to your code. Argument grammar: the recipe uses natural-language scope arguments throughout (/impeccable audit customer pages for site-wide, /impeccable audit [page] for per-page). The Impeccable docs describe audit as accepting natural-language input, but exact argument grammar may vary by skill version. If a scope argument fails ("unknown scope"), retry with a file path or a single-word scope, and note the working form for use in the rest of the recipe.
      TYPE THIS COMMAND — type the green command below into Claude Code. Wait for it to finish before pasting the framing prompt.
      /impeccable audit customer pages
      THEN PASTE THIS PROMPT — paste the framing prompt below into Claude Code. It captures the output and applies the per-page actions.
      Step 3 — Run audit and save the baseline
      After audit produces its report: 1. Save the FULL output to /redesign/BASELINE-AUDIT.md. 2. Verify the file contains all 5 dimension scores: Accessibility, Performance, Theming, Responsive, Anti-patterns. 3. Count P0 findings — these are blockers Phase 7 must clear. 4. Tell me the 5 dimension scores in plain English plus the P0 count, then STOP.

      Step 4 — Score the design quality

      What this command does — /impeccable critique is different from audit. Audit measures implementation quality (a11y, perf, anti-patterns). Critique measures design quality — visual hierarchy, cognitive load, persona testing, plus an "AI-slop verdict" (does this look machine-generated?). It scores Nielsen's 10 usability heuristics 0–4 each. For customer-facing work the AI-slop verdict is especially load-bearing — a marketing page that reads as machine-generated to a visitor is a commercial fail.
      TYPE THIS COMMAND — type the green command below into Claude Code. Wait for it to finish before pasting the framing prompt.
      /impeccable critique customer pages
      THEN PASTE THIS PROMPT — paste the framing prompt below into Claude Code. It captures the output and applies the per-page actions.
      Step 4 — Run critique and save the baseline
      After critique produces its report: 1. Save the FULL output to /redesign/BASELINE-CRITIQUE.md. 2. Verify the file contains all 10 Nielsen heuristic scores. 3. Verify the AI-slop verdict is present (pass / borderline / fail) — this is the bar Phase 7 must clear. 4. Tell me the 10 heuristic scores plus the AI-slop verdict in plain English, then STOP.

      Step 5 — Audit the existing copy

      What this does — /impeccable clarify natively rewrites copy across six surfaces. The Impeccable docs do NOT document any flag, mode, or argument that makes clarify produce a report without changes — clarify is a mutating command, full stop. We need a graded "before" benchmark for Phase 7 to compare against, so this step uses a regular Claude Code prompt (no slash command at all) that asks the model to read existing copy and grade it against PRODUCT.md. The grading dimensions match the six surfaces clarify natively owns, so Phase 7's clarify pass has a comparable baseline.
      Step 5 — Grade existing copy (regular prompt — no slash command)
      Read PRODUCT.md (Brand Personality, Target Market, Conversion Strategy) and /redesign/RECON.md. This is a GRADING task. Do NOT invoke /impeccable clarify under any circumstances — the Impeccable docs only describe clarify as a rewrite command, with no documented assessment / report-only / dry-run mode. If clarify runs, it WILL mutate copy in your codebase and destroy the "before" baseline this step is creating. Use a regular Claude Code prompt instead (i.e., what you are reading now). For every customer-facing page in RECON.md, extract the existing copy and grade it on this 4-point scale: Strong / Adequate / Needs work / Poor. Grade each of these surfaces (these are the six surfaces /impeccable clarify natively owns — we use the same dimensions so Phase 7's clarify pass has a comparable baseline): - Hero headline - Subheadline / tagline - Section headings - CTA button text (verb-first? outcome-specific? or generic like "Submit"?) - Form labels and placeholders - Error messages, empty states, tooltips, confirmations (the four microcopy surfaces) Plus two customer-specific dimensions: - Brand voice match: does it sound like the voice defined in PRODUCT.md? - SEO-copy alignment: do headings include the relevant terms from SEO-MANIFEST.md? Save the graded report to /redesign/COPY-AUDIT.md. If you find yourself rewriting any copy, STOP — that's Phase 5 (per-page) and Phase 7 (site-wide) work. This step is grade-only. Output summary: how many pages, how many "Needs work" or "Poor" copy assessments, top 3 specific copy issues to fix. Live preview handoff: confirm dev server, give EXACT URL. Update /redesign/RECIPE-PROGRESS.md — Phase 2 complete. STOP. Review baselines and copy audit before designing.

      1. Three baseline files (audit + critique + copy-audit) plus the screenshots are all you need to prove "improved" later.
      2. COPY-AUDIT.md flags pages you already suspected were weak — feels honest, not generic.
      3. The dev server URL works on your phone AND your laptop before approving.

      4
      Design System Creation~40 min  ·  DESIGN.md
      ▶
      This is the most important output. Phase 5 briefs and Phase 6 builds all read DESIGN.md. We build it in two passes: /impeccable document writes the canonical six-section format, then we append customer-facing patterns.

      Step 1 — Generate the canonical DESIGN.md

      What this command does — /impeccable document writes DESIGN.md in the canonical Google Stitch format with six fixed sections (Overview, Colors, Typography, Elevation, Components, Do’s and Don’ts) plus a machine-readable DESIGN.json sidecar. This is the file every later Impeccable command reads. Do not invent a different format.
      TYPE THIS COMMAND — type the green command below into Claude Code. Wait for it to finish before pasting the framing prompt.
      /impeccable document --seed
      Plain-English gloss for the prompt below — the prompt uses technical terms because /impeccable document needs them. You don't need to evaluate the math; just paste and trust the AI.
      • OKLCH = a modern way to write colour values that produces visually-balanced palettes (more reliable than HSL or hex).
      • Chroma 0.005–0.015 = a tiny amount of colour mixed into otherwise grey neutrals, so backgrounds and borders feel like they belong to your brand.
      • WCAG ratio (4.5:1) = the contrast required for text to be readable. The AI prints these next to every colour pair. For customer-facing CTAs this MUST pass 4.5:1 — if not, conversion drops.
      • Performance budget (LCP < 2.5s, max page weight 2MB) = the speed targets. Customer-facing sites that miss these get penalised in Google ranking, not just user experience.
      What to check after — the "What to check in DESIGN.md" list at the bottom of this phase covers things you CAN evaluate without understanding the math: every colour pair has a ratio, the CTA passes 4.5:1, fonts aren't on anti-references, and every component listed exists in RECON.md.
      THEN PASTE THIS PROMPT — paste the framing prompt below into Claude Code. It captures the output and applies the per-page actions.
      Phase 4 Step 1 — Generate the design system
      Read first: /redesign/RECON.md, PRODUCT.md (incl. Target Market, Conversion Strategy, and the new ## Design Direction section), /redesign/CUSTOMER-JOURNEY.md, /redesign/SEO-MANIFEST.md. IMPORTANT — file location: /impeccable document writes DESIGN.md AND DESIGN.json to PROJECT ROOT. That's where they MUST stay — the skill expects them at project root. DESIGN.json sits at root as a sidecar to DESIGN.md, used by Live Mode's design panel. DO NOT move these files into /redesign/. Only recipe-supplementary files (RECON.md, SEO-MANIFEST.md, CUSTOMER-JOURNEY.md, BASELINE-*, RECIPE-PROGRESS.md, briefs/, etc.) live inside /redesign/. Generate a NEW DESIGN.md based on the direction in PRODUCT.md. This is greenfield design — do not extract from current code. Brand register applies. Write the six canonical sections: 1. Overview — design principles + atmosphere (3–5 sentences specific to THIS business and target market — no generic platitudes). Each principle must directly serve the conversion goal. 2. Colors — OKLCH colour space. - Tinted neutrals: chroma 0.005–0.015 toward the chosen brand hue - Roles: surface-page, surface-card, surface-input, surface-hover, trust-bg - Borders: subtle, default, strong, focus - Text: primary, secondary, muted, inverse - Actions: cta-primary (the conversion button — cannot fail 4.5:1), cta-primary-hover, secondary, destructive - Highlight: for pricing, key stats, emphasis - Status: success/warning/error/info - Every pair must have its WCAG ratio printed next to it. CTAs MUST pass 4.5:1. 3. Typography — respects PRODUCT.md anti-references (NEVER pick Inter / Plus Jakarta / Cormorant / Fraunces / Space Grotesk / Instrument by reflex). Brand register permits typographic risk if the brief invites it. Specify font-display, font-body. Maximum 2 families. Mobile hero size readable at 375px without zoom. 4. Elevation — surface scale, shadow rules. For brand work, motion + colour can replace shadow as the depth signal. 5. Components — ONLY components that exist in /redesign/RECON.md. CTA Button is THE most important component — specify size (min 48px on mobile), contrast, hover, focus. Plus: hero, social proof / testimonial, service card, contact/booking form, trust signals, footer, mobile nav. 6. Do’s and Don’ts — derived from PRODUCT.md anti-references. Be specific about what NOT to copy from the chosen reference. Validation: - Every colour pair has WCAG ratio - CTA passes 4.5:1 contrast - Hero type readable at 375px - Fonts NOT in anti-references list - Every component exists in RECON.md - Performance budget: max page weight 2MB, max image 200KB (WebP), LCP < 2.5s Tell me the system summary in plain English. Wait for confirmation before Step 2.

      Read DESIGN.md, /redesign/RECON.md, /redesign/SEO-MANIFEST.md, /redesign/CUSTOMER-JOURNEY.md. CAVEAT — recipe-supplementary section: per the Impeccable docs, DESIGN.md has six fixed canonical sections in fixed order (Overview, Colors, Typography, Elevation, Components, Do's and Don'ts). Append at the BOTTOM of DESIGN.md: ## Customer-Facing Patterns (Use existing tokens from the six canonical sections — do not introduce new ones.) ### Hero treatment per page type [Different hero shapes for: homepage / service / product / about / contact] Above-fold CTA visibility: required at every viewport (P0 if missing on mobile) ### Trust-signal placement and styling [Where testimonials / reviews / badges appear; style per type] ### CTA hierarchy across the conversion path [From CUSTOMER-JOURNEY.md — primary CTA appears at: top + bottom + after social proof] Sticky mobile CTA: [yes/no based on conversion goal] ### Form patterns [Booking forms, contact forms, newsletter signup — without modifying third-party widget HTML] Third-party integration rule: preserve embed code exactly; only restyle surrounding HTML ### Footer pattern [Standard footer treatment — links, contact, social] ### Schema integration [Preserve existing schema.org markup per SEO-MANIFEST.md; never remove] ### Imagery rules Photography style required: [from PRODUCT.md Design Direction] Stock photography: banned as final Per-image standards: WebP, ≤200KB, descriptive alt text, search the brand’s physical object not the generic category (Note: the next two subsections — Motion Tokens and Status Communication — are design-system guidelines. /impeccable animate may produce specific durations within these ranges, e.g. 180ms / 220ms / 260ms per its docs. Values are tuneable as long as they fit the fast / normal / slow philosophy.) ### Motion Tokens duration-fast: 150ms (toast appear, dropdown, hover state) duration-normal: 250ms (modal open/close, tab switch) duration-slow: 400ms (page transition, drawer slide, hero entrance) easing: ease-out for entrances, ease-in for exits, ease-in-out for two-way reduced-motion: @media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) — disable all transitions ### Status Communication Success (action confirmation, e.g. saved): toast top-right, auto-dismiss 4s, status-success colours Success (newsletter / form submission): toast top-right, auto-dismiss 6s — longer attention window for marketing acknowledgements Error: inline below the field for form errors; toast for system-level errors Warning: inline banner if action-blocking; toast if informational Loading: skeleton screens for content; spinner for CTAs Confirmation: modal for destructive actions (account deletion); inline for non-destructive Do NOT modify the six canonical sections. Append only. Update /redesign/RECIPE-PROGRESS.md — Phase 4 complete. Present the additions in plain English. Wait for confirmation before Phase 5.

      Each brief is a complete one-shot input for /impeccable craft. We generate them with /impeccable shape — the per-feature discovery interview command — then add draft copy with /impeccable clarify, then SEO preservation. You can edit any brief before Phase 6 runs.
      Customer-facing copy is conversion-critical. Review the Copy section of every brief carefully before Phase 6 — the AI drafts copy from your target market inputs, but you know the brand voice better. Edit freely.
      Heads-up: shape runs twice per page. Per the Impeccable docs, /impeccable craft runs /impeccable shape internally as its first non-skippable phase. Phase 5 runs shape standalone to produce a reviewable brief; Phase 6 then runs craft, which runs shape again using your brief as discovery input. The token cost reflects this doubling. The upside is your brief is reviewed and editable before any code is written. If your token budget is tight and you don't need the per-page review gate, you can skip Phase 5 standalone shape and feed PRODUCT.md + RECON.md + SEO-MANIFEST.md + CUSTOMER-JOURNEY.md directly to Phase 6's craft — accepting that the brief is generated implicitly inside craft and not separately reviewable.

      Step 1 — Shape a brief for every customer page

      What this command does — /impeccable shape runs a 5–10 question discovery interview for ONE page, then writes a brief. We run it once per customer page from RECON.md. Tell shape it’s redesigning AND brand-register, so it asks about preservation, conversion intent, and aesthetic-lane discipline. Convention: use the URL slug for [page-name] (e.g. homepage, services, contact, booking) — not the file path or display title. The same slug is used in the brief filename, IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md row, and per-page-scope file.
      How this step works. Phase 5 Step 1 produces one brief per customer page. Each page needs its own shape invocation — Impeccable's shape command is an interactive interview that runs once per feature, and Claude Code cannot loop it automatically. You drive the loop manually:

      1. Paste the first prompt below ("Generate per-page shape command list"). Claude Code reads RECON.md, SEO-MANIFEST.md, and CUSTOMER-JOURNEY.md, then emits a ready-to-paste list of /impeccable shape commands, one per customer page, ordered: shared components first, highest-traffic pages next, conversion-path pages after that, then the rest.

      2. For each command in that list: type the command into Claude Code, answer shape's discovery questions, wait for brief.md to be written at project root.

      3. After each shape interview completes, paste the second prompt below ("After each shape interview"). It instructs Claude Code to rename brief.md to /redesign/briefs/[page-slug]-brief.md and append the Recipe Context + Implementation Notes sections that downstream phases need.

      4. Repeat steps 2–3 for every command in the list. Move down the list top to bottom. For each customer-page interview, also reference SEO targets from SEO-MANIFEST.md and the page's role in CUSTOMER-JOURNEY.md when answering shape's discovery questions.

      5. After the final command in the list completes and Step 1b has run for the last page: paste the Step 1c prompt below. This runs once only — it produces the post-loop summary, flags any pages needing new images to IMAGES-NEEDED.md, and updates RECIPE-PROGRESS.md.
      Step 1a — Generate per-page shape command list
      Read /redesign/RECON.md, /redesign/SEO-MANIFEST.md, and /redesign/CUSTOMER-JOURNEY.md. Find the "Customer Pages Inventory" table in RECON.md (one row per customer page, columns include page name, file path, route, purpose, key elements). For each row, derive a URL slug from the page's route or page-name column. Slug rules: - Lowercase - Hyphens for spaces - No file path, no display title, just the slug Emit a code block containing one command per page, formatted exactly: /impeccable shape redesign of customer page [slug] Order the list as follows: 1. First line: /impeccable shape redesign of customer page 00-shared-layout (the 00 prefix ensures the shared-layout brief sorts first in /redesign/briefs/) 2.       TYPE THIS COMMAND FIRST — pick the next command from the list above and type it directly into Claude Code.       /impeccable shape redesign of customer page [page-name]
      THEN PASTE THIS PROMPT — once shape has written brief.md to project root, paste this prompt into Claude Code.       Phase 5 Step 1b — After each shape interview
      Read first: /redesign/RECON.md, PRODUCT.md, DESIGN.md, /redesign/SEO-MANIFEST.md, /redesign/CUSTOMER-JOURNEY.md, /redesign/COPY-AUDIT.md, /redesign/BASELINE-CRITIQUE.md. Create the folder /redesign/briefs/. CRITICAL — handling shape's brief output: the Impeccable docs say shape produces a "brief" but don't explicitly specify the filename, location, or multi-run behavior (overwrite/append/version). Empirically, shape writes a single file called brief.md at project root, and the next shape invocation overwrites it. This recipe assumes that empirical behavior. To collect one brief per page, you MUST move/rename brief.md after every single shape call BEFORE running the next one. Recipe convention: rename to /redesign/briefs/[page-slug]-brief.md. The 00-shared-* prefix is a recipe convention to make shared-layout briefs sort first — these are recipe-supplementary file names, not skill-native. The skill only ever sees brief.md at root. After shape writes brief.md, rename it to /redesign/briefs/[page-slug]-brief.md. Shape's native brief is deliberately minimal — five sections (Purpose / User / Content / Feeling / Constraints). Per the docs, it's "a compass, not a spec. It captures intent, not UI." Do NOT push state details, per-viewport layout intent, or SEO data into shape's five native sections — that violates shape's compass-not-spec philosophy. INSTEAD, after shape's interview completes and the 5-section brief is written, append TWO recipe-supplementary sections at the END of the brief file: first `## Recipe Context`, then `## Implementation Notes`. Both are required — Phase 6 craft reads Recipe Context for the files-to-edit / untouchable / preservation / journey-role lists, and Implementation Notes for state, viewport, and SEO detail. (A third recipe-supplementary section, `## SEO Preservation`, is added by Phase 5 Step 3 below.) If the discovery interview didn't naturally surface the Implementation Notes details, ask the user one short clarification round to fill them in. Generate Recipe Context from RECON.md / SEO-MANIFEST.md / CUSTOMER-JOURNEY.md (you've already read them) — no extra interview needed. The brief file structure should end up looking like: # Brief: [page-name] ## Purpose (shape native) ## User (shape native) ## Content (shape native) ## Feeling (shape native) ## Constraints (shape native) ## Recipe Context (recipe-supplementary — append after shape finishes) - Files to edit (from RECON.md) - Files to NEVER touch (untouchable list) - Feature Preservation Manifest (forms, buttons, JS hooks, third-party widgets, analytics) - Role in conversion journey (from CUSTOMER-JOURNEY.md) - Information hierarchy and section order - Design direction (which DESIGN.md components, which tokens) ## Implementation Notes (recipe-supplementary — append after Recipe Context) Per-state intent (for Phase 6 Step 4 harden): - Empty: Heading / Body / CTA / Visual treatment - Loading: skeleton vs spinner / behaviour - Error: Heading / Body / Action (retry / go back / contact) - Post-submission success: heading / body / next-action CTA — conversion-critical Per-viewport intent (for Phase 6 Step 1 craft + Step 3 adapt): - Mobile (375px) — customer-facing essentials: Navigation: collapse behaviour (hamburger / drawer / bottom-tab) Hero: how it adapts (image stack vs side-by-side, headline truncation rules) CTA placement: MUST remain above the fold at 375px (P0 contract for conversion) Forms: how fields stack vertically Trust signals: how testimonials / social proof / logos reflow Sticky mobile CTA: yes/no based on conversion goal Content: what reflows, what stays - Tablet (768px): only if different from mobile/desktop derivation - Desktop (1440px): primary canvas spec Verification steps (for Phase 6 Step 6 verify): Playwright + DevTools + /impeccable audit + /impeccable critique (The ## SEO Preservation subsection will be added in Phase 5 Step 3.) Phase 6 craft will read both shape's 5 native sections AND the Recipe Context + Implementation Notes + SEO Preservation appendices. Don't be alarmed when shape's brief looks shorter than the original recipe template described — the long detail lives below shape's native output, not inside it. STOP. This prompt covers ONE page only. Do not run shape for any other page. Do not generate any other briefs. Do not delete or rewrite the brief that shape just produced. Wait for me to type the next /impeccable shape command.
      ✅ STEP 1C — paste this once after the final shape command — only after every page in your command list has been through shape + Step 1b. This produces the summary and implementation order.

      Step 2 — Pre-write the customer-facing copy in every brief

      What this command does — /impeccable clarify drafts copy for every brief. Customer-facing copy includes hero headlines (3 options each), subheadlines, CTAs, trust signals, form microcopy, and post-submission messages. Voice from PRODUCT.md.
      How this step works. Phase 5 Step 2 produces a draft Copy section inside each brief from Step 1. /impeccable clarify is an interactive command that runs once per brief, using a framing prompt you load first to set its scope and constraints. You drive the loop manually:

      1. Paste the first prompt below ("Generate per-brief clarify command list"). Claude Code reads /redesign/briefs/ and emits a ready-to-paste list of /impeccable clarify commands, one per brief, in alphabetical order (so 00-shared-layout comes first).

      2. Paste the second prompt below ("Framing prompt for clarify") into Claude Code. This loads the framing — what copy surfaces to draft, what voice to use, where to save output, plus customer-specific surfaces like hero headlines and trust signals — into the session context. You only paste this prompt ONCE per Phase 5 Step 2 session.

      3. For each command in the list from step 1: type the command into Claude Code. Clarify runs with the framing in context, asks targeted questions about voice and tone for that brief, and appends a ## Copy section to the brief file. Move to the next command.

      4. Repeat the command-by-command loop until every brief has a Copy section. Then STOP for review.
      Step 2a — Generate per-brief clarify command list
      List every file in /redesign/briefs/ matching the pattern *-brief.md (these were produced by Phase 5 Step 1). For each brief, emit a single line in this exact format: /impeccable clarify draft copy for brief /redesign/briefs/[filename] Where [filename] is the actual brief filename (e.g. 00-shared-layout-brief.md, homepage-brief.md, services-brief.md). Order the list alphabetically. The Step 1 naming convention (00-shared-layout-brief.md) ensures the shared-layout brief sorts first. Output the list as a single code block, one command per line, no commentary between commands. After the code block, note the total count of briefs (e.g. "12 briefs found — 12 clarify commands listed"). Then STOP.       Step 2b — Framing prompt for clarify
      Read the brief, then draft these copy surfaces (the six clarify natively owns): - Form labels (every input the brief lists) - Form button text — verb-first, outcome-specific ("Book my appointment" not "Submit") - Error messages — specific, NEVER blames the user - Empty-state text — heading + body + CTA - Tooltip text (only where the brief specifies tooltips) - Confirmation dialog text — specific actions, not Yes/No Plus customer-specific surfaces: - Hero headline (3 options: A=benefit-focused, B=curiosity, C=direct/bold) - Hero subheadline + sub-CTA - Trust signals per section (specific reviews / certifications / guarantees) - Schema-friendly heading text (matches SEO-MANIFEST.md or improvement [REQUIRES APPROVAL]) - Form privacy / terms microcopy - Post-submission success message - 404 / error page copy Voice from PRODUCT.md (Brand Personality + Target Market).       Step 3 — Append SEO preservation to every brief

      Impeccable doesn’t natively read SEO-MANIFEST.md. We bake SEO requirements into each brief manually so Phase 6’s /impeccable craft sees them as part of the brief.
      Phase 5 Step 3 — SEO append per brief
      For every brief in /redesign/briefs/, read /redesign/SEO-MANIFEST.md and append a new section: ## SEO Preservation Title tag: "[exact current value from SEO-MANIFEST.md]" — preserve OR proposed improvement: "[new value]" [REQUIRES APPROVAL] Meta description: same shape H1: same shape Schema markup type: [type from SEO-MANIFEST.md] Internal links that must stay identical: [list of hrefs] Hreflang: [if any] Open Graph: og:title / og:description / og:image / og:url — preserve exact values If we are PROPOSING any SEO change, mark it [REQUIRES APPROVAL] and tell me before Phase 6 begins. Do NOT modify other sections of the brief. Append only. Confirm every brief now has a "## SEO Preservation" section. VERIFICATION CHALLENGE: for each brief, quote back to me — verbatim — the title tag, meta description, and H1 you appended. I will diff your quotes against /redesign/SEO-MANIFEST.md. If the values don't match exactly, the brief's preservation contract is wrong — we re-append before continuing. Paraphrasing here breaks SEO silently. STOP for review.
      ✅ What to check in /redesign/briefs/*.md (CRITICAL — last chance to edit before code):
      1. Each brief has discovery (from shape) + Copy (from clarify) + SEO Preservation (from Step 3).
      2. Skim 3 random briefs — copy and CTA wording match your brand voice from PRODUCT.md.
      3. Any [REQUIRES APPROVAL] SEO change you don’t want — strike it from the brief now.
      4. If imagery is needed but no IMAGES-NEEDED.md entries exist for the page, add them now.
      What this step does — you now have a folder of briefs with discovery, copy, and SEO sections. This step asks Claude Code to read RECON.md, PRODUCT.md, DESIGN.md, SEO-MANIFEST.md, CUSTOMER-JOURNEY.md, BASELINE-CRITIQUE.md, and the briefs themselves, then produce /redesign/IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md — a single file that locks in the order you'll redesign pages in Phase 6 and pre-fills the per-page re-prime prompt for each page.

      Why it matters — Phase 6 is a fresh-session-per-page loop that may stretch across days. Without this, you'd manually pick the order each session, manually fill in the page slug in the re-prime prompt every time, and track progress in your head. For a customer-facing redesign, ordering also has SEO consequences — you want the high-traffic, conversion-critical pages built while attention is high. The plan file gives you that order, pre-filled and durable.

      The order is a recommendation. Reorder freely if you have a real constraint — the recipe doesn't break.

      This phase produces /redesign/IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md, which is exactly the deliverable Superpowers' writing-plans skill is designed for.       Backend Layer 3 — Per-Page Gap Analysis + IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md backend section

      Layer 3 is the per-page backend gap check across every brief in /redesign/briefs/. The block has three sub-passes — Part A, Part A.5, Part B — that all run AFTER Phase 5's main Steps 1–4 are complete. Part A is a single batched pass across every brief (so the gap review doesn't disrupt shape's compass-not-spec rhythm during Step 1). Part A.5 reconciles FOUNDATION-FLOOR.md + BUSINESS-COMPLETENESS.md predictions against what the briefs actually need. Part B appends a backend section to IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md after Step 4 generates the per-page rows.
      Lightweight when empty. A page brief with zero backend gaps gets one line ("no backend gaps found for this page") and the model moves on. Overhead must be proportional to gaps found, not constant per page. Scope discipline: a gap qualifies only if (a) a specific element on this specific page directly requires it and won't work without it, OR (b) it would be embarrassing to launch a professional version of this business type without it. The model is not timid, but is precise — and asks before adding anything that feels out of scope. Customer-specific elevations: any gap that blocks a step on the CUSTOMER-JOURNEY.md conversion path is auto-elevated to BUILD + BLOCKS-REDESIGN; any gap whose fix would change a public URL, canonical, schema, sitemap, or robots entry is labeled MODIFYING and requires an SEO-RISK section in its plan file.
      When to paste this prompt. Run ONCE after ALL Phase 5 briefs (Steps 1–3: shape + clarify + SEO append) are complete and reviewed. Layer 3 is spec-level activity (BUILD/STUB/FAKE/DEFER decisions, plan files); injecting it per-page would conflict with shape's compass-not-spec rhythm. Batch across every brief in one focused session.
      Layer 3 Part A — Batched gap review (run ONCE after all briefs complete)
      WALK EVERY BRIEF IN ONE PASS: For each /redesign/briefs/*.md, walk every design element / state / interaction. For each, ask: does the backend already support this exactly, or does it require a capability that doesn't yet exist? Process page-by-page within this single session — don't try to compress; each page gets its own gap review. But you produce the entire output at the end, in one batch, for me to review as a single artifact. For each brief: read the brief file, DESIGN.md, PRODUCT.md, /redesign/CUSTOMER-JOURNEY.md, /redesign/SEO-MANIFEST.md, /redesign/BUSINESS-COMPLETENESS.md, /redesign/FOUNDATION-FLOOR.md. LAYER 3 — DESIGN-DRIVEN GAPS FOR THIS PAGE Walk every design element / state / interaction in the brief. For each, ask: does the backend already support this exactly, or does it require a capability that does not yet exist? A gap qualifies for the list ONLY if: (a) a specific element on this specific page directly requires it and won't work without it, OR (b) it would be embarrassing to launch a professional version of this page without it. Do not list nice-to-haves. Do not list capabilities the user can confirm later. Be precise, not timid. CUSTOMER-SPECIFIC AUTO-ELEVATIONS: 1. If the gap blocks any step on a CUSTOMER-JOURNEY.md conversion path, the DEFAULT action is BUILD + BLOCKS-REDESIGN. Override allowed if the user accepts the risk: STUB with a "coming soon" note is acceptable for early-stage launches; FAKE with a clear placeholder is acceptable if marketing has approved shipping with the placeholder; DEFER is acceptable if the conversion-path step itself is being de-prioritized for this launch. Specify the override reason inline. The default protects conversion; the override gives the user explicit choice. 2. If the fix would alter a URL, canonical, schema, sitemap, or robots entry from SEO-MANIFEST.md, label the plan file MODIFYING (not ADDITIVE) and add an explicit SEO-RISK section. If zero gaps for this page, write exactly one line in the brief: "Backend gaps: none for this page." and return — do not continue to the action review. If one or more gaps: 1. Number each gap (1, 2, 3...) with a one-line description of what's missing and which brief element requires it. 2. For EACH gap, propose ONE action — BUILD / FAKE / STUB / DEFER: - BUILD = the brief depends on real data or behavior that doesn't exist; we must build it. Will produce a plan file. - FAKE = the brief shows the feature but the page can ship before it works. Render the placeholder behind a feature flag (e.g. `if (FLAGS.feature_X_real) { ... real component ... } else { ... placeholder ... }`) so the swap-out at production is a flag flip, not search-and-replace through HTML. Add the flag to `/redesign/feature-flags.md` (recipe-supplementary) with one line: `feature_X_real: false # flip to true when [BUILD-feature-X.md] ships`. Mark the placeholder DOM with `data-backend-fake="[feature-slug]"`. Will be noted as a placeholder in the brief. - STUB = visible but inactive in UI, flagged as coming soon.       5→6
      Per-Page Session Ritual — Phase 5 to Phase 6~5 min  ·  REQUIRED — repeats once per page redesigned
      ▶
      Required step. No skip-if escape hatch on this card. Per-page fresh sessions are non-negotiable for Phase 6 — context bleed corrupts shipped code, and on customer-facing sites that means lost SEO and lost conversions.
      Why a fresh session per page — Phase 6 is where actual code gets written. A fresh session per page prevents the AI from accidentally pulling tokens, components, copy, OR SEO elements from the previous page into this one. You'll repeat this ritual once for each page in the redesign.

      Step 1 — Closing checklist (use BOTH the once-after-Phase-5 list and the between-pages list)

      Once, immediately after Phase 5 completes:
      • ☐ All briefs saved under /redesign/briefs/ with descriptive names — no stray brief.md at project root
      • ☐ Shared-layout brief exists with 00- prefix (sorts first)
