      • ☐ Any [REQUIRES APPROVAL] SEO changes in briefs are decided before Phase 6 begins
      • ☐ /redesign/RECIPE-PROGRESS.md updated — Phase 5 complete
      Between pages — every iteration of the Phase 6 loop:
      • ☐ Previous page is approved and committed / zipped / staging-snapshotted (per the Phase 0a branch you chose)
      • ☐ /redesign/PER-PAGE-SCORES.md has the previous page's audit + critique scores appended
      • ☐ /redesign/RECIPE-PROGRESS.md updated — pages done vs remaining
      • ☐ Mobile screenshot from Step 3 (adapt) saved for the previous page
      BackendOnce, immediately after Phase 5 — before the per-page session loop starts:
      • ☐ Every brief in /redesign/briefs/ has a "Backend gaps" line (either "none for this page" or a confirmed numbered list with actions)
      • ☐ Every approved BUILD or STUB gap has a plan file in /redesign/backend-plans/ named [ACTION]-[feature-slug].md
      • ☐ Every approved DEFER gap has a row in /redesign/DEFERRED-COMPLETENESS.md
      • ☐ /redesign/IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md has a ## Backend Plan Files section listing every plan file in dependency order, with conversion-path notes and SEO labels
      • ☐ Every BLOCKS-REDESIGN plan file shows owner + target-handled-by-date (set them now, even if approximate)
      • ☐ Create /redesign/backend-smoke-tests/ now if any BLOCKS-REDESIGN plan file exists — this folder is where endpoint verification transcripts will be saved when the 6→7 handoff runs functional smoke tests. Empty folder is fine.
      • ☐ For every BLOCKS-REDESIGN plan file marked HANDLED, a functional smoke test was run (curl, Postman, or equivalent): hit the BUILD endpoint with a sample request, confirm 200 response with expected payload shape per the plan file's "What information it stores or retrieves" section. Save the request/response transcript to /redesign/backend-smoke-tests/[feature-slug]-[date].txt as evidence. Self-attested checkboxes prove nothing — the smoke test does.
      • ☐ No backend plan file proposes a URL / canonical / schema / sitemap change without an SEO-RISK section that cross-references SEO-MANIFEST.md

      6
      Implementation~varies (typically 20–60 min per page)  ·  Redesigned customer-facing pages
      ▶
      Phase 6 is an executing-plans task per page. Invoke Superpowers' executing-plans skill at the start of each per-page session.
      How autonomy works during craft — Zones 1, 2, 3
      BUSINESS-COMPLETENESS.md tagged each gap with a zone in Phase 1. Phase 6 craft applies them.
      Zone 1 means craft fixes it inline without asking — for things already in the codebase, broken or incomplete, fixable within the existing stack, with no third-party config or secrets touched. Zone 1 is processed via Ralph loop:
      Ralph — Zone 1 batch processor
      /ralph-loop "Process Zone 1 fixes from BUSINESS-COMPLETENESS.md one at a time. Find the next item with status NOT-STARTED or PARTIAL and Zone tag 1. Apply the fix within the existing tech stack. Log a one-line entry to /redesign/AUTONOMOUS-LOG.md (filename, what changed, why). Mark the item HANDLED in BUSINESS-COMPLETENESS.md. If no Zone 1 items remain, output <promise>ZONE-1-BATCH-COMPLETE</promise>." --max-iterations 30 --completion-promise "ZONE-1-BATCH-COMPLETE"
      Each Zone 1 fix gets a one-line entry in /redesign/AUTONOMOUS-LOG.md as it happens, AND a consolidated summary surfaces at every existing recipe STOP gate. No new STOP gates added.
      Zone 2 means craft writes a one-paragraph plan, asks yes/no, proceeds on yes. Tiebreaker: anything touching third-party config, secrets, environment variables, or billable usage drops into Zone 2 even if it looks like a fix.
      Zone 3 (3a / 3b / 3c) — craft fully stops and waits. These should already have plan files in /redesign/backend-plans/ from Phase 5: BUILD-*.md / STUB-*.md / ADR-NNN-*.md / PROPOSAL-*.md.
      Per-page polish loop
      After /impeccable craft produces the initial page implementation, polish-to-brief is a Ralph candidate. Run:
      Ralph — per-page polish loop
      /ralph-loop "Read the brief at /redesign/briefs/[page-slug]-brief.md. Compare the current implementation to the brief's requirements (5 native sections + Recipe Context + Implementation Notes). Apply one focused improvement. Re-check against the brief. If all brief requirements are met, output <promise>PAGE-POLISH-COMPLETE</promise>." --max-iterations 8 --completion-promise "PAGE-POLISH-COMPLETE"
      Use only after craft has produced a working initial version. Don't use Ralph during initial craft itself — that's not the right shape.
      SEO rules apply during implementation: every title tag, meta description, H1, and internal link href must match the brief. If a brief proposes changing SEO elements, that requires your explicit approval.
      Per-page commits + a fresh Claude session per page prevent context drift. Strongly recommended for customer-facing work where SEO and conversion paths are at risk.
      Pick your responsive priority:
      M — Mobile-first (recommended)
      D — Desktop-first
      B — Balanced
      The recommended default for customer-facing sites — mobile traffic dominates and Google indexes mobile-first. Build mobile (375px) first, then enhance for tablet and desktop.
      Pick this for desktop-dominant audiences: B2B SaaS, professional services, luxury/configurator sites. Mobile is still required (Google indexes mobile-first) but desktop is the primary canvas.
      Build mobile (375px) and desktop (1440px) layouts in parallel — both are first-class. Tablet is a derived intermediate.

      The per-page loop

      For every page (in implementation order: shared layout, homepage, conversion-path pages, supporting pages), run the eight-step loop below. Six Impeccable commands run per page: craft → iterate → adapt → harden → clarify → audit → critique. Verification with Playwright and Chrome DevTools sits between Impeccable commands as the secondary check.

      🚀 Ready to start? Open /redesign/IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md and find the next row that's still [ ] (or the one labelled "Currently on"). Copy that row's pre-filled re-prime prompt into a fresh Claude Code session and paste. Reply primed — go after the session confirms it's read everything, then follow the eight-step loop below.
      Don't have an IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md? Go back to Phase 5 Step 4 — Phase 6 expects it as input.

      Step 1 — Build the page

      What this command does — /impeccable craft is the primary build command. Per the Impeccable docs, craft accepts a feature description as a natural-language argument and runs /impeccable shape internally as a non-skippable discovery phase, then loads references, then builds. The recipe pre-prepared a brief in Phase 5; we feed that brief into shape's discovery questions instead of re-interviewing.
      How this step works. Phase 6 Step 1 builds one customer-facing page at a time. The page list lives in /redesign/IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md (produced in Phase 5 Step 4) — open that file and pick the next row marked [ ]. Then drive the per-page build manually:

      3. Paste the yellow framing prompt below the command. Edit the RESPONSIVE STRATEGY block inside it to keep only your chosen strategy (mobile-first recommended) before pasting. The framing prompt also tells craft to honour the brief's Copy and SEO Preservation sections during the build.

      4. Work through the remaining Step 1–8 steps for this single page, then return to IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md and pick the next row.

The token cost reflects this; the upside is your brief is reviewed and editable before any code is written. Match DESIGN.md tokens exactly. Preserve every SEO element from the brief’s SEO Preservation section (or honour any [REQUIRES APPROVAL] flag). [RESPONSIVE STRATEGY: edit-before-paste — keep one of: mobile-first: build 375px first, then enhance to 768 → 1440 (recommended) desktop-first: build 1440px first, then narrow to 768 → 375 (mobile must still be functional) balanced: build mobile (375) and desktop (1440) in parallel; tablet (768) is derived] THIRD-PARTY INTEGRATION RULE: preserve embed code (Calendly, Stripe, Mailchimp, chat) exactly. Only restyle surrounding HTML. IMAGE HANDLING (every image you touch): 1. Already optimised (WebP, ≤200KB, descriptive alt) → proceed 2. Needs a real new photo → keep current, add data-redesign-needs-photo="reason", log to IMAGES-NEEDED.md Never ship stock photography as final. BACKEND FAKE MARKER: If this page renders any FAKE-tagged backend feature from /redesign/backend-plans/ (or per the brief's "Backend FAKE: …" notes), mark the relevant DOM element with `data-backend-fake="[feature-slug]"` (where [feature-slug] matches the corresponding plan filename).       Step 7 — Per-page audit + critique
      Save the per-page audit summary (5 dimension scores + P0/P1/P2/P3 findings) appending to /redesign/PER-PAGE-SCORES.md under "## [page-name] — audit". MEASURING LCP (procedure — this gate is enforceable only with a defined measurement method): Open Chrome DevTools → Performance tab. Throttle to "Slow 4G" via the Network throttling dropdown. Enable "Disable cache" in Network tab. Hard-reload the page (Ctrl/Cmd+Shift+R) and let the trace capture for 5 seconds. Stop recording. Read the LCP value from the Performance Insights pane or the trace summary — should be a single number under 2.5s. Alternative: run `npx lighthouse [page-url] --form-factor=mobile --throttling.cpuSlowdownMultiplier=4 --output=json --output-path=./lighthouse-[page-slug].json` and read `audits.largest-contentful-paint.numericValue` (in ms; divide by 1000 for seconds). STOP if either method exceeds 2.5s. Do not eyeball it — the threshold is enforceable only with consistent measurement. Tell me the per-page scores, the P0 list, and LCP value before Step 8.
      Routing rule for audit findings — P0: fix immediately. P1: batch into Phase 7 gauntlet. P2/P3: nice-to-have.

      If any of these fail: do NOT commit. Open the escape valves block above — failure mode 1 (re-run craft with corrections) or failure mode 4 (SEO regression) is usually the right path.

      ▶
      Phase 7 runs the named "pre-ship gauntlet" from the Impeccable docs (audit → clarify → harden — three commands), followed by polish as a separate post-gauntlet finishing step.       Gate-ordering tradeoff: optimize (Gate 4) runs BEFORE polish (Gate 7) so polish corrects any post-optimize layout shifts (e.g. lazy-loaded images, removed render-blocking JS, WebP conversions). Reversing the order would have polish do CLS work that optimize then undoes. The current order is deliberate; the cost is that polish must survive optimize's structural changes.

      Gate 7 — Polish (ALWAYS LAST — final alignment / consistency / state coverage)

      What this command does — the meticulous final-detail pass. Polish refines what exists. Catches alignment drift, spacing inconsistency, missing interaction states, copy-tone misalignment across the site. Per the Impeccable docs, polish is the LAST step — it runs after every other command that might restructure code, so its alignment work isn't undone. Re-cycle rule for Gate 7 → Gate 9 critique: if Gate 9 critique flags STRUCTURAL issues (broken hierarchy, drift, anti-patterns), re-run the relevant earlier gate (typically harden or one of the conditional optimize/adapt/onboard gates) to fix the structure, THEN re-run polish. If Gate 9 critique flags only AESTHETIC issues (alignment, spacing, hover states), polish handles them in place — no re-cycle needed. Don't re-run polish more than once per page; if it's still wrong after one re-run, the underlying brief is the problem, not polish.
      How this gate works.       8
      Maintain — Consolidate & Refresh DESIGN.md~25 min  ·  DESIGN.md (refreshed) + EXTRACT-REPORT.md
      ▼
      Maintain is the fourth phase the Impeccable docs name. Even a clean redesign creates drift — the same testimonial card, pricing block, or CTA pattern repeats with tiny differences across pages. Without consolidation, the next campaign or new page inherits this drift silently.
      Why this matters even for non-coders — when you (or a future developer) come back next sprint to add a new campaign page, the next /impeccable craft call reads whatever DESIGN.md says. If DESIGN.md still describes the system from Phase 4 — before any pages were built — craft has no way to match the patterns Phase 6 actually established. Phase 8 closes that loop.

      Step 1 — Find the patterns that repeat

      What this command does — /impeccable extract finds patterns repeated 3 or more times across the redesigned site and consolidates them into reusable tokens or components. Two usages are not a pattern — extract requires three.
      TYPE THIS COMMAND — type the green command below into Claude Code. Wait for it to finish before pasting the framing prompt.
      /impeccable extract for customer-facing pages
      THEN PASTE THIS PROMPT — paste the framing prompt below into Claude Code. It captures the output and applies the per-page actions.
      Phase 8 Step 1 — Run extract
      Constrain scope to customer-facing pages only. Bare `/impeccable extract` may run across the whole codebase including admin / blog / unrelated areas, which inflates the consolidation surface and risks extracting patterns that aren't really shared. If the skill rejects the scope argument, fall back to bare `/impeccable extract` and manually filter results to customer-facing patterns only. Read DESIGN.md, every brief in /redesign/briefs/, and the redesigned customer-facing source code. Find every pattern that appears 3+ times. Examples: - A testimonial card layout used on 4 pages - A hero shape repeated across the conversion path - A trust-signal arrangement (logos / reviews / certifications) - A form input style with the same border / focus / label position - A CTA button variant For each pattern propose: - A token name or component name - A variant API if it has variants - The CSS rule that consolidates them Migrate every duplicated CSS in the same pass. Extract is not a "find now, fix later" command — it consolidates and migrates atomically. Save the migration log to /redesign/EXTRACT-REPORT.md (which patterns, where they were, what they became). Then STOP. I will review the migration before Step 2.

      Step 2 — Refresh DESIGN.md to reflect the post-redesign state

      What this command does — /impeccable document regenerates DESIGN.md from the actual code as it ships now. This OVERWRITES the Phase 4 DESIGN.md. The Phase 4 version is preserved in git history and in the redesign-baseline tag from Phase 0a.
      How this step works. /impeccable document overwrites DESIGN.md — you must preserve the supplementary patterns section BEFORE it runs, then re-attach it after. Three parts in strict order:

      1. Paste the first prompt below (Step 2a). It reads DESIGN.md and saves the ## Customer-Facing Patterns section to a temp file at /redesign/.preserved-design-section.md. Then STOP.

      2. Type the green /impeccable document command. Let it finish and regenerate DESIGN.md.

      3. Paste the second prompt (Step 2b). It confirms the file location and re-attaches the preserved patterns section from the temp file.
      Step 2a — Preserve supplementary section before document runs
      Read the current DESIGN.md (at project root). Find the "## Customer-Facing Patterns" section (added in Phase 4 Step 2 — covers hero treatments, trust-signal layouts, CTA hierarchies, conversion-focused component variants, and customer-specific motion patterns). Copy the entire ## Customer-Facing Patterns section verbatim to a temp file at /redesign/.preserved-design-section.md. Confirm the temp file exists and contains the full section. Then STOP — do not run /impeccable document yet. Type the green command above when ready.
      THEN TYPE THIS COMMAND — type the green command below into Claude Code. Wait for it to finish before pasting the framing prompt.
      /impeccable document
      THEN PASTE THIS PROMPT — paste the framing prompt below into Claude Code. It captures the output and applies the per-page actions.
      Phase 8 Step 2b — Re-attach after document runs
      STEP C — Confirm file location: /impeccable document writes DESIGN.md AND DESIGN.json to PROJECT ROOT (overwriting the Phase 4 versions). That’s the correct location — leave them there. NOTE — document confirms before overwriting: when /impeccable document is about to overwrite an existing DESIGN.md, it will pause and ask you to confirm. Confirm yes. STEP D — Re-attach the preserved section: Read /redesign/.preserved-design-section.md (the temp file you wrote in Step 2a). Append its contents to the BOTTOM of the new DESIGN.md (after the last canonical section, before any EOF). VERIFY: open DESIGN.md (at project root). It should contain the six canonical sections (Overview, Colors, Typography, Elevation, Components, Do's and Don'ts) PLUS the re-attached ## Customer-Facing Patterns section at the bottom. Update /redesign/RECIPE-PROGRESS.md — Phase 8 complete. The recipe is now done.
      ✅ What to check after Phase 8:
      1. EXTRACT-REPORT.md lists at least one consolidated pattern. If it lists none — either the redesign was unusually clean, or extract ran on the wrong files.
      2. The new DESIGN.md describes components that match what’s actually rendered.
      3. The "## Customer-Facing Patterns" section was reattached.
      ⏸
      STOP — recipe complete
      You can now safely deploy. Production deployment happens AFTER your explicit "yes deploy". Plan to monitor Google Search Console for 4 weeks post-deploy. If you've been running this recipe in a git repo with feature branches, invoke Superpowers' finishing-a-development-branch skill before merging or closing the branch — it walks the close-out checklist (commit hygiene, branch cleanup, final verification) better than freehand.

      After the recipe — adding new pages later

      The recipe was built for a planned redesign of an existing site. Once shipped, the artifacts you generated — PRODUCT.md, DESIGN.md, /redesign/SEO-MANIFEST.md, /redesign/CUSTOMER-JOURNEY.md, and the brief format — keep paying dividends. Here's the minimal workflow when a client or product owner asks for a new customer-facing page later.

      Workflow (one new page)

      1. Open a fresh Claude Code session. Briefs and design system are at project root, so no recipe-style re-prime is needed.
      2. Run /impeccable shape for the new page. It reads PRODUCT.md and DESIGN.md automatically. Save the resulting brief to /redesign/briefs/[new-page-slug]-brief.md.
      3. Append a ## SEO Preservation section to the brief — same mechanic as Phase 5 Step 3 customer. Read /redesign/SEO-MANIFEST.md for context, draft title / meta / H1 / schema for the new page, flag any reused SEO weight from existing pages.
      4. Run /impeccable clarify draft copy for brief /redesign/briefs/[new-page-slug]-brief.md to append a ## Copy section.
      5. Run /impeccable craft redesign of customer page [new-page-name] with the brief loaded. Same mechanic as Phase 6 Step 1.
      6. Run /impeccable adapt [page] for mobile and tablet. Verify CTA-above-fold at 375px — same mechanic as Phase 6 Step 3 customer.
      7. Run /impeccable audit [page] and /impeccable critique [page]. P0 fix-on-the-spot, P1 batch for next time.
      8. Smoke test, commit, ship. No new gauntlet needed for one page.
      9. (Optional) Append a row to /redesign/IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md for record-keeping. Mark [x] immediately with commit hash.
      10. If the new page joins the conversion path (it's a step in the funnel, not a side page) — update /redesign/CUSTOMER-JOURNEY.md to insert it at the right position in the journey.
      Adding 3 or more pages at once? Run /impeccable shape for all pages first, save each brief, then follow the Phase 6 per-page loop in sequence — one fresh session per page. Don't skip straight to craft without briefs for each.

      What NOT to do

      • Don't re-run /impeccable teach — PRODUCT.md already exists and is correct.
      • Don't re-run /impeccable document — DESIGN.md already exists and is correct.
      • Don't re-run the Phase 7 gauntlet site-wide — gauntlet is for redesign-scale work, not single-page additions.
      • Don't skip the ## SEO Preservation step. New customer-facing pages compete with your other pages on Google — without an explicit SEO plan you may cannibalise rankings of existing pages.

      When to refresh DESIGN.md

      If the new page introduces a genuinely new component pattern (not just a variant of existing components), run /impeccable extract on the new page after it ships, then /impeccable document to regenerate DESIGN.md. That's Phase 8's mechanic, repeated manually. Re-append the ## Customer-Facing Patterns section afterwards (just like Phase 8 Step D).

      The two prompts you need — open
      Prompt 1 — New page workflow (shape → SEO append → clarify → craft → adapt → audit/critique)
      I want to add a new customer-facing page using the existing design system. Project context is already in place: PRODUCT.md and DESIGN.md are at project root, /redesign/SEO-MANIFEST.md and /redesign/CUSTOMER-JOURNEY.md document the existing site, /redesign/briefs/ contains historical briefs. The new page: [describe purpose, audience, conversion role — 2-3 sentences] Suggested page slug: [new-page-slug] Joins the conversion path? [yes/no — if yes, where in the funnel] STEP 1 — Read PRODUCT.md, DESIGN.md, /redesign/SEO-MANIFEST.md, /redesign/CUSTOMER-JOURNEY.md. Confirm in one message what you read. STEP 2 — Run /impeccable shape for the new page. Save the brief to /redesign/briefs/[new-page-slug]-brief.md. STEP 3 — Append a ## SEO Preservation section to the brief: title tag, meta description, H1, schema type, internal-link plan. Cross-reference SEO-MANIFEST.md to ensure the new page does not cannibalise existing pages' search terms. Flag anything that needs my approval as [REQUIRES APPROVAL]. STEP 4 — Run /impeccable clarify draft copy for brief /redesign/briefs/[new-page-slug]-brief.md. The clarify pass appends a ## Copy section. STEP 5 — Show me the brief, including its SEO Preservation and Copy sections. STOP. Wait for me to approve before craft. STEP 6 — On approval, run /impeccable craft redesign of customer page [new-page-name]. Use the brief as the source for craft's internal shape discovery (don't re-interview me). Preserve every SEO element verbatim unless flagged [REQUIRES APPROVAL]. STEP 7 — Run /impeccable adapt [page] for mobile and tablet. Verify CTA above fold at 375x812. STEP 8 — Run /impeccable audit [page] and /impeccable critique [page]. P0 audit findings: fix before commit. P1: list and we'll batch later. STEP 9 — Smoke test (forms, buttons, keyboard, mobile, analytics, third-party widgets per the brief's Feature Preservation Manifest). STEP 10 — Commit. If the page joins the conversion path, update /redesign/CUSTOMER-JOURNEY.md. Optionally append a row to /redesign/IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md.
      Prompt 2 — Refresh DESIGN.md if the new page introduced a new pattern
      The new page [page-name] introduced a component pattern that isn't currently in DESIGN.md (e.g. [describe: new hero treatment, new trust-signal pattern, new sticky-CTA variant, new schema usage]). I want to consolidate it back into the design system. STEP 1 — Run /impeccable extract on [page-name]. Save the extract report to /redesign/EXTRACT-REPORT-[YYYYMMDD].md. STEP 2 — Show me the extracted patterns + which are genuinely new vs. variants of existing patterns. STOP for my review. STEP 3 — Before /impeccable document overwrites DESIGN.md, save the existing "## Customer-Facing Patterns" section verbatim — you'll re-append it after. STEP 4 — On approval, run /impeccable document. It will overwrite DESIGN.md and DESIGN.json at project root — that's correct, the new version absorbs the new pattern. STEP 5 — Re-append the preserved "## Customer-Facing Patterns" section to the bottom of the new DESIGN.md, paste verbatim. Do not let document drop it. STEP 6 — Tell me which sections of DESIGN.md changed before I commit.
